On the Capacities Associated with Local Muckenhoupt Weights
Keng Hao Ooi

Abstract
. We develop a theory of capacities associated with local Muckenhoupt weights. Fundamental properties of local Muckenhoupt weights will be revisited. Weak type boundedness of nonlinear potential and capacitary strong type inequalities associated with such weights will be addressed. The boundedness of the local maximal function on the spaces of Choquet integrals associated with such weighted capacities will be justified as an application of the theory. We also address on the thinness of sets with the Kellogg property as another application.



1.
Introduction

To measure the discontinuities of Sobolev functions, one uses the capacities. To begin with, let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<\infty
, and
1<p<\infty
. We define the Bessel capacities
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
by

\displaystyle\text{Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}:f\geq 0,~G_{\alpha}\ast f\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},

(1.0.1)

where
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an arbitrary set,

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left[\left(1+4\pi^{2}\left|\cdot\right|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right](x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

is the Bessel kernel, and
\mathcal{F}^{-1}
is the inverse distributional Fourier transform on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. A Sobolev function
f
is of the form that
f=G_{\alpha}\ast g
for some
g\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. It can be proved that for any
\varepsilon>0
, there is an open set
G\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(G)<\varepsilon
and
f|_{G^{c}}
is a continuous function on
G^{c}
(see
[
AH2
, Proposition 6.1.2]
). Further, the standard Sobolev embedding theorem states that

\displaystyle|E|^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}}\leq C(n,\alpha,p){\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E),\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad 1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha},

where we use the convention that
C(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\ldots)
to designate the positive constant depending only on the parameters
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\ldots
. The previous embedding shows that the Bessel capacities
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
can be viewed as a refined Lebesgue measure as
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)=0
entails
|E|=0
. Hence
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
is more sensitive in measuring the discontinuities of Sobolev functions than the Lebesgue measure.

A very thorough study of the theory of capacities is given by Maz’ya and Havin
[
MH
]
. Landkof
[
LN
]
also gave a modern perspective of such a theory. While Adams and Hedberg
[
AH2
]
also provided an excellent standard graduate text in that area. Later, Adams
[
AD
]
developed the weighted version of the theory of capacities. A modern book on weighted capacities is due to Turesson
[
TB
]
. The weighted theories therein all assume the Muckenhoupt
A_{p}
condition on such weights. For instance,
[
TB
, Theorem 3.1.2]
, which known to be the Muckenhoupt-Wheeden type inequality, states that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)^{p}dx,

(1.0.2)

where
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
I_{\alpha,\rho}(\cdot)=\left|\cdot\right|^{-(n-\alpha)}\chi_{\left|\cdot\right|\leq\rho}
is the local Riesz kernel,
M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(\cdot)=\sup\limits_{0<r\leq\rho}r^{-(n-\alpha)}\mu(B_{r}(\cdot))
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
is an
A_{\infty}
weight. While the estimate (
1.0.2
) is used to show that the weighted local Riesz
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
and weighted Bessel
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
capacities are equivalent that

\displaystyle C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p}})^{-1}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\leq B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p}})R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E),

(1.0.3)

where
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is arbitrary,
\omega
is an
A_{p}
weight,

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}^{p}:f\geq 0,~I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},

\displaystyle B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(E)

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}^{p}:f\geq 0,~G_{\alpha}\ast f\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},

and
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x)|^{p}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
(see
[
TB
, Theorem 3.3.7 and Lemma 3.3.8]
). However, there are some results which suggest that the weight condition on (
1.0.2
) and (
1.0.3
) is not optimal. Let
{\bf M}^{\rm loc}
be the (centered) local Hardy-Littlewood maximal function defined by

\displaystyle{\bf M}^{\rm loc}f(x)=\sup_{0<r\leq 1}\frac{1}{|B_{r}(x)|}\int_{B_{r}(x)}|f(y)|dy,

where
f
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. A weight
\omega
belongs to local Muckenhoupt
A_{1}^{\rm loc}
class if there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle{\bf M}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)\leq C\omega(x)\quad\text{a.e.}.

Let
V^{\mu}=G_{\alpha}\ast(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}
be the nonlinear potential of
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
. Roughly speaking,
V^{\mu}
is the minimizer of (
1.0.1
). It is shown in
[
OP
, Theorem 3.1]
that
(V^{\mu})^{\delta}
is an
A_{1}^{\rm loc}
weight for admissible exponents
\delta
. On the other hand, if we define the Choquet integrals by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f|d{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|f(x)|>t\})dt

for admissible functions
f
, then it is shown in
[
OP2
]
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left({\bf M}^{\rm loc}f\right)^{q}d{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,q)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f|^{q}d{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p},\quad q>\frac{n-\alpha p}{n},

where
{\bf M}^{\rm loc}
is replaced by the usual Hardy-Littlewood maximal function
{\bf M}
if the Riesz capacities
{\rm cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
(defined by replacing
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
with
I_{\alpha}(\cdot)=\left|\cdot\right|^{-(n-\alpha)}
in (
1.0.1
)) are taken into account. In view of the aforementioned results, one may reasonably suspect that the weighted theory corresponding to (
1.0.2
) and (
1.0.3
) should work with local Muckenhoupt
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
class. By
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
class we mean that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p}^{\rm loc}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|Q|\leq 1}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1},\quad 1<p<\infty,

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p}^{\rm loc}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|Q|\leq 1}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)},\quad p=1,\quad Q~\text{is any cube in}~\mathbb{R}^{n},

it can shown that the maximal characterization of
A_{1}^{\rm loc}
coincides with the above definition (see Proposition
2.3.2
). The definition of
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
for
1<p<\infty
goes back to Rychkov
[
RV
]
. We will show that (
1.0.2
) holds for
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}=\bigcup\limits_{1\leq p<\infty}A_{p}^{\rm loc}
(see Proposition
3.3.2
). Moreover, it will be shown that

\displaystyle C^{-1}R_{\alpha,p;\rho_{2}}^{\omega}(E)\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho_{1}}^{\omega}(E)\leq CR_{\alpha,p;\rho_{2}}^{\omega}(E),\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}

(1.0.4)

for some suitable constant
C>0
, where
\omega^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
(see Proposition
3.5.1
). Meanwhile, if
\omega
is an
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
weight that does not decay too fast, then (
1.0.3
) holds (see Theorem
3.5.2
). On the other hand,
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
becomes trivial, i.e.,
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=0
for
\omega=e^{\delta\left|\cdot\right|}\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, where
\delta>0
is large enough (see Theorem
3.5.5
). This also reminisces a result from Aikawa
[
AH
]
that the weighted Riesz capacities
R_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
are trivial for rapidly decay
\omega
.

The proof of (
1.0.4
), which mainly relies on (
1.0.2
), is not verbatim to that of
[
TB
, Theorem 3.1.2]
. We need to introduce the local Muckenhoupt
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
class with scale
0<\rho<\infty
, which is by definition, the condition
|Q|\leq 1
being replaced by the length of cubes
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
in the previous definition of
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. We will prove that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)^{p}dx

(see Proposition
3.3.2
). The constant
C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}})
plays a crucial role in our theory. For fixed
n
,
\alpha
, and
p
, we will require the function
\mathcal{N}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
defined by
\mathcal{N}(\cdot)=C(n,\alpha,p,\cdot)
is increasing, and this will be needed in proving that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(E),\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n},

and (
1.0.4
) will then follow (see Remarks
3.3.3
and
3.3.6
). To obtain the fact that
\mathcal{N}(\cdot)
is monotonic increasing, we first prove
A_{p;\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}=A_{p;\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}
for any
0<\rho_{1},\rho_{2}<\infty
, and there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle C^{-1}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}}\leq C[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}},

while a very careful analysis on the parameters in
C
needs to be taken throughout the proof (see, for example, Theorems
2.3.3
,
2.5.3
, and Remark
2.4.3
). The analysis of such a constant
C
is not covered in Rychkov
[
RV
]
.

Now we introduce the capacitary strong type inequality (CSI), which reads as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(G_{\alpha}\ast f)(x)^{p}d{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}dx,

where
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is any nonnegative function (see
[
AH2
, Theorem 7.1.1]
). It is used in studying the trace class inequalities and the compactness of embedding of potentials (see
[
MV2
]
). The weighted analogue of such an inequality is given by
[
AD
, Theorem 5.1]
, where the weighted Riesz capacities and
A_{\infty}
weights are assumed therein. Our corresponding result tackles with
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
and
A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
weights (see Theorem
3.9.1
). The proof of Theorem
3.9.1
uses bounded maximum principle for Wolff potential, to wit:

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)>t\right\}\right)\leq C(n)\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{t^{p-1}},\quad 0<t<\infty

(see Theorem
3.8.3
). Such a principle also appears in
[
AH2
, Theorem 2.6.3]
for convolution kernels. However,
[
AH2
, Theorem 2.6.3]
is not applicable to the Wolff potential
W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
since it is not of convolution type. As an application of the CSI, we obtain the boundedness of
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}
(defined by replacing
0<r\leq 1
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
in
{\bf M}^{\rm loc}
) on the spaces of Choquet integrals associated with
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
, to wit:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left({\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right)^{q}d\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;160\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f|^{q}d\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega},~q>\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}

(1.0.5)

(see Theorem
4.3.8
), where the capacities
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is an equivalent variant of
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
(see Theorem
3.5.3
). When
\omega=1
, the estimate (
1.0.5
) reduces to
[
OP2
, Theorem 1.2]
, and it has been shown in
[
OP2
]
that the exponent
\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}
is sharp. A related result to the estimate (
1.0.5
) is given in
[
OV
]
, where Hausdorff content is taken into account instead of capacities. However, the argument given in
[
OV
]
uses certain type of covering lemma of dyadic cubes, and this argument seems not to work for capacities since capacities are not defined in terms of covering. When
q=\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}
, the estimate (
1.0.5
) reduces to a weak type estimate (see Theorem
4.3.6
).

The last application in this paper is about the thinness of sets. It is used in studying the regularity of the Dirichlet problem for the
p
-Laplace equation
\Delta_{p}u=0
(see Maz’ya
[
MV
]
and Kilpeläinen and Malý
[
KM
]
). The Kellogg property will also be addressed (see Theorem
4.4.5
).

We end this section by listing out all the main notations used in this paper. The sets
\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathbb{Q}
, and
\mathbb{R}
have their usual meanings. The symbol
C(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\ldots)
designates the positive constant depending only on the parameters
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\ldots
. Given two quantities
A
and
B
, we write
A\approx B
to refer
C^{-1}B\leq A\leq CB
, where the constant
C>0
is independent of the main parameters in
A
and
B
. The characteristic function of a set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is denoted by
\chi_{E}
. Denote by
\left|\cdot\right|_{\infty}
the Euclidean maximum norm that

\displaystyle|x|_{\infty}=\max\{|x_{1}|,\ldots,|x_{n}|\},\quad x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

For the word “cube”, we always refer to open cube with symbol
Q
. We denote the closed cubes by
\overline{Q}
. We write
Q_{r}(x)
the cube with length
\ell(Q)=2r
and center
x
, and
\overline{Q}_{r}(x)
is the closed version of
Q_{r}(x)
. For any
\lambda>0
and cube
Q
, we write
\lambda Q
to refer the cube concentric to
Q
with side length
\lambda
times that of
Q
. Similar notation will do for
\lambda\overline{Q}
for closed cube
\overline{Q}
. Suppose that
\omega
is a weight. Then we write
\omega(E)=\int_{E}\omega(x)dx
for Lebesgue measurable sets
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. While
{\rm Avg}_{E}f=\frac{1}{|E|}\int_{E}f(x)dx
designates the average of a Lebesgue measurable function
f
on
E
with positive Lebesgue measure
|E|
. For any weight
\omega
and
1<p<\infty
, we write
\omega^{\prime}=\omega^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}=\omega^{1-p^{\prime}}
, where
p^{\prime}
is the Hölder’s conjugate of
p
that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1
. For any
0<p<\infty
, the space
L^{p}(\omega)
consists of Lebesgue measurable functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
with

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x)|^{p}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}<\infty.

Given a positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the space
L_{\mu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for
0<p<\infty
consists of
\mu
-measurable functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
with

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{\mu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x)|^{p}d\mu(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}<\infty.

The symbols
L^{p}(\omega)^{+}
and
L_{\mu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{+}
are the positive cone (nonnegative elements) of
L^{p}(\omega)
and
L_{\mu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
respectively. The space of positive measures on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with support in
E
is denoted by
\mathcal{M}^{+}(E)
. Finally, the space
C_{0}(\Omega)
consists of continuous functions
f
on
\Omega
with compact support, where
\Omega
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

2.
On the Local Muckenhoupt Class
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. A nonnegative measurable function
\omega
is said to be a weight provided that
\omega
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\omega(x)>0
almost everywhere. We define the local Muckenhoupt class
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
to consist of all weights
\omega
such that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\ell(Q)\leq\rho}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1},\quad 1<p<\infty,

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\ell(Q)\leq\rho}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)},\quad p=1,

where the suprema are taken over all cubes
Q
with volumes
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. When the condition
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
is replaced to all cubes
Q
with arbitrary volumes, we obtain the usual Muckenhoupt
A_{p}
class. As an immediate result, we have
A_{p}\subseteq A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
.

2.1.
Basic Properties of
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}

We begin with the following basic properties of
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
.

Proposition 2.1.1
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then

(1)

[\delta^{\lambda}(\omega)]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\lambda}^{\rm loc}}
, where
\delta^{\lambda}(\omega)(x)=\omega(\lambda x_{1},\ldots,\lambda x_{n})
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
\lambda>0
.

(2)

[\tau^{z}\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
, where
\tau^{z}(\omega)(x)=\omega(x-z)
,
x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(3)

[\lambda\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
, where
\lambda>0
.

(4)

If
1<p<\infty
, then the weight
\omega^{1-p^{\prime}}\in A_{p^{\prime};\rho}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle[\omega^{1-p^{\prime}}]_{A_{p^{\prime};\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{p^{\prime}-1}.

Therefore,
\omega\in A_{2;\rho}^{\rm loc}
if and only if
\omega^{-1}\in A_{2;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with
[\omega^{-1}]_{A_{2;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{2;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

(5)

[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\geq 1
for all
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. The equality holds if and only if
\omega
is almost everywhere a constant.

(6)

For
1\leq p<q<\infty
, it holds that
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}\subseteq A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

(7)

If
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{q\rightarrow 1^{+}}[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

(8)

The following formula

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell(Q)\leq\rho\\
f\in L^{p}(Q,\omega dx)\\
\int_{Q}|f|^{p}\omega dx>0\end{subarray}}\frac{\left(\dfrac{1}{|Q|}\displaystyle\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx\right)^{p}}{\dfrac{1}{\omega(Q)}\displaystyle\int_{Q}|f(x)|^{p}\omega(x)dx}

holds.

Proof.

Property (1):
For
Q=Q_{r}(c)
, it holds that
\{\lambda^{-1}x:x\in Q\}=Q_{\frac{r}{\lambda}}\left(\frac{c}{\lambda}\right)
and
|\{\lambda^{-1}x:x\in Q\}|=\lambda^{-n}|Q|
. The result follows by the standard dilation formula for the Lebesgue integrals.

Property (2):
We have
|z+Q|=|Q|
,
z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The result follows by the translation formula for the Lebesgue integrals.

Property (3):
Trivial.

Property (4):
The result follows by switching the duality role of
\omega
in the definition of
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
.

Property (5):
As in the proof of Proposition
2.2.4
, we have

\displaystyle 1=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p}}dx\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

The equality holds if and only if
\omega(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}=c\cdot\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p}}
almost everywhere for some
c>0
.

Property (6):
Observe that
0<q^{\prime}-1<p^{\prime}-1\leq\infty
and the statement

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}

is equivalent to

\displaystyle\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{q^{\prime}-1}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}\leq\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}-1}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})},

which holds by Hölder’s inequality.

Property (7):
Note that
Property (6)
implies that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}},\quad q>1.

(2.1.1)

Let
\eta<[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
be given. There is a cube
Q\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and

\displaystyle\eta<\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}.

It is a standard fact

\displaystyle\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\|f\|_{L^{p}(X,\mu)}=\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(X,\mu)}

provided that
f\in L^{p_{0}}(X,\mu)
for some
0<p_{0}<\infty
and
(X,\mu)
is an arbitrary measure space with positive measure
\mu
on
X
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle<\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{q\rightarrow 1^{+}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{q-1}}dx\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{q\rightarrow 1^{+}}[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Since
\eta<[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
is arbitrary, one has
[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq\liminf\limits_{q\rightarrow 1^{+}}[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
, which yields

\displaystyle\lim_{q\rightarrow 1^{+}}[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}

by combining (
2.1.1
).

Property (8):
For
1<p<\infty
, one has by Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}|f(x)|\omega(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p}}dx\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|Q|^{p}}\left(\int_{Q}|f(x)|^{p}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{p}}dx\right)^{\frac{p}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\omega(Q)}\int_{Q}|f(x)|^{p}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega(Q)}\int_{Q}|f(x)|^{p}\omega(x)dx\right).

The above estimates also hold for
p=1
by obvious modification, which proves the
\geq
direction of the inequality in
Property 8
. For the other direction, we simply let
f=\omega^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{p}}
.
∎

If
k\geq 0
is a bounded function with strictly positive lower bound, then
k\omega
is still an
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
weight for
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
, as the following shown.

Proposition 2.1.2
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
k
be a nonnegative measurable function such that
k,k^{-1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. If
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
for some
1\leq p<\infty
, then
k\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle[k\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|k^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\omega\|_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Assume that
p=1
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}k(x)\omega(x)dx\right)\left\|(k\omega)^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}

\displaystyle\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\|k^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}

\displaystyle\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|k^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\omega\|_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

which yields
[k\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|k^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\omega\|_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

Consider now that
1<p<\infty
. We have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}k(x)\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}[k(x)\omega(x)]^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|k^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\omega\|_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

which yields
[k\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|k^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\omega\|_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.
∎

Proposition 2.1.3
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then for any
1<p<\infty
,
\omega_{1}\omega_{2}^{1-p}\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle\left[\omega_{1}\omega_{2}^{1-p}\right]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega_{1}]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}[\omega_{2}]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{p-1}.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{1}(x)\omega_{2}(x)^{1-p}dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}[\omega_{1}(x)\omega_{2}(x)^{1-p}]^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{1}(x)dx\right)\|\omega_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}^{p-1}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{2}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}\|\omega_{1}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega_{1}]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{2}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)\|\omega_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega_{1}]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}[\omega_{2}]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{p-1},

as expected.
∎

If we raise an
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
weight
\omega
up to exponent
0<\delta<1
, then
\omega^{\delta}
belongs to a smaller class
A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}
, where
q
is the linear ratio between
1
and
p
with respect to
\delta
.

Lemma 2.1.4
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
,
0<\delta<1
, and
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then
\omega^{\delta}\in A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}
for
q=\delta p+1-\delta
with

\displaystyle\left[\omega^{\delta}\right]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\delta}.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Consider first that
p=1
. Then
q=1
. We have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{\delta}dx\right)\left\|\omega^{-\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{\delta}dx\right)\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}^{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\delta}\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}^{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\delta},

which yields
\left[\omega^{\delta}\right]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\delta}
.

Consider now that
1<p<\infty
. We have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{\delta}dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\left[\omega(x)^{\delta}\right]^{-\frac{1}{q-1}}dx\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\delta}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{\delta}{q-1}}dx\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\delta}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{\delta(p-1)}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\delta},

which yields
\left[\omega^{\delta}\right]_{A_{q;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\delta}
.
∎

The only weights with uniformly bounded
\left[\cdot\right]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
constants are those of
A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
class.

Proposition 2.1.5
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
. Assume that there is a constant
C>0
such that
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq C
for all
1<p<\infty
. Then
\omega\in A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with
[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq C
.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}\leq C.

Note that

\displaystyle\lim_{p\rightarrow 1^{+}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\lim_{p\rightarrow 1^{+}}\frac{1}{|Q|^{p-1}}\lim_{p\rightarrow 1^{+}}\left(\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\lim_{q\rightarrow\infty}\left(\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-q}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=\sup_{\ell(Q)\leq\rho}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\leq C<\infty,

as expected.
∎

The truncation
\min(\omega,k)
of an
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
weight
\omega
up to height
k
still belongs to the same class.

Proposition 2.1.6
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
k>0
,
1\leq p<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle[\min(\omega,k)]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq C(p)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

where
C(p)=1
for
p=1
,
C(p)=2
for
1<p\leq 2
, and
C(p)=2^{p-1}
for
2<p<\infty
.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Consider first that
p=1
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)dx

\displaystyle\leq\min\left(k,\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)

\displaystyle\leq\min\left(k,[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\cdot{\rm ess.inf}_{Q}\omega\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left([\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}},1\right)\min\left(k,{\rm ess.inf}_{Q}\omega\right)

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\cdot{\rm ess.inf}_{Q}\omega,

which yields

\displaystyle[\min(\omega,k)]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Now consider that
1<p\leq 2
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\max\left(\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}},k^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx+\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}k^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx+k^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}.

As
0<p-1\leq 1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx+k^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}+k^{-1}.

As a result, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left(k,\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}+k^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}+k\cdot k^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}+1

\displaystyle\leq 2[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

which shows
[\min(\omega,k)]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq 2[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
for
1<p\leq 2
.

Consider the final case where
p>2
. Then
p-1>1
and hence

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx+k^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-2}\left[\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}+k^{-1}\right].

Subsequently,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x),k)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-2}\min\left(k,\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}+k^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-2}\left([\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

which implies that
[\min(\omega,k)]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq 2^{p-1}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
for
1<p\leq 2
and the proof is now complete.
∎

Recall the standard interpolation theorem that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{p}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{p_{0}}}^{1-\theta}\|f\|_{L^{p_{1}}}^{\theta},

where
0<p_{0}<p<p_{1}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1-\theta}{p_{0}}+\frac{\theta}{p_{1}}.

We have a similar statement for
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
weights.

Proposition 2.1.7
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
\omega_{0}\in A_{p_{0};\rho}^{\rm loc}
,
\omega_{1}\in A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}
, and
1\leq p_{0},p_{1}<\infty
. Suppose that
0\leq\theta\leq 1
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1-\theta}{p_{0}}+\frac{\theta}{p_{1}},\qquad\omega^{\frac{1}{p}}=\omega_{0}^{\frac{1-\theta}{p_{0}}}\omega_{1}^{\frac{\theta}{p_{1}}}.

Then
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p_{0};\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{(1-\theta)\frac{p}{p_{0}}}[\omega_{1}]_{A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\theta\frac{p}{p_{1}}}.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. By applying Hölder’s inequality to the exponents
\frac{p_{0}}{(1-\theta)p}
and
\frac{p_{1}}{\theta p}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{0}(x)^{(1-\theta)\frac{p}{p_{0}}}\omega_{1}(x)^{\theta\frac{p}{p_{1}}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{0}(x)dx\right)^{(1-\theta)\frac{p}{p_{0}}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{1}(x)dx\right)^{\theta\frac{p}{p_{1}}}.

On the other hand, since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=\frac{1-\theta}{p_{0}^{\prime}}+\frac{\theta}{p_{1}^{\prime}},

applying Hölder’s inequality again, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{0}(x)^{-(1-\theta)\frac{p}{p_{0}(p-1)}}\omega_{1}(x)^{-\theta\frac{p}{p_{1}(p-1)}}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{0}(x)^{-(1-\theta)\frac{p^{\prime}}{p_{0}}}\omega_{1}(x)^{-\theta\frac{p^{\prime}}{p_{1}}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{0}(x)^{-\frac{p_{0}^{\prime}}{p_{0}}}dx\right)^{(1-\theta)\frac{p^{\prime}}{p_{0}^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{1}(x)^{-\frac{p_{1}^{\prime}}{p_{1}}}dx\right)^{\theta\frac{p^{\prime}}{p_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{0}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p_{0}-1}}dx\right)^{p_{0}-1}\right]^{(1-\theta)\frac{p^{\prime}}{p_{0}}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega_{1}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p_{1}-1}}dx\right)^{p_{1}-1}\right]^{\theta\frac{p^{\prime}}{p_{1}}}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|Q|\leq\rho}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{p_{0};\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{(1-\theta)\frac{p}{p_{0}}}[\omega_{1}]_{A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\theta\frac{p}{p_{1}}},

as expected.
∎

The following shows that the triangle inequality holds for
\left[\cdot\right]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

Proposition 2.1.8
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p_{1},p_{2}<\infty
,
\omega_{1}\in A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}
, and
\omega_{2}\in A_{p_{2};\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then
\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle[\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega_{1}]_{A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}}+[\omega_{2}]_{A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}},

where
p=\max(p_{1},p_{2})
.

Proof.

We will use the convention that
\frac{1}{0}=\infty
in the sequel. Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}}\leq\min\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{1}},\frac{1}{\omega_{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p-1}=\min\left(\frac{1}{p_{1}-1},\frac{1}{p_{2}-1}\right).

Denote by

\displaystyle m=\min\left(\left\|\omega_{1}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{p_{1}-1}}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})},\left\|\omega_{2}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{p_{2}-1}}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}\right).

Then

\displaystyle\|\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\|_{L^{1}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}\left\|(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{p-1}}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\omega_{1}\|_{L^{1}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}+\|\omega_{2}\|_{L^{1}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}\right)m

\displaystyle\leq\|\omega_{1}\|_{L^{1}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}\left\|\omega_{1}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{p-1}}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}+\|\omega_{2}\|_{L^{1}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}\left\|\omega_{2}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{p-1}}(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega_{1}]_{A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}}+[\omega_{2}]_{A_{p_{1};\rho}^{\rm loc}},

and the result follows by taking supremum over all such cubes
Q
.
∎

2.2.
Relations between
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
and
A_{p}

The following states that an
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
weight can be extended to an element of
A_{p}
based on a given cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)=\rho
(see
[
RV
, Lemma 1.1]
).

Theorem 2.2.1
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
,
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
, and
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)=\rho
. Then there exists a
\overline{\omega}\in A_{p}
such that
\overline{\omega}=\omega
on
Q
and

\displaystyle[\overline{\omega}]_{A_{p}}\leq\max\left(2^{np},(2\rho+1)^{np}\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Proof.

We may assume that the “left lower” corner of
Q
is the origin. Let
\widetilde{Q}
be the cube with
\ell(\widetilde{Q})=2\rho
centered at the origin. Define
\overline{\omega}=\omega
on
Q
. We extend
\overline{\omega}
from
Q
to
\widetilde{Q}
symmetrically with respect to all the coordinate axes. Subsequently, we extend
\overline{\omega}
from
\widetilde{Q}
to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
periodically with the period
2\rho
to all coordinates. Note that
\overline{\omega}
is defined everywhere except on the edges of such cubes.

Let
R
be a cube with
\ell(R)\geq\rho
. Choose a
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k\rho\leq\ell(R)<(k+1)\rho
. Then
R
is contained at most
N=(\lfloor(k+1)\rho\rfloor+1)^{n}
cubes
Q_{j}
being integer translates of
Q
. For
p=1
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)dx\right)\left\|\overline{\omega}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(R)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|R|}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\int_{Q_{j}}\overline{\omega}(x)dx\right)\sup_{1\leq l\leq N}\left\|\overline{\omega}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{l})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N}{|R|}\left(\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2+\frac{1}{\rho}\right)^{n}\rho^{n}\frac{1}{|Q|}\left(\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}

\displaystyle\leq(2\rho+1)^{n}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Similarly, for
1<p<\infty
, one can show that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{N}{|R|}\right)^{p}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\overline{\omega}(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\overline{\omega}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq(2\rho+1)^{np}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Now we consider the case where
\ell(R)<\rho
. We may assume that
R
contains the origin. Denote by
\Pi_{j}
the octants of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
j=1,\ldots,2^{n}
. Then
R\cap\Pi_{j}
is contained in a cube being integer translate of
Q
, which yields

\displaystyle\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)dx=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{n}}\int_{R\cap\Pi_{j}}\overline{\omega}(x)dx=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{n}}\int_{R\cap\Pi_{j}}\omega(x)dx\leq 2^{n}\int_{R}\omega(x)dx.

For
p=1
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)dx\right)\left\|\overline{\omega}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(R)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2^{n}}{|R|}\int_{R}\omega(x)dx\right)\sup_{1\leq j\leq 2^{n}}\left\|\overline{\omega}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(R\cap\Pi_{j})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2^{n}}{|R|}\int_{R}\omega(x)dx\right)\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(R)}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n}\|\omega\|_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Similarly, for
1<p<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{np}}{|R|^{p}}\left(\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)dx\right)\left(\int_{R}\overline{\omega}(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{np}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

the result follows by combining the above estimates.
∎

It is a standard fact that an
\omega\in A_{p}
weight is of doubling: there exists a constant
C>0
such that
\omega(tQ)\leq Ct^{n}\omega(Q)
for any
t\geq 1
and cubes
Q
. For weights in
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
, we have the exponential growths (see
[
RV
, Lemma 1.4]
).

Proposition 2.2.2
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle\omega(tQ)\leq\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{t}\omega(Q)

for all
t\geq 1
and cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)=\rho
.

Proof.

Consider first that
t\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
S=(t+1)Q\setminus(t-1)Q
. There is a sequence of disjoint cubes
I_{k}\subseteq S
with
\ell(I_{k})=\rho
,
|I_{k}\cap tQ|\geq\frac{\rho^{n}}{2^{n}}
, and each point of
S
, except those on the edges of
I_{k}
, belongs to at most
C_{n}
of the
I_{k}
, here we use the convention that
(t-1)Q=\emptyset
for
t=1
. For
p=1
, we have

\displaystyle\omega(S)\leq\sum_{k}\omega(I_{k})\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\sum_{k}\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{k})}^{-1}\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\sum_{k}\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{k}\cap tQ)}^{-1}.

Subsequently,

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{n}}{2^{n}}=\int_{I_{k}\cap tQ}\omega(x)\omega(x)^{-1}dx\leq\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{k}\cap tQ)}\left(\int_{I_{k}\cap tQ}\omega(x)dx\right),

and hence

\displaystyle\omega(S)\leq\frac{2^{n}}{\rho^{n}}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\sum_{k}\left(\int_{I_{k}\cap tQ}\omega(x)dx\right)\leq\frac{2^{n}}{\rho^{n}}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}C(n)\omega(tQ)

For
1<p<\infty
, using Hölder’s inequality, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{n}}{2^{n}}=\int_{I_{k}\cap tQ}\omega(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p}}dx\leq\left(\int_{I_{k}\cap tQ}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{I_{k}\cap tQ}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}.

Then we have similarly that

\displaystyle\omega(S)\leq\left(\frac{2^{n}}{\rho^{n}}\right)^{p}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\sum_{k}\left(\int_{I_{k}\cap tQ}\omega(x)dx\right)\leq\left(\frac{2^{n}}{\rho^{n}}\right)^{p}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}C(n)\omega(tQ).

Hence

\displaystyle\omega((t+1)Q)\leq C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\omega(tQ),\quad 1\leq p<\infty.

Iteration yields

\displaystyle\omega((t+1)Q)\leq\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{t}\omega(Q),\quad t\in\mathbb{N},\quad 1\leq p<\infty.

Since
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\geq 1
, the above estimate also holds for
t=0
. As a consequence, for any
t\geq 1
and
1\leq p<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\omega(tQ)

\displaystyle\leq\omega\left((\lfloor t\rfloor+1)Q\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{\lfloor t\rfloor}\omega(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{t}\omega(Q),

and the result follows.
∎

Remark 2.2.3
.

It is routine to check that
e^{c\left|\cdot\right|}\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
for
1\leq p<\infty
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
, which shows that the exponential bound in Proposition
2.2.2
cannot be improved to polynomial growths.

Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
0<\rho<\infty
. We say that
\mu
satisfies the local doubling property with respect to
\rho
provided that

\displaystyle\mu(Q)\leq D\cdot\mu\left(\frac{1}{3}Q\right),\quad\ell(Q)\leq\rho

(2.2.1)

for some constant
D>0
. In which case, we have either
\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})=0
or
\mu(Q)>0
for every cube
Q
.

In contrast to Proposition
2.2.2
,
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
weights possess the local doubling property.

Proposition 2.2.4
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. For any measurable subset
E
of a cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, it holds that

\displaystyle\omega(Q)\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\left(\frac{|Q|}{|E|}\right)^{p}\omega(E).

In particular,
\omega
satisfies the local doubling property with respect to
\rho
.

Proof.

If
p=1
, then

\displaystyle|E|=\int_{E}\omega(x)\omega(x)^{-1}dx\leq\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\int_{E}\omega(x)dx=\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\omega(E),

which yields

\displaystyle\omega(Q)\leq\frac{|Q|}{|E|}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\omega(Q)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\right)\omega(E)\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\frac{|Q|}{|E|}\omega(E).

If
1<p<\infty
, then

\displaystyle|E|

\displaystyle=\int_{E}\omega(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{E}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{E}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\omega(E)^{\frac{1}{p}}|Q|^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p}}\omega(E)^{\frac{1}{p}}|Q|^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)^{-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\frac{\omega(E)}{\omega(Q)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}|Q|,

which gives the result by routine simplification.
∎

2.3.
Characterization of
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
Weights via Local Maximal Function

The following covering lemma is taken from
[
GL
, Lemma 7.1.10]
and it can be viewed as a weak version of Besicovitch covering theorem (see
[
DE
, Theorem 18.1]
).

Lemma 2.3.1
.

Let
K
be a bounded set in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and for every
x\in X
, let
Q_{x}
be an open cube with center
x
and sides parallel to the axes. Then there are an
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and a sequence of points
\{x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
in
K
such that

\displaystyle K\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_{x_{j}}

and for almost all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\chi_{Q_{x_{j}}}(y)\leq 72^{n}.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
. We define the (uncentered) local Hardy-Littlewood maximal function
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}
by

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Q\\
\ell(Q)\leq\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}|f(y)|dy,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(2.3.1)

where
f
is a locally integrable function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We can now characterize the
A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
weights in terms of maximal function.

Proposition 2.3.2
.

Let
\omega
be a weight. Then
\omega\in A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
if and only if there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)\leq C\cdot\omega(x)\quad{\rm a.e.}.

(2.3.2)

The infimum of all such
C>0
in
(\ref{first A1})
equals to
[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

Proof.

Assume that
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
Q
is an arbitrary cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Then there is a set
N_{Q}
with
|N_{Q}|=0
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\cdot\omega(x)^{-1}\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}},\quad x\in Q\setminus N_{Q},

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\omega(x),\quad x\in Q\setminus N_{Q}.

Let
\{Q\}
be the countable collection of all cubes with rational vertices and
|Q|\leq\rho
. Denote by
N=\bigcup_{Q}N_{Q}
. Then we have
|N|=0
. Let
0<\varepsilon<1
be given. For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N
and cubes
R
that containing
x
with
\ell(R)<\rho
, there is a
Q\in\{Q\}
with
R\subseteq Q
and
|Q|\leq(1+\varepsilon)|R|
. As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\omega(t)dt\leq\frac{1+\varepsilon}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\leq(1+\varepsilon)[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\omega(x).

Taking
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R}\omega(t)dt\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\omega(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N.

One may easily see that the above estimate also holds for cubes
R
with
x\in R
and
\ell(R)=\rho
by approximating
R
with
x\in R_{N}\subsetneq R
,
\ell(R_{N})<\rho
, and
\chi_{R_{N}}\uparrow\chi_{R}
except on the boundary of
R
, hence (
2.3.2
) follows.

On the other hand, if (
2.3.2
) holds, say,

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)\leq C\cdot\omega(x)\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N

for some
N
with
|N|=0
, then for any cube
Q
that containing
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\leq{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)\leq C\cdot\omega(x).

Necessary,
\omega(x)>0
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\cdot\omega(x)^{-1}\leq C.

Since the above estimate holds for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N
, it holds that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=\sup_{\ell(Q)\leq\rho}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\right)\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\leq C,

and the proof is now complete.
∎

The following shows that
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
are all the same class regardless of the scaling
\rho
.

Theorem 2.3.3
.

Let
0<\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}<\infty
and
1\leq p<\infty
. Then

\displaystyle A_{p;\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}=A_{p:\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}.

In fact, it holds that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{np}}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{p\delta+1}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}},\quad\delta>1.

(2.3.3)

Proof.

For
0<\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}<\infty
, it is clear that
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}}
, which yields
A_{p;\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}\subseteq A_{p:\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}
. To prove the converse, it suffices to justify (
2.3.3
). Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Consider first that
p=1
. We use the maximal characterization of
A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
condition as in Proposition
2.3.2
. Choose an
N\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
|N|=0
and

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\omega(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N.

Fix an
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N
, we see that

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)

\displaystyle=\max\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Q\\
\ell(Q)\leq\rho/\delta\end{subarray}}\frac{\omega(Q)}{|Q|},\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Q\\
\rho/\delta<\ell(Q)\leq\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{\omega(Q)}{|Q|}\right)

\displaystyle=\max\left({\bf M}_{\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}(x),\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Q\\
\rho/\delta<\ell(Q)\leq\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{\omega(Q)}{|Q|}\right)

For any cube
Q
with
\frac{\rho}{\delta}<\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and
x\in Q
, there is a cube
R\subseteq Q
with
x\in R
and
\ell(R)=\frac{1}{\beta}\ell(Q)
,
\beta=\frac{\delta\ell(Q)}{\rho}>1
. Note that
\ell(R)=\frac{\rho}{\delta}
. We claim that
Q\subseteq(\beta+1)R
. Indeed, by letting
c_{R}
the center of
R
, for all
z\in Q
, one has

\displaystyle|z-c_{R}|_{\infty}\leq|z-x|_{\infty}+|x-c_{R}|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\ell(Q)}{2}+\frac{\ell(R)}{2}=(\beta+1)\frac{\ell(R)}{2},

as claimed. Using Proposition
2.2.2
, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q)}{|Q|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\omega(Q)}{|R|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\omega((\beta+1)R)}{|R|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(n)\max\left(\frac{1}{\ell(R)^{n}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{1;\ell(R)}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{\delta+1}\frac{\omega(R)}{|R|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(n)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{n}}{\rho^{n}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{\delta+1}\frac{\omega(R)}{|R|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(n)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{n}}{\rho^{n}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{\delta+1}{\bf M}_{\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}(x),

and hence

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left({\bf M}_{\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}(x),\left(C(n)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{n}}{\rho^{n}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{\delta+1}{\bf M}_{\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(C(n)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{n}}{\rho^{n}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{\delta+1}{\bf M}_{\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}(x),

which yields

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq\left(C(n)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{n}}{\rho^{n}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{\delta+1}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}.

For
1<p<\infty
, it holds that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}

\displaystyle=\max\left(\sup_{\ell(Q)\leq\rho/\delta}\frac{1}{|Q|^{p}}\omega(Q)[\omega^{\prime}(Q)]^{p-1},\sup_{\rho/\delta<\ell(Q)\leq\rho}\frac{1}{|Q|^{p}}\omega(Q)[\omega^{\prime}(Q)]^{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\max\left([\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}},\sup_{\rho/\delta<\ell(Q)\leq\rho}\frac{1}{|Q|^{p}}\omega(Q)[\omega^{\prime}(Q)]^{p-1}\right).

For any
\frac{\rho}{\delta}<\eta\leq\rho
and cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)=\eta
, Proposition
2.2.2
yields

\displaystyle\omega(Q)[\omega^{\prime}(Q)]^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\omega\left(\frac{\eta\delta}{\rho}\cdot\frac{\rho}{\eta\delta}Q\right)\left[\omega^{\prime}\left(\frac{\eta\delta}{\rho}\cdot\frac{\rho}{\eta\delta}Q\right)\right]^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{np}}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{p\frac{\eta\delta}{\rho}}\omega\left(\frac{\rho}{\eta\delta}Q\right)\left[\omega^{\prime}\left(\frac{\rho}{\eta\delta}Q\right)\right]^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{np}}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{p\delta^{1/n}}\omega\left(\frac{\rho}{\eta\delta}Q\right)\left[\omega^{\prime}\left(\frac{\rho}{\eta\delta}Q\right)\right]^{p-1}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left([\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}},\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{np}}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{p\delta}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{\delta^{np}}{\rho^{np}},1\right)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{p\delta}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/\delta}^{\rm loc}},

which yields the result for
1<p<\infty
.
∎

In view of Theorem
2.3.3
, we write
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
instead of
A_{p;1}^{\rm loc}
and the symbol
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
is used only when we wish to measure
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
in terms of
\left[\cdot\right]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

On the other hand, the
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
weights can also be characterized in terms of weak type boundedness of maximal function.

Proposition 2.3.4
.

For
0<\rho<\infty
and
1\leq p<\infty
, the weak type
(p,p)
estimate

\displaystyle\omega(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)>\lambda\})\leq\frac{C}{\lambda^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x)|^{p}\omega(x)dx

(2.3.4)

entails
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq C
.

Proof.

Let
Q
be an arbitrary cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Consider first that
p=1
. Then
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)\geq{\rm Avg}_{Q}|f|
for all
x\in Q
, it follows by (
2.3.4
) that for all
0<\lambda<{\rm Avg}_{Q}|f|
, we have

\displaystyle\omega(Q)\leq\omega(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)>\lambda\})\leq\frac{C}{\lambda^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x)|\omega(x)dx.

(2.3.5)

Taking
f\chi_{Q}
instead of
f
in (
2.3.5
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left({\rm Avg}_{Q}|f|\right)^{p}=\frac{1}{|Q|^{p}}\left(\int_{Q}|f(t)|dt\right)^{p}\leq\frac{C}{\omega(Q)}\int_{Q}|f(x)|\omega(x)dx.

(2.3.6)

Taking
S\subseteq Q
be a measurable set and
f=\chi_{S}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|S|}{|Q|}\right)^{p}\leq C\frac{\omega(S)}{\omega(Q)}.

(2.3.7)

Now we consider first the case where
p=1
. Denote by

\displaystyle m_{E}=\inf\{b>0:|\{x\in E:\omega(x)<b\}|>0\}

for any measurable set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then for every
a>m_{Q}
, there exists a measurable set
S_{a}\subseteq Q
with
|S_{a}|>0
and
\omega(x)<a
for all
x\in S_{a}
. Applying (
2.3.7
) to the set
S_{a}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\leq\frac{C}{|S_{a}|}\int_{S_{a}}\omega(t)dt\leq C\cdot a,

which yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(t)dt\leq C\cdot\omega(x)

for all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and almost everywhere
x\in Q
. By repeating the proof of Proposition
2.3.2
, one deduces that
\omega\in A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
with
[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}}^{\rm loc}\leq C
.

It remains to consider the case where
1<p<\infty
. Let
f_{N}=\min(\omega^{-\frac{1}{p-1}},N)\chi_{Q}
in (
2.3.6
),
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\displaystyle\omega(Q)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min\left(\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}},N\right)dx\right)^{p}\leq C\int_{Q}\min(\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}},N)dx,

which yields

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\min\left(\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}},N\right)dx\right)^{p-1}\leq C.

Taking
N\rightarrow\infty
, monotone convergence theorem implies that
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq C
, which gives the result.
∎

It will be seen later that the converse of Proposition
2.3.4
also holds.

The following is a local form of Fefferman-Stein type inequality
[
FS
]
. Note that the estimate (
2.3.8
) below gives the converse statement of Proposition
2.3.4
for the case where
p=1
.

Theorem 2.3.5
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\omega
be a weight. Then

\displaystyle\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)>\alpha\right\}\right)\leq\frac{72^{n}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x)|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)dx,\quad\alpha>0,

(2.3.8)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C_{n,q}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x)|^{p}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)dx,\quad 1<p<\infty.

(2.3.9)

Proof.

The estimate (
2.3.9
) will follow by (
2.3.8
) and Marcinkiewicz interpolation theorem as we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\omega)}=\left\|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\|f\|_{L^{\infty}({\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega)}

by noting that the measures
\omega dx,{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega dx
are absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure and vice versa.

It remains to show (
2.3.8
). Denote by
E_{\alpha}=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)>\alpha\right\}
. Let
K\subseteq E_{\alpha}
be an arbitrary compact set. For each
x\in K
, there is a cube
Q_{x}
that containing
x
such that
\ell(Q_{x})\leq\rho
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q_{x}|}\int_{Q_{x}}|f(y)|dy>\alpha.

We could assume all the cubes
Q_{x}
are open. Using Lemma
2.3.1
, there are an
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and a sequence
\{x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}\subseteq K
such that

\displaystyle K\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_{x_{j}}

and for almost everywhere
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\chi_{Q_{x_{j}}}(y)\leq 72^{n}.

On the other hand, for each
z\in Q_{x_{j}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q_{x_{j}}\right|}\int_{Q_{x_{j}}}\omega(y)dy\leq{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(z).

Multiplying the above inequalities with
|f(z)|
and integrating with respect to
\left(Q_{x_{j}},dz\right)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\omega\left(Q_{x_{j}}\right)\frac{1}{\left|Q_{x_{j}}\right|}\int_{Q_{x_{j}}}|f(z)|dz\leq\int_{Q_{x_{j}}}|f(z)|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(z)dz.

Then

\displaystyle\omega\left(Q_{x_{j}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\int_{Q_{x_{j}}}|f(z)|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(z)dz

and hence

\displaystyle\omega(K)

\displaystyle=\omega\left(\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_{x_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\omega\left(Q_{x_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\chi_{Q_{x_{j}}}(z)\right)|f(z)|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(z)dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{72^{n}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(z)|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(z)dz.

The estimate (
2.3.8
) follows by the inner regularity of the Lebesgue measure applied to
E_{\alpha}
.
∎

In the sequel, for
0<\rho<\infty
, we define the center local maximal function
\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)=\sup_{0<r\leq\rho/2}\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x)|}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}|f(y)|dy,

where
f
is a locally integrable function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Note that the supremum is taken over all cubes
Q_{r}(x)
with lengths
\ell(Q_{r}(x))=2r\leq\rho
.

The converse of Proposition
2.3.4
for the case where
p=1
is also shown in the following if we replace the maximal function
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}
by
\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}
.

Theorem 2.3.6
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\omega\in A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\right\|_{L^{1}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{1,\infty}(\omega)}\leq 72^{n}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

Conversely, if
\omega
is a weight such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\right\|_{L^{1}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{1,\infty}(\omega)}\leq C

(2.3.10)

for some constant
C>0
, then
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
.

Proof.

One can show this theorem by noting that
\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\leq{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f
and applying Proposition
2.3.2
and (
2.3.8
) of Theorem
2.3.5
. However, we give a somewhat different proof as follows.

Denote by
d\mu=\omega dx
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mu}f(x)=\sup_{r>0}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{r}(x))}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}|f(y)|d\mu(y)

for admissible functions
f
. We first show that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\mu}\right\|_{L^{1}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{1,\infty}(\omega)}\leq 72^{n}

(2.3.11)

Let
\alpha>0
be given. Denote by
E_{\alpha}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{M}_{\mu}f(x)>\alpha\}
. Let
K
be an arbitrary compact subset of
E_{\alpha}
. For each
x\in K
, there exists a cube
Q_{r_{x}}(x)
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{r_{x}}(x))}\int_{Q_{r_{x}}(x)}|f(y)|d\mu(y)>\alpha.

Using Lemma
2.3.1
, there are an
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and a sequence
\{x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}\subseteq K
such that

\displaystyle K\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_{r_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})

and for almost everywhere
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\chi_{Q_{r_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})}(y)\leq 72^{n}.

Then

\displaystyle\mu(K)\leq\mu\left(\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_{r_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\right)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu\left(Q_{r_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\right)\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{Q_{r_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})}|f(y)|d\mu(y).

The absolute continuity of
\mu
with respect to Lebesgue measure yields

\displaystyle\mu(K)\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\chi_{Q_{r_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})}(y)|f(y)|d\mu(y)\leq\frac{72^{n}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)|d\mu(y).

The inner regularity of
\mu
yields

\displaystyle\omega(E_{\alpha})=\mu(E_{\alpha})\leq\frac{72^{n}}{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}=\frac{72^{n}}{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\omega)}.

Now we observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{0<r\leq\rho}\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x)|}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}|f(y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<r\leq\rho}\frac{\omega(Q_{r}(x))}{|Q_{r}(x)|}\left\|\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{r}(x))}\frac{1}{\omega(Q_{r}(x))}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}|f(y)|\omega(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\mathcal{M_{\mu}}f(x).

As a result,

\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha>0}\alpha\cdot\omega(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)>\alpha\})\leq 72^{n}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)|\omega(y)dy,

as expected. Finally, (
2.3.10
) follows by Proposition
2.3.4
as
\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\leq{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f
.
∎

On the other hand, the converse statement of Proposition
2.3.4
for the case where
1<p<\infty
is shown in the following as
2^{-n}{\bf M}_{\rho/2}^{\rm loc}f\leq\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\leq{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f
holds pointwise.

Theorem 2.3.7
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1<p<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\rho/3}^{\rm loc}\right\|_{L^{p}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{p}(\omega)}\leq C(n,p)[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

(2.3.12)

Conversely, if
\omega
is a weight such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\right\|_{L^{p}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{p}(\omega)}\leq C

for some constant
C>0
, then
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
.

Proof.

Fix an open cube
Q=Q_{r}(z)
with
\ell(Q)=2r\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx=\frac{\omega(Q)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\omega^{\prime}(3Q)}{|Q|^{\frac{p}{p-1}}}\left(\frac{|Q|}{\omega(Q)}\left(\frac{1}{\omega^{\prime}(3Q)}\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx\right)^{p-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

For any
y\in Q
, it holds that
Q\subseteq Q_{2r}(y)\subseteq Q_{3r}(z)=3Q
and hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\prime}(3Q)}\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx\leq\frac{1}{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2r}(y))}\int_{Q_{2r}(y)}|f(x)|dx\leq\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)(y).

Raising both sides with exponent
p-1
and taking integral with respect to
(dy,Q)
, we have

\displaystyle|Q|\left(\frac{1}{\omega^{\prime}(3Q)}\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx\right)^{p-1}\leq\int_{Q}\left[\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)\right]^{p-1}dy,

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx\leq\frac{\omega(Q)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\omega^{\prime}(3Q)}{|Q|^{\frac{p}{p-1}}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega(Q)}\int_{Q}\left[\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)\right]^{p-1}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Note that
\ell(3Q)=3\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Hence

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q)\omega^{\prime}(3Q)^{p-1}}{|Q|^{p}}\leq 3^{np}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}|f(x)|dx\leq 3^{\frac{np}{p-1}}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\left[\left[\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)\right]^{p-1}\omega^{-1}\right](z)\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Taking supremum over all
Q=Q_{r}(z)
with
\ell(Q)=2r\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\rho/3}^{\rm loc}f\leq 3^{\frac{np}{p-1}}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\left[\left[\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)\right]^{p-1}\omega^{-1}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Taking
L^{p}(\omega)
norms both sides, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\rho/3}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{\frac{np}{p-1}}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\left[\left[\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)\right]^{p-1}\omega^{-1}\right]\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{\frac{np}{p-1}}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}\left\|\left[\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)\right]^{p-1}\omega^{-1}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle=3^{\frac{np}{p-1}}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\left(|f|\omega^{\prime-1}\right)\right\|_{L^{p}(\omega^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{\frac{np}{p-1}}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{p^{\prime}}(\omega)}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\omega^{\prime})\rightarrow L^{p}(\omega^{\prime})}\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)},

and the estimate (
2.3.12
) follows provided we show that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\right\|_{L^{q}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{q}(\omega)}\leq C(n,q)<\infty

(2.3.13)

for any
1<q<\infty
and weight
\omega
. Indeed, by combining (
2.3.11
) and the simple fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\omega}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\omega)\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\omega)}\leq 1,

then (
2.3.13
) follows by Marcinkiewicz interpolation theorem.

As in the last part of the proof of Theorem
2.3.6
, the converse statement follows by Proposition
2.3.4
.
∎

Denote the general uncentered local maximal function by

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\mu,\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Q\\
\ell(Q)\leq\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\mu(Q)}\int_{Q}|f(y)|d\mu(y)

for admissible functions
f
, where
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. A generalization of Theorems
2.3.6
and
2.3.7
in terms of uncentered local maximal function is given as follows.

Proposition 2.3.8
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Assume that
\mu
satisfies the local doubling property
(\ref{local doubling property})
with respect to
\rho
. Suppose that
\mu
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure. Then

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\mu,\rho/3}^{\rm loc}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mu)\rightarrow L^{1,\infty}(\mu)}\leq D

(2.3.14)

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\mu,\rho/3}^{\rm loc}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mu)\rightarrow L^{p}(\mu)}\leq C_{n,p}\cdot D

(2.3.15)

for every
1<p<\infty
.

Proof.

We may assume that
\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})>0
. Then
\mu(Q)>0
for every cube
Q
. It suffices to prove (
2.3.14
) since (
2.3.15
) follows by Marcinkiewicz interpolation theorem since

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\mu,\rho/3}^{\rm loc}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mu)}\leq 1.

Let
f\in L^{1}(\mu)
and
\alpha>0
be given. Denote by
E_{\alpha}=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\mu,\rho/3}^{\rm loc}f(x)>\alpha\right\}
. Assume that
K
is a compact subset of
E_{\alpha}
. For every
x\in K
, there is a cube
Q_{x}
such that
x\in Q_{x}\subseteq E_{\alpha}
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x})}\int_{Q_{x}}|f(y)|d\mu(y)>\alpha.

Since
K
is compact,
K
possesses a finite covering
\left\{Q_{x_{i}}\right\}_{i=1}^{k}
. By
[
GL
, Lemma 2.1.5]
, there is a finite disjoint subcollection
\left\{Q_{x_{j_{i}}}\right\}_{i=1}^{l}
of
\left\{Q_{x_{i}}\right\}_{i=1}^{k}
such that

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}Q_{x_{i}}\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{l}3Q_{x_{j_{i}}}.

As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\mu(K)\leq\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}Q_{x_{i}}\right)\leq\mu\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{l}3Q_{x_{j_{i}}}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{l}\mu\left(3Q_{x_{j_{i}}}\right)\leq D\sum_{i=1}^{l}\mu\left(Q_{x_{j_{i}}}\right),

where the latter is due to the fact that
\ell\left(3Q_{x_{j_{i}}}\right)=3\ell\left(Q_{x_{j_{i}}}\right)\leq\rho
, hence the local doubling property applies.

Subsequently, it holds that

\displaystyle D\sum_{i=1}^{l}\mu\left(Q_{x_{j_{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D}{\alpha}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\int_{Q_{x_{j_{i}}}}|f(y)|d\mu(y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D}{\alpha}\int_{\bigcup_{i=1}^{l}Q_{x_{j_{i}}}}|f(y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)|d\mu(y).

The inner regularity of
\mu
entails

\displaystyle\mu(E_{\alpha})\leq\frac{D}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)|d\mu(y),

hence (
2.3.14
) follows.
∎

Recall that the Lebesgue differentiation theorem says that

\displaystyle\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{B_{r}(x)}\int_{B_{r}(x)}|f(x)-f(y)|dy=0

holds almost everywhere
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
f
is a locally integrable function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. A standard proof to the above differentiation theorem is to use the weak type
(1,1)
boundedness of
{\bf M}
. Having established the weak type boundedness in Proposition
2.3.8
, one expects the following differentiation theorem in terms of local doubling measure
\mu
.

Corollary 2.3.9
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Assume that
\mu
satisfies the local doubling property
(\ref{local doubling property})
with respect to
\rho
. Suppose that
\mu
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure and
Q
is a fixed cube. For each
x\in\overline{Q}
, let
\left\{\overline{Q}_{x,N}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence of closed cubes that containing
x
with
\overline{Q}=\overline{Q}_{x,1}\supseteq\overline{Q}_{x,2}\supseteq\cdots\supseteq\overline{Q}_{x,N}\supseteq\cdots
and each
\overline{Q}_{x,N+1}
is a dyadic subcube of
\overline{Q}_{x,N}
. Then for any
f\in L_{\mu}^{1}\left(\overline{Q}\right)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x,N})}\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-f(x)|d\mu(y)=0

holds almost everywhere
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with respect to
\mu
.

Proof.

Since the lengths of
Q_{x,N}
decreases to zero uniformly in
x
, we may assume that each
Q_{x,N}
satisfies
\ell(Q_{x,N})\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. Let
\varepsilon,\eta>0
be given. Extend
f
canonically to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
by letting
f(y)=0
for
y\in\overline{Q}^{c}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)|d\mu(y)<\infty

and there is a compactly supported continuous function
g
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)-g(y)|d\mu(y)<\varepsilon\cdot\frac{\eta}{2(D+1)}.

Fix an
x\in Q
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-f(x)|d\mu(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-g(y)+g(y)-g(x)+g(x)-f(x)|d\mu(y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-g(y)|d\mu(y)+\int_{Q_{x,N}}|g(y)-g(x)|d\mu(y)+\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(x)-g(x)|d\mu(y)

\displaystyle\leq\mu(Q_{x,N}){\bf M}_{\mu,\rho/3}^{\rm loc}(f-g)(x)+\int_{Q_{x,N}}|g(y)-g(x)|d\mu(y)+|f(x)-g(x)|\mu(Q_{x,N}).

The uniform continuity of
g
implies

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x,N})}\int_{Q_{x,N}}|g(y)-g(x)|d\mu(y)=0.

Using (
2.3.14
) of Proposition
2.3.8
, we have

\displaystyle\mu\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x,N})}\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-f(x)|d\mu(y)>\varepsilon\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mu\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\mu,\rho/3}^{\rm loc}(f-g)(x)>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right\}\right)+\mu\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|f(x)-g(x)|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(D+1)}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)-g(y)|d\mu(y)

\displaystyle<\eta.

The arbitrariness of
\eta>0
gives

\displaystyle\mu\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x,N})}\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-f(x)|d\mu(y)>\varepsilon\right\}\right)=0.

By letting
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{k}
,
k=1,2,\ldots
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mu\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x,N})}\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-f(x)|d\mu(y)>0\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu\left(\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x,N})}\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-f(x)|d\mu(y)>\frac{1}{k}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mu\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{x,N})}\int_{Q_{x,N}}|f(y)-f(x)|d\mu(y)>\frac{1}{k}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=0,

which completes the proof.
∎

2.4.
The Local Reverse Hölder’s Inequality for
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}

Given a probability space
(X,\mu)
, we have by Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\int_{X}|f(x)|d\mu(x)\leq\left(\int_{X}|f(x)|^{p}d\mu(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},\quad 1<p<\infty

for all measurable functions
f
. The local reverse form of Hölder’s inequality is valid for
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
weights. First we start with the following lemma.

Lemma 2.4.1
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
,
0<\alpha<1
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then there exists a
0<\beta<1
such that whenever
S
is a measurable subset of a cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and
|S|\leq\alpha|Q|
, it holds that
\omega(S)\leq\beta\omega(Q)
.

Proof.

Taking
f=\chi_{A}
in the
Property 8
of Proposition
2.1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|A|}{|Q|}\right)^{p}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\frac{\omega(A)}{\omega(Q)}

(2.4.1)

whenever
A\subseteq Q
is a measurable set. Let
S=Q\setminus A
. Then

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{|S|}{|Q|}\right)^{p}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\left(1-\frac{\omega(A)}{\omega(Q)}\right).

Given
0<\alpha<1
, we let

\displaystyle\beta=1-\frac{(1-\alpha)^{p}}{[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}}.

A routine simplification yields
\omega(S)\leq\beta\omega(Q)
.
∎

The local reverse Hölder’s inequality is given as follows.

Theorem 2.4.2
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\omega
be a weight. Assume that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
which satisfies the local doubling property
(\ref{local doubling property})
with respect to
\rho
. Suppose that
\mu
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure and there exist
0<\alpha,\beta<1
such that

\displaystyle\mu(S)\leq\alpha\mu(Q)\quad\text{entails}\quad\int_{S}\omega(x)d\mu(x)\leq\beta\int_{Q}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

whenever
S
is a measurable subset of a cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. Then there exist
0<C,\gamma<\infty
which depend only on
n
,
D
,
\alpha
, and
\beta
such that for every cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\mu(Q)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}d\mu(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\leq\frac{C}{\mu(Q)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)d\mu(x).

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. Denote by

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)d\mu(x).

Define
\alpha_{k}=(D\alpha^{-1})^{k}\alpha_{0}
for each
k\geq 1
. For each dyadic subcube
R
of
Q
, let

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu(R)}\int_{R}\omega(y)d\mu(y)>\alpha_{k}

(2.4.2)

be the selection criterion. The cube
Q
is not selected since it does not satisfy the selection criterion. We divide
Q
into
2^{n}
dyadic subcubes and select those which satisfy (
2.4.2
). Then we subdivide each unselected cubes into
2^{n}
dyadic subcubes and continue in this way indefinitely. Denote by
\{Q_{k,j}\}_{j}
the collection of all selected subcubes of
Q
. We have the following properties.

(1)

\alpha_{k}<\dfrac{1}{\mu(Q_{k,j})}\displaystyle\int_{Q_{k,j}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)\leq D\cdot 2^{k}
.

(2)

For
\mu
-a.e.
x\in Q\setminus U_{k}
, we have
\omega(x)\leq\alpha_{k}
, where
U_{k}=\bigcup_{j}Q_{k,j}
.

(3)

Each
Q_{k+1,j}
is contained in some
Q_{k,l}
.

Property (1) is satisfied since the unique dyadic parent of
Q_{k,j}
is not chosen in the selection procedure and is contained in
3Q_{k,j}
with
\ell(3Q_{k,j})=3\ell(Q_{k,j})\leq\rho
hence the local doubling property applies. Property (2) follows by Corollary
2.3.9
since each
x\in Q
is contained in a sequence of unselected closed dyadic cubes of decreasing lengths. On the other hand, each
Q_{k,j}
is the maximal dyadic subcube of
Q
which satisfies (
2.4.2
). Since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{k+1,j})}\int_{Q_{k+1,k}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)>\alpha_{k+1}>\alpha_{k},

it follows that
Q_{k+1,j}
is contained in some maximal cube that satisfying (
2.4.2
), and hence Property (3) follows.

For fixed
k,l
, we have

\displaystyle 2^{n}\alpha_{k}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{k,l})}\int_{Q_{k,l}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{k,l})}\int_{Q_{k,l}\cap U_{k+1}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{k,l})}\sum_{j}\int_{Q_{k+1,j}\cap Q_{k,l}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{k,l})}\sum_{j:Q_{k+1,j}\subseteq Q_{k,l}}\mu(Q_{k+1,j})\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{k+1,j})}\int_{Q_{k+1,j}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\mu(Q_{k,l})}\sum_{j:Q_{k+1,j}\subseteq Q_{k,l}}\mu(Q_{k+1,j})\alpha_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu(Q_{k,l}\cap U_{k+1})}{\mu(Q_{k,l})}\alpha_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu(Q_{k,l}\cap U_{k+1})}{\mu(Q_{k,l})}2^{n}\alpha^{-1}\alpha_{k},

it follows that
\mu(Q_{k,l}\cap U_{k+1})\leq\alpha\mu(Q_{k,l})
. We have by assumption that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{k,l}\cap U_{k+1}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)\leq\beta\int_{Q_{k,l}}\omega(x)d\mu(x).

Summing the above expression with respect to
l
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{U_{k+1}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{U_{k}\cap U_{k+1}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\int_{Q_{k,l}\cap U_{k+1}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq\beta\sum_{l}\int_{Q_{k,l}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\beta\int_{U_{k}}\omega(x)d\mu(x),

which yields

\displaystyle\int_{U_{k}}\omega(x)d\mu(x)\leq\beta^{k}\int_{U_{0}}\omega(x)d\mu(x).

We also have
\mu(\alpha_{k+1})\leq\alpha\mu(U_{k})
and hence
\mu(U_{k})\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. Then

\displaystyle Q=(Q\setminus U_{0})\bigcup\left(\bigcup_{k=0}^{\infty}(U_{k}\setminus U_{k+1})\right)

modulo a set of
\mu
measure zero. Let
\gamma>0
be determined later. Since
\omega(x)\leq\alpha_{k}
for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in Q\setminus U_{k}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{Q\setminus U_{0}}\omega(x)^{\gamma}\omega(x)d\mu(x)+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{U_{k}\setminus U_{k+1}}\omega(x)^{\gamma}\omega(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{0}^{\gamma}\omega(Q\setminus U_{0})+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k+1}^{\gamma}\omega(U_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{0}^{\gamma}\omega(Q\setminus U_{0})+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left((D\cdot\alpha^{-1})^{k+1}\alpha_{0}\right)^{\gamma}\beta^{k}\omega(U_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{0}^{\gamma}\left(1+(D\cdot\alpha^{-1})^{\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(D\cdot\alpha^{-1})^{\gamma k}\beta^{k}\right)\omega(Q)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\mu(Q)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)d\mu(x)\right)^{\gamma}\left(1+\frac{(D\alpha^{-1})^{\gamma}}{1-(D\cdot\alpha^{-1})^{\gamma}\beta}\right)\int_{Q}\omega(x)d\mu(x),

where
\gamma
is chosen such that

\displaystyle 0<\gamma<\frac{-\log\beta}{\log D-\log\alpha}.

By letting

\displaystyle C=\left(1+\frac{(D\cdot\alpha^{-1})^{\gamma}}{1-(D\cdot\alpha^{-1})^{\gamma}\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma+1}},

the result follows.
∎

Remark 2.4.3
.

If
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
,
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
, then by letting
\mu
to be the Lebesgue measure in Theorem
2.4.2
, one can use Lemma
2.4.1
to conclude that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\leq\frac{C}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx,\quad\ell(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{3}

(2.4.3)

with the constants
\gamma,C>0
depending only on
n
,
p
, and
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
. In fact, the constant
C
