can be taken as

\displaystyle C=\left[1+\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{-1}\right)}\right]^{\frac{2\log\frac{3^{n}}{\alpha}}{2\log\frac{3^{n}}{\alpha}-\log\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{-1}\right)}}

(2.4.4)

(see
[
GL
, Remark 7.2.3]
). Since
1-\frac{3}{4}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{-1}}\geq\frac{1}{4}
and the function
t\rightarrow\sqrt{t}-t
is decreasing on
(\frac{1}{4},1)
, the constant
C
increases as
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
increases.

On the other hand, the scaling
\frac{\rho}{3}
in (
2.4.3
) is purely technical. Fix a
0<\rho^{\prime}<\infty
. For any weight
\omega\in A_{p;\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}
, since
A_{p;\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}=A_{p;3\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}
, if we let
\rho=3\rho^{\prime}
, then (
2.4.3
) reduces to

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\leq\frac{C}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx,\quad\ell(Q)\leq\rho^{\prime},

where the constant
C
now depends on a larger quantity
[\omega]_{A_{p;3\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}}
, and
C
is indeed larger as well by the argument following (
2.4.4
).

Given a nonnegative measurable function
\omega
, we know that
{\bf M}\omega
is not integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
unless
\omega=0
almost everywhere. Moreover,
{\bf M}\omega
is generally not locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
unless
\omega
satisfies the
L\log L
condition (see
[
SE2
, 8.14, page 43]
). Nevertheless, with the aid of the local reverse Hölder’s inequality, we can show that
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
if
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
.

Theorem 2.4.4
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proof.

Let
M>0
and
R=[-M,M]^{n}
. Let
0<\rho^{\prime}<\infty
be determined later. Note that
\omega\in A_{p;3\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}
. Using Theorem
2.4.3
, there are
0<\gamma,C<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\leq\frac{C}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx,\quad\ell(Q)\leq\rho^{\prime}.

(2.4.5)

Let
\overline{R}=[-M-2\rho,M+2\rho]^{n}
. Then it is easy to see that
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)={\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\left(\omega\chi_{\overline{R}}\right)(x)
for all
x\in R
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\int_{R}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{R}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\left(\omega\chi_{\overline{R}}\right)(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{R}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\left(\omega\chi_{\overline{R}}\right)(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}|R|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq|R|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\bf M}\left(\omega\chi_{\overline{R}}\right)(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\gamma)|R|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\omega\chi_{\overline{R}}\right)(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle=C(n,\gamma)|R|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\left(\int_{\overline{R}}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}},

where we have used the standard fact that
{\bf M}:L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is bounded for any
1<q<\infty
in the third inequality. Now we choose
\rho^{\prime}=2M+4\rho
in (
2.4.5
). Then
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega\in L^{1}(R)
. Since the cube
R
is of arbitrary large, the local integrability of
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\omega
follows.
∎

An
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
weight
\omega
is indeed locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, but we can show that
\omega^{1+\sigma}
is also locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
as well for some
\sigma>0
.

Theorem 2.4.5
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then there exists a constant
\gamma>0
depending only on
n
,
p
, and
[\omega]_{A_{p}^{\rm loc}}
such that
\omega^{1+\gamma}\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
.

Proof.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
C
be the constant in Remark
2.4.3
. When
p=1
, we apply Remark
2.4.3
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}\leq\left(\frac{C}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)^{1+\gamma}\leq C^{1+\gamma}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{1+\gamma}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}

for almost everywhere
x\in Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. Therefore, we have
\omega^{1+\gamma}\in A_{1;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle\left[\omega^{1+\gamma}\right]_{A_{1;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}}\leq C^{1+\gamma}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{1+\gamma}.

When
1<p<\infty
, Remark
2.4.3
entails some
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}>0
and
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma_{1}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}(1+\gamma_{2})}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx

where
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. Taking
\gamma=\min(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
, the above estimates are satisfied with
\gamma
in place of
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
. Hence
\omega^{1+\gamma}\in A_{p;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}
with

\displaystyle\left[\omega^{1+\gamma}\right]_{A_{p;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}}\leq\left(C_{1}C_{2}^{p-1}\right)^{1+\gamma}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{1+\gamma}.

which completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.4.6
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then there exists a
q
depending only on
n
,
p
, and
[\omega]_{A_{p}^{\rm loc}}
such that
\omega\in A_{q}^{\rm loc}
. In other words,

\displaystyle A_{p}^{\rm loc}=\bigcup_{q\in(1,p)}A_{q}^{\rm loc}.

Proof.

Let
\gamma
,
C_{1}
, and
C_{2}
be as in the proof of Theorem
2.4.5
. For
0<\rho<\infty
, since
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
, it holds that
\omega^{1+\gamma}\in A_{p;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}
. By letting
\delta=\frac{1}{1+\gamma}
and

\displaystyle q=p\frac{1}{1+\gamma}+1-\frac{1}{1+\gamma}=\frac{p+\gamma}{1+\gamma}

in Lemma
2.1.4
, we have
\omega\in A_{q;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}
,
1<q<p
, and

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{q;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}}=\left[\left(\omega^{1+\gamma}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\right]_{A_{q;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}}\leq\left[\omega^{1+\gamma}\right]_{A_{p;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\leq C_{1}C_{2}^{p-1}[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},

then the result follows.
∎

Lemma 2.4.7
.

Let
S
be an operator that maps
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to
L^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with norm
B
. Suppose that
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. For any finite measure set
A
and
0<q<1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{A}|S(f)(x)|^{q}dx\leq\frac{1}{1-q}B^{q}|A|^{1-q}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\int_{A}{\bf M}f(x)^{q}dx\leq\frac{1}{1-q}3^{nq}|A|^{1-q}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\int_{A}|S(f)(x)|^{q}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}q\alpha^{q-1}|\{x\in A:|S(f)(x)|>\alpha\}|d\alpha

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\frac{B}{|A|}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}}+\int_{\frac{B}{|A|}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}}^{\infty}\right)q\alpha^{q-1}|\{x\in A:|S(f)(x)|>\alpha\}|d\alpha

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B^{q}}{|A|^{q}}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}|A|+\int_{\frac{B}{|A|}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}}^{\infty}q\alpha^{q-1}\alpha^{-1}B\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}d\alpha

\displaystyle=B^{q}|A|^{1-q}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}+\frac{q}{1-q}B\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\left(\frac{B}{|A|}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-q}B^{q}|A|^{1-q}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q},

which completes the proof.
∎

As an application of the local reverse Hölder’s inequality, we can decompose an
A_{1}^{\rm loc}
weight as a bounded function multiplied with a maximal function with small exponent.

Theorem 2.4.8
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\omega\in A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then there exist
0<\varepsilon<1
, a nonnegative function
k
such that
k,k^{-1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and a nonnegative locally integrable function satisfying
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)<\infty
almost everywhere with

\displaystyle\omega(x)=k(x){\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm a.e.}.

(2.4.6)

Proof.

Note that
\omega\in A_{1;3\rho}^{\rm loc}
. By Remark
2.4.3
, there exist
0<\gamma,C<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\leq\frac{C}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\leq C[\omega]_{A_{1;3\rho}^{\rm loc}}\omega(x),\quad\ell(Q)\leq\rho

for all
x\in Q\setminus E_{Q}
, where
E_{Q}\subseteq Q
satisfies
|E_{Q}|=0
. Let

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\frac{1}{1+\gamma},\quad f=\omega^{1+\gamma}=\omega^{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}},

and
N
be the union of
E_{Q}
over all
Q
with rational vertices and
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)\leq C^{1+\gamma}[\omega]_{A_{1;3\rho}^{\rm loc}}\omega(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus N.

By letting

\displaystyle k(x)=\frac{f(x)^{\varepsilon}}{{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{\varepsilon}},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(
2.4.6
) follows as
C^{-1}[\omega]_{A_{1;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{-1}\leq k(x)\leq 1
almost everywhere.
∎

Remark 2.4.9
.

The converse statement of Theorem
2.4.8
is somewhat tricky. Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
0<\varepsilon<1
, and
f
be a locally integrable function with
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)<\infty
almost everywhere. Define
\omega={\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f
. We claim that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(y)^{\varepsilon}dy\leq\frac{C_{n}}{1-\varepsilon}{\bf M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{\varepsilon}

(2.4.7)

for almost everywhere
x\in Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. We write

\displaystyle f=f\chi_{3Q}+f\chi_{(3Q)^{c}}.

Then Lemma
2.4.7
entails

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}(f\chi_{3Q})(y)^{\varepsilon}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n}^{\prime}}{1-\varepsilon}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(f\chi_{3Q})(y)dy\right)^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n}^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{\varepsilon}

for all
x\in Q
since
\ell(3Q)=3\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. On the other hand, for all
x,y\in Q
and cube
R
with
y\in R
,
R\cap(3Q)^{c}\neq\emptyset
,
\ell(R)\leq\rho
, we have
x\in 2R
. Indeed, choose a
z_{0}\in R\cap(3Q)^{c}
. Denote by
c_{Q}
and
c_{R}
the centers of
Q
and
R
respectively. Then

\displaystyle\frac{3\ell(Q)}{2}\leq|z_{0}-c_{Q}|_{\infty}\leq|z_{0}-y|_{\infty}+|y-c_{R}|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\ell(R)}{2}+\frac{\ell(Q)}{2},

which yields
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{1}{2}\ell(R)
. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle|x-c_{R}|_{\infty}\leq|x-y|_{\infty}+|y-c_{R}|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\ell(Q)}{2}+\frac{\ell(R)}{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\ell(R)+\ell(R)\right)<\ell(R),

which shows that
x\in 2R
. Therefore, for any
y\in Q
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\left(f\chi_{(3Q)^{c}}\right)(y)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in R\\
\ell(R)\leq\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R\cap(3Q)^{c}}|f(z)|dz

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in 2R\\
\ell(R)\leq\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|R|}\int_{R\cap(3Q)^{c}}|f(z)|dz

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n}{\bf M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x).

We obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(y)^{\varepsilon}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\left({\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}(f\chi_{3Q})(y)+{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\left(f\chi_{(3Q)^{c}}\right)(y)\right)^{\varepsilon}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}(f\chi_{3Q})(y)^{\varepsilon}dy+\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\left(f\chi_{(3Q)^{c}}\right)(y)^{\varepsilon}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n}^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{\varepsilon}+2^{n}{\bf M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n}}{1-\varepsilon}{\bf M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{\varepsilon},

which yields (
2.4.7
). However, we can only conclude that

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho/3}^{\rm loc}\left[\left({\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right)^{\varepsilon}\right]\leq\frac{C_{n}}{1-\varepsilon}\left({\bf M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right)^{\varepsilon},

which is very closed to the
A_{1}^{\rm loc}
condition but not exactly.

Another application of the local reverse Hölder’s inequality is about the following reverse property to (
2.4.1
).

Proposition 2.4.10
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
,
1\leq p<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then there exist
\delta>0
and
C>0
depending only on
n
,
p
, and
[\omega]_{A_{p}^{\rm loc}}
such that for any measurable subset
S
of a cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(S)}{\omega(Q)}\leq C\left(\frac{|S|}{|Q|}\right)^{\delta}.

Proof.

Let
C
and
\gamma
be as in Theorem
2.4.2
. We have by Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(S)}{\omega(Q)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\omega(Q)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)\chi_{S}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega(Q)}\left(\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}|S|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\omega(Q)}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\gamma}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}|Q|^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}|S|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\omega(Q)}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)|Q|^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}|S|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle=C\left(\frac{|S|}{|Q|}\right)^{\delta},

where
\delta=\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}
and the second inequality is an application of Theorem
2.4.2
.
∎

2.5.
The
A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
Class

Recall the Jensen’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{X}|h|^{q}d\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\geq\exp\left(\int_{X}\log|h|d\mu\right)

(2.5.1)

for all
0<q<\infty
and measurable functions
h
on a probability space
(X,\mu)
. Putting
f=\omega^{-1}
with
\omega\in A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
q=\frac{1}{p-1}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q)}{|Q|}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{p-1}\geq\frac{\omega(Q)}{|Q|}\exp\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\log\omega(x)^{-1}dx\right),

where
Q
is any cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Thus we define that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=\sup_{\ell(Q)\leq\rho}\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right)\exp\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\log\omega(x)^{-1}dx\right),

and
A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
consists of those weights
\omega
with finite quantities
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
. As an immediate consequence of the above Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}},\quad 1\leq p<\infty,\quad 0<\rho<\infty,

and hence

\displaystyle\bigcup_{1\leq p<\infty}A_{p}^{\rm loc}\subseteq A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}.

Now we introduce some elementary properties of
A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
.

Proposition 2.5.1
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\omega\in A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
. Then

(1)

[\delta^{\lambda}(\omega)]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\frac{\rho}{\lambda}}^{\rm loc}}
, where
\delta^{\lambda}(\omega)(x)=\omega(\lambda x_{1},\ldots,\lambda x_{n})
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
\lambda>0
.

(2)

[\tau^{z}\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
, where
\tau^{z}(\omega)(x)=\omega(x-z)
,
x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(3)

[\lambda\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
, where
\lambda>0
.

(4)

[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\geq 1
.

(5)

The following formula

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell(Q)\leq\rho\\
\log|f|\in L^{1}(Q)\\
0<\int_{Q}|f|\omega dx<\infty\end{subarray}}\frac{\omega(Q)}{\displaystyle\int_{Q}|f(x)|\omega(x)dx}\exp\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\log|f(x)|dx\right)

holds.

(6)

The measure
\omega dx
satisfies the local doubling with respect to
\rho
. To be more specific, for all
\lambda>1
and cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, one has

\displaystyle\omega(Q)\leq 2^{\lambda^{n}}[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\lambda^{n}}\omega\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}Q\right).

Proof.

Properties (1), (2), and (3):
These can be argued similarly as in the proof of Proposition
2.1.1
for the first three properties.

Property (4):
This follows by the Jensen’s inequality (
2.5.1
) with the probability space
(Q,\frac{dx}{|Q|})
.

Property (5):
By taking
f=\omega^{-1}
, we obtain the
\leq
direction of the inequality. For the converse, (
2.5.1
) gives

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\log(|f(x)|\omega(x))dx\right)\leq\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}|f(x)|\omega(x)dx,

and it can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q)}{\displaystyle\int_{Q}|f(x)|\omega(x)dx}\exp\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\log|f(x)|dx\right)\leq\frac{\omega(Q)}{|Q|}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\log|\omega(x)|dx\right).

Taking supremum over all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, we obtain the other direction of the inequality.

Property (6):
We apply
Property (5)
to the cube
Q
and the function

\displaystyle f=c\chi_{\frac{1}{\lambda}Q}+\chi_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\frac{1}{\lambda}Q},

where
c>1
is chosen such that
c=(2[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}})^{\lambda^{n}}
. Then we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q)}{c\cdot\omega\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}Q\right)+\omega\left(Q\setminus\frac{1}{\lambda}Q\right)}\exp\left(\frac{\log c}{\lambda^{n}}\right)\leq[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}.

The estimate then follows by routine simplification.
∎

Lemma 2.5.2
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose that there are
0<\alpha,\beta<1
such that for all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and measurable sets
A\subseteq Q
,

\displaystyle|A|\leq\alpha|Q|\quad\text{entails}\quad\mu(A)\leq\beta\mu(Q).

Then
\mu
is a local doubling measure with respect to
\rho
.

Proof.

Given a cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and measurable set
S\subseteq Q
, let
A=Q\setminus S
. We have by assumption that

\displaystyle|Q|-|S|\leq\alpha|Q|\quad\text{entails}\quad\mu(Q)-\mu(S)\leq\beta\mu(Q),

which yields

\displaystyle|S|\geq(1-\alpha)|Q|\quad\text{entails}\quad\mu(S)\geq(1-\beta)\mu(Q).

Choose a
0<\lambda<1
such that
\lambda^{n}>1-\alpha
. Since
0<\lambda<1
, we have
S=\lambda Q\subseteq Q
. Then
|S|=\lambda^{n}|Q|>(1-\alpha)|Q|
entails

\displaystyle\mu(Q)\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta}\mu(\lambda Q).

Pick a
q\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\lambda^{q}<1/3
. Since
\ell(\lambda Q)=\lambda\ell(Q)<\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, iteration yields

\displaystyle\mu(Q)\leq\frac{1}{(1-\beta)^{q}}\mu(\lambda^{q}Q)\leq\frac{1}{(1-\beta)^{q}}\mu\left(\frac{1}{3}Q\right),

and the result follows.
∎

Further characterizations of
A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
are given as follows.

Theorem 2.5.3
.

Let
0<\rho<\infty
and
\omega
be a weight. Then
\omega\in A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
if and only if any one of the following conditions holds.

(1)

There exist
0<\alpha,\beta<1
such that for all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\left\{x\in Q:\omega(x)\leq\gamma\cdot{\rm Avg}_{Q}\omega\right\}\right|\leq\delta|Q|.

(2)

There exist
0<\alpha,\beta<1
such that for all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and measurable subsets
A
of
Q
, it holds that

\displaystyle|A|\leq\alpha|Q|\quad\text{entails}\quad\omega(A)\leq\beta\omega(Q).

(3)

There exist
0<C_{1},\varepsilon<\infty
such that for all cubes with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\varepsilon}dx\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx.

(4)

There exist
0<C_{2},\varepsilon_{0}<\infty
such that for all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and measurable subsets
A
of
Q
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(A)}{\omega(Q)}\leq C_{2}\left(\frac{|A|}{|Q|}\right)^{\varepsilon_{0}}.

(5)

There exist
0<\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}<1
such that for all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and measurable subsets
A
of
Q
, it holds that

\displaystyle\omega(A)<\alpha^{\prime}\omega(Q)\quad\text{entails}\quad|A|<\beta^{\prime}|Q|.

(6)

There exist
p,C_{3}<\infty
such that
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq C_{3}
. In other words,
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
for some
1\leq p<\infty
.

Proof.

\omega\in A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
entails (1):
By
Property (3)
of Proposition
2.5.1
, we have
[\lambda\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}=[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
for any
\lambda>0
. Fix a cube
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. By choosing

\displaystyle\lambda=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx\right),

one may assume that

\displaystyle\int_{Q}\log\omega(x)dx=0.

Then
{\rm Avg}_{Q}\leq[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
. Hence

\displaystyle\left|\left\{x\in Q:\omega(x)\leq\gamma\cdot{\rm Avg}_{Q}\omega\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\{x\in Q:\omega(x)\leq\gamma[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\{x\in Q:\log\left(1+\omega(x)^{-1}\right)\geq\log\left(1+\left(\gamma[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{-1}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\log\left(1+\left(\gamma[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{-1}\right)}\int_{Q}\log\frac{1+\omega(x)}{\omega(x)}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\log\left(1+\left(\gamma[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{-1}\right)}\int_{Q}\log(1+\omega(x))dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\log\left(1+\left(\gamma[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{-1}\right)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}|Q|}{\log\left(1+\left(\gamma[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\right)^{-1}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}|Q|,

where
\gamma=[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{-1}\left(e^{2[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}}-1\right)^{-1}
and
\delta=\frac{1}{2}
.

(1) entails (2):
Let
Q
be a fixed cube with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Assume that
A\subseteq Q
is measurable with
\omega(A)>\beta\omega(Q)
for some
\beta>0
to be determined later. By letting
S=Q\setminus A
, we have
\omega(S)<(1-\beta)\omega(Q)
. We write
S=S_{1}\cup S_{2}
, where

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\{x\in S:\omega(x)>\gamma\cdot{\rm Avg}_{Q}\omega\right\},

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\{x\in S:\omega(x)\leq\gamma\cdot{\rm Avg}_{Q}\omega\right\}.

The assumption in (1) entails
|S_{2}|\leq\gamma|Q|
. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle|S_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{\gamma\cdot{\rm Avg}_{Q}\omega}\int_{S}\omega(x)dx=\frac{|Q|}{\gamma}\frac{\omega(S)}{\omega(Q)}<\frac{1-\beta}{\gamma}|Q|.

Then

\displaystyle|S|\leq|S_{1}|+|S_{2}|<\frac{1-\beta}{\gamma}|Q|+\delta|Q|=\left(\delta+\frac{1-\beta}{\gamma}\right)|Q|.

Let
\alpha=\frac{1-\delta}{2}
and
\beta=1-\frac{(1-\delta)\gamma}{2}
. We obtain
|S|<(1-\alpha)|Q|
, then
|A|>\alpha|Q|
.

(2) entails (3):
Note that the Lebesgue measure is local doubling with respect to any
0<\rho^{\prime}<\infty
. By letting
\rho^{\prime}=3\rho
, then Theorem
2.4.2
finishes the proof with
D=3^{n}
and

\displaystyle\varepsilon=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log\beta}{\log 3^{n}-\log\alpha},\quad C_{1}=1+\frac{(3^{n}\alpha^{-1})^{\varepsilon}}{1-(3^{n}\alpha^{-1})^{\varepsilon}\beta}.

(3) entails (4):
We have by Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\int_{A}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{A}\omega(x)^{1+\varepsilon}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}|A|^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{1+\varepsilon}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}|Q|^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}|A|^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\omega(x)dx|Q|^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}|A|^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}},

which yields

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(A)}{\omega(Q)}\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{|A|}{|Q|}\right)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}.

We may now choose
\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}
and
C_{2}=C_{1}
.

(4) entails (5):
Pick an
0<\alpha^{\prime\prime}<1
such that
\beta^{\prime\prime}=C_{2}(\alpha^{\prime\prime})^{\varepsilon_{0}}<1
. Then

\displaystyle|A|\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}|Q|\quad\text{entails}\quad\omega(A)\leq\beta^{\prime\prime}\omega(Q)

(2.5.2)

for all cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
and measurable subsets
A
of
Q
. Replacing
A
by
Q\setminus A
, the entailment in (
2.5.2
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle|A|\geq(1-\alpha^{\prime\prime})|Q|\quad\text{entails}\quad\omega(A)\geq(1-\beta^{\prime\prime})\omega(Q).

(2.5.3)

In other words, for measurable sets
A\subseteq Q
, one has

\displaystyle\omega(A)<(1-\beta^{\prime\prime})\omega(Q)\quad\text{entails}\quad|A|<(1-\alpha^{\prime\prime})|Q|.

(2.5.4)

Now we let
\alpha^{\prime}=1-\beta^{\prime\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime}=1-\alpha^{\prime\prime}
.

(5) entails (6):
By letting
d\mu(x)=\omega(x)dx
, the assumption says that

\displaystyle\mu(A)<\alpha^{\prime}\mu(Q)\quad\text{entails}\quad|A|<\beta^{\prime}|B|.

A similar form of the equivalence between (
2.5.2
) and (
2.5.4
) yields

\displaystyle|A|\leq(1-\beta^{\prime})|B|\quad\text{entails}\quad\mu(A)\leq(1-\alpha^{\prime})\omega(Q),

which implies by Lemma
2.5.2
that
\mu
is local doubling with respect to
\rho
. Note that the assumption is also equivalent to

\displaystyle\mu(A)<\alpha^{\prime}\mu(Q)\quad\text{entails}\quad\int_{A}\omega(x)^{-1}d\mu(x)<\beta^{\prime}\int_{B}\omega(x)^{-1}d\mu(x),

which implies by Theorem
2.4.2
that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\mu(Q)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-1-\gamma}d\mu(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\leq\frac{C}{\mu(Q)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-1}d\mu(x)

for all cubes
Q
with
|Q|\leq\frac{\rho}{3}
. By letting
p=1+\frac{1}{\gamma}
, the above can be written as

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\omega(Q)}\int_{Q}\omega(x)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dx\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\leq\frac{C}{\omega(Q)}|Q|,

which yields the
A_{p;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}
condition of
\omega
with
[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho/3}^{\rm loc}}\leq C^{p}
. Using Theorem
2.3.3
, we may take

\displaystyle C_{3}=\left(C(n,p)\max\left(\frac{3^{np}}{\rho^{np}},1\right)C^{p}\right)^{3p+1}C^{p}.

(6) entails
\omega\in A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
:
Simply note that
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.
∎

Since
A_{p;\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}=A_{p;\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}
for any
0<\rho_{1},\rho_{2}<\infty
and
1\leq p<\infty
, the characterizations above show that
A_{\infty;\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}=A_{\infty;\rho_{2}}^{\rm loc}
. Denote
A_{\infty;1}^{\rm loc}
by
A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. Then we have

\displaystyle A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}=\bigcup_{1\leq p<\infty}A_{p}^{\rm loc}.

As before, the symbol
A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}
is used when we want to measure
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
with
\left[\cdot\right]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
. It is worth noting that Rychkov
[
RV
]
defined
A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}(\alpha)
to be the class that consisting of weights
\omega
such that

\displaystyle[\omega]_{A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}(\alpha)}=\sup_{|Q|\leq 1}\left(\sup_{F\subseteq Q,|F|\geq\alpha|Q|}\frac{\omega(Q)}{\omega(F)}\right)<\infty,\quad 0<\alpha<1.

As we have seen that (
2.5.2
) and (
2.5.3
) are equivalent, and they are also equivalent to say that
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, we see that
A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}(\alpha)=A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. Moreover, by Lemma
2.4.1
, (
2.5.3
), and Theorem
2.5.3
, the finiteness of
\left[\cdot\right]_{A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}(\alpha_{0})}
for a fixed
0<\alpha_{0}<1
implies
\left[\cdot\right]_{A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}(\alpha)}<\infty
for all
0<\alpha<1
.

Remark 2.5.4
.

Throughout the proof of Theorem
2.5.3
(1), one may choose that
\gamma=[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{-1}\left(e^{c[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}}-1\right)^{-1}
and
\delta=\frac{1}{c}
for arbitrary
c>1
. Since
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\geq 1
, the term
\gamma
decreases exponentially as
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
increases. As a direct consequence, the term
\beta=1-\frac{(1-\delta)\gamma}{2}
in (2) satisfies
\beta\geq\frac{1}{4}
for large
c>1
. After a routine simplification, the term
C_{1}
in (3) becomes

\displaystyle C_{1}=1+\frac{(3^{n}\alpha^{-1})^{\varepsilon}}{1-(3^{n}\alpha^{-1})^{\varepsilon}\beta}=1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}-\beta},

and
C_{1}
increases as
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
increases since the function
t\rightarrow\sqrt{t}-t
is decreasing on
(\frac{1}{4},1)
. Finally, we conclude that when
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
increases, the term
\varepsilon_{0}
decreases while
C_{2}=C_{1}
increases in (4).

3.
On the Capacities Associated with
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}

Let
0<\alpha<\infty
,
1<p<\infty
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We define the Bessel capacities by

\displaystyle\text{Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}:f\geq 0,~G_{\alpha}\ast f\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},

where

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left[\left(1+4\pi^{2}\left|\cdot\right|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right](x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

is the Bessel kernel,
\mathcal{F}^{-1}
is the inverse distributional Fourier transform on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. When
0<\alpha<n
, the Riesz capacities are defined by

\displaystyle\text{cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}:f\geq 0,~I_{\alpha}\ast f\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},

where
I_{\alpha}(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\left|\cdot\right|^{-\alpha})(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the Riesz kernel, and we have

\displaystyle I_{\alpha}(x)=C(n,\alpha)|x|^{\alpha-n},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

for some constant
C(n,\alpha)>0
depending only on
n
and
\alpha
. Meanwhile, the Bessel and Riesz kernels coincide locally at
x=0
, to wit:

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}(x)=C(n,\alpha)|x|^{\alpha-n}+o(|x|^{\alpha-n}),\quad|x|\rightarrow 0.

(3.0.1)

On the other hand, the behavior of
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
is given as

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}(x)=C(\alpha)|x|^{-\frac{n+1-\alpha}{2}}e^{-|x|}+o\left(|x|^{-\frac{n+1-\alpha}{2}}e^{-|x|}\right),\quad|x|\rightarrow\infty,

which yields

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}(x)=O\left(e^{-c|x|}\right),\quad|x|\rightarrow\infty

(3.0.2)

for any
0<c<1
(see
[
AH2
, Section 1.2.5]
).

Assume further that
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
. Then the Bessel capacities are of local Riesz type in the sense that

\displaystyle{\rm cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha,p){\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)

holds for arbitrary set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and

\displaystyle{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,R){\rm cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)

holds for arbitrary set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
with diameter at most
R>0
(see
[
AH2
, Proposition 5.1.4]
).

Now we investigate the general construction of capacities, from which we can construct the capacities associated with local Muckenhoupt weights.

3.1.
General Construction of Capacities

Here we present the construction of capacities in accord with Meyers’ theory
[
MN
]
. The assertions in this subsection are mainly taken from
[
TB
, Chapter 3]
, which their proofs will be omitted here, the readers may consult the chapter therein for details.

Let
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\nu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Suppose that
k
is a nonnegative measurable function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
. We say that
k
is a kernel provided that the function
k(\cdot,y)
is lower semicontinuous on
\mathbb{R}^{n}

\nu
-a.e.
y\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
and the function
k(x,\cdot)
is
\nu
-measurable on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. If
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the potential
k(\mu,\cdot)
of
\mu
is defined by

\displaystyle k(\mu,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(x,y)d\mu(x),\quad\nu\text{-a.e.}~y\in\mathbb{R}^{m}.

For general measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we write the canonical Jordan decomposition of
\mu
as
\mu=\mu^{+}-\mu^{-1}
. For
\nu
-a.e.
y\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we then define

\displaystyle k(\mu,y)=k(\mu^{+},y)-k(\mu^{-},y)

provided that either
k(\mu^{+},y)
or
k(\mu^{-},y)
is finite, and
k(\mu,y)=\infty
for otherwise. Let
f
be a nonnegative
\nu
-measurable function on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. The potential
k(\cdot,f\nu)
is then defined by

\displaystyle k(x,f\nu)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(x,y)f(y)d\nu(y),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

If
f=f^{+}-f^{-}
is an arbitrary
\nu
-measurable function on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we define

\displaystyle k(x,f\nu)=k(x,f^{+}\nu)-k(x,f^{-}\nu),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

provided that at least one of
k(x,f^{+}\nu)
,
k(x,f^{-}\nu)
is finite.

Let
1<p<\infty
. The
L^{p}
-capacity
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
of a set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
with respect to
k
and
\nu
is defined by

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}(E)=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{p}:f\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})^{+},~k(\cdot,f\nu)\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},

where
L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})^{+}
is the cone of nonnegative functions in
L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. Given a property
P(\cdot)
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we say that
P(\cdot)
holds
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
provided that the set

\displaystyle N=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:P(x)~\text{is false}\}

satisfies that
C_{k,\nu,p}(N)=0
. Suppose that
f\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. Then
k(x,f\nu)
is defined
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proposition 3.1.1
.

If
f\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and

\displaystyle E=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:k(x,|f|\nu)=\infty\},

then
C_{k,\nu,p}(E)=0
.

The dual definition of the capacity
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
is a consequence of the minimax theorem.

Proposition 3.1.2
.

If
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a Borel set, then

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}(E)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\sup\left\{\mu(E):\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(E),~\|k(\mu,\cdot)\|_{L_{\nu}^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq 1\right\}.

Assume further that
0<C_{k,\nu,p}(E)<\infty
. Then

\displaystyle C_{k,nu,p}(E)^{-p^{\prime}}=\inf\{\|k(\mu,\cdot)\|_{L_{\nu}^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{p^{\prime}}:\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(E),~\mu(E)=1\}.

(3.1.1)

The capacity
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
satisfies the outer regularity.

Proposition 3.1.3
.

For every
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}(E)=\inf\{C_{k,\nu,p}(G):G\supseteq E,~G~\text{open}\}.

The capacity
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
is subadditive.

Proposition 3.1.4
.

If
E_{N}
is a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
for
N=1,2,\ldots
, then

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\bigcup_{N=1}^{\infty}E_{N}\right)\leq\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(E_{N}).

The next two propositions show that
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
is continuous from the right on compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and continuous from the left on arbitrary subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proposition 3.1.5
.

If
K_{1}\supseteq K_{2}\supseteq\cdots
is a decreasing sequence of compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\bigcap_{N=1}^{\infty}K_{N}\right)=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(E_{N}).

Proposition 3.1.6
.

If
E_{1}\subseteq E_{2}\subseteq\cdots
is an increasing sequence of subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\bigcup_{N=1}^{\infty}E_{N}\right)=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(E_{N}).

By a theorem of Choquet (see
[
CG
]
), one can show that the capacity
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
is inner regular with respect to Borel subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proposition 3.1.7
.

If
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a Borel set, then

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}(E)=\sup\{C_{k,\nu,p}(K):K\subseteq E,~K~\text{compact}\}.

Assume that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The nonlinear potential
V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu}
of
\mu
is defined to be

\displaystyle V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu}(x)=k\left(x,k(\mu,\cdot)^{p^{\prime}-1}\nu\right),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Note that
V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu}
is lower semicontinuous by Fatou’s lemma. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(x,y)k(\mu,y)^{p^{\prime}-1}d\nu(y)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(x,y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(z,y)d\mu(z)\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}d\nu(y)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(x,y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(z,y)d\mu(z)\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}d\mu(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(x,y)d\mu(x)\right)^{p^{\prime}}d\mu(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(\mu,y)^{p^{\prime}}d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\|k(\mu,\cdot)\|_{L_{\nu}^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{p^{\prime}}.

Proposition 3.1.8
.

If
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is compact with
0<C_{k,\nu,p}(K)<\infty
, then there is a measure
\mu^{K}\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(K)
such that

\displaystyle V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu^{K}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1\quad C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere}~x\in K,

\displaystyle V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu^{K}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 1\quad\text{for every}~x\in{\rm supp}\left(\mu^{K}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mu^{K}(K)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(\mu,y)^{p^{\prime}}d\nu(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu^{K}}(x)d\mu(x)=C_{k,\nu,p}(K).

A consequence of the above proposition is the following.

Proposition 3.1.9
.

If
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is compact, then

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}(K)=\sup\left\{\mu(K):\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(K),~V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu}(x)\leq 1~\text{for every}~x\in{\rm supp}(\mu)\right\}.

Proposition
3.1.8
can be extended to arbitrary sets
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
if the kernel
k
satisfies certain extra conditions.

Proposition 3.1.10
.

Suppose that the function
k(\cdot,\varphi\nu)
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\lim\limits_{|x|\rightarrow\infty}k(x,\varphi\nu)=0
for every function
\varphi
in a dense subset of
L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. Let
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an arbitrary subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
0<C_{k,\nu,p}(E)<\infty
. Then there is a measure
\mu^{E}\in\mathcal{M}^{+}\left(\overline{E}\right)
such that

\displaystyle V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu^{E}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1\quad C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere}~x\in E,

\displaystyle V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu^{E}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 1\quad\text{for every}~x\in{\rm supp}\left(\mu^{E}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mu^{E}\left(\overline{E}\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(\mu,y)^{p^{\prime}}d\nu(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu^{E}}(x)d\mu(x)=C_{k,\nu,p}(E).

3.2.
Definitions of Weighted Capacities

We begin by introducing the capacities associated with general weights. Let
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
\omega
is a weight. We define the weighted Bessel capacities
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
by

\displaystyle B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(E)=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}^{p}:f\in L^{p}(\omega)^{+},~G_{\alpha}\ast f\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}.

While the weighted local Riesz capacities are defined to be

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}^{p}:f\in L^{p}(\omega)^{+},~I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n},

where
I_{\alpha,\rho}(\cdot)
is the local Riesz kernel defined by

\displaystyle I_{\alpha,\rho}(x)=|x|^{\alpha-n}\chi_{\{|x|<\rho\}},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Since the definition of
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
weights are framed in terms of cubes along with the norm
\left|\cdot\right|_{\infty}
, as it will be seen later that it is more convenient to work with the definition of
I_{\alpha,\rho}(\cdot)
that

\displaystyle I_{\alpha,\rho}(x)=|x|_{\infty}^{\alpha-n}\chi_{Q_{\rho}(x)},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

To show that the above weighted capacities can be instantiated by the general theory given in the previous subsection, we proceed in a similar way as in Adams
[
AD
]
. Let

\displaystyle k(x,y)=I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)\omega(y)^{-1},\quad x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

and
d\nu(y)=\omega(y)dy
. Then

\displaystyle k(x,f\nu)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(x,y)f(y)d\nu(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)f(y)dy=(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x).

As a result, we have

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}(E)=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E),\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}.

The corresponding nonlinear potential of a positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is given by

\displaystyle V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

\displaystyle=k\left(x,k(\mu,\cdot)^{p^{\prime}-1}\nu\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(x,y)k(\mu,y)^{p^{\prime}-1}d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)\omega(y)^{-1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)\omega(y)^{-1}d\mu(x)\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\omega(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)d\mu(x)\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dy

\displaystyle=\left(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\left((I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}\omega^{\prime}\right)\right)(x).

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}k(\mu,y)^{p^{\prime}}d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)\omega(y)^{-1}d\mu(x)\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)d\mu(x)\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(y)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy.

Exactly the same way, one can show that
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)=C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
by letting

\displaystyle k(x,y)=G_{\alpha}(x-y)\omega(y)^{-1},\quad d\nu(y)=\omega(y)dy.

Now we instantiate Proposition
3.1.10
to the capacities
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
. Note that
C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is dense in
L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=L^{p}(\omega^{-1})
. The term

\displaystyle\lim_{|x|\rightarrow\infty}k(x,\varphi\nu)=\lim_{|x|\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)\varphi(y)dy=0

clearly holds for every function
\varphi\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. The premise of Proposition
3.1.10
is then fulfilled hence the following holds.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

If
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an arbitrary set with
0<R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)<\infty
, then there is a measure
\mu^{E}\in\mathcal{M}^{+}\left(\overline{E}\right)
such that

\displaystyle V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu^{E}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1\quad R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere}~x\in E,

\displaystyle V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu^{E}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 1\quad\text{for every}~x\in{\rm supp}\left(\mu^{E}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mu^{E}\left(\overline{E}\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(y)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu^{E}}(x)d\mu(x)=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E).

A similar statement holds for
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
if we make use of (
3.0.2
). Indeed, we have

\displaystyle|k(x,\varphi\nu)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}G_{\alpha}(x-y)\varphi(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|}{2}}|\varphi(y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{|x|-M}{2}}\int_{|y|\leq M}|\varphi(y)|dy

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0

as
|x|\rightarrow\infty
, where
{\rm supp}(\varphi)\subseteq\{|y|\leq M\}
for some
M>0
and
\varphi\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

A variant of weighted local Riesz capacities
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is given as follows. Let
\chi
be the characteristic function for the open cube
Q_{1}(0)
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
(y,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, define the kernel
k
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
by

\displaystyle k(x,(y,t))=\begin{cases}t^{-(n-\alpha)}\chi\left(\frac{x-y}{t}\right),\quad\text{if}~0<t<\rho,\\
0,\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We also define a positive measure
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
by

\displaystyle d\nu(y,t)=\chi_{0<t<\rho}\cdot\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dt}{t}.

If
f
is a function on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, then

\displaystyle k(x,f\nu)=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\frac{f(y,t)}{t^{n-\alpha}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

If
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then

\displaystyle k(\mu,(y,t))=\chi_{0<t<\rho}\cdot t^{-(n-\alpha)}\mu(Q_{t}(y)),\quad(y,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}.

Then the capacities
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is defined to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\|f\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})}^{p}:f\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})^{+},~k(\cdot,f\nu)\geq 1~\text{on}~E\right\},

where
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an arbitrary set. The corresponding nonlinear potential
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
of a positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is then

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=V_{k,\nu,p}^{\mu}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}}k(x,(y,t))k(\mu,(y,t))^{p^{\prime}-1}d\nu(y,t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{\rho}t^{-(n-\alpha)}\chi\left(\frac{x-y}{t}\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}\mu(Q_{t}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dt}{t}.

To instantiate Proposition
3.1.10
for
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
, we need the following technical lemma.

Lemma 3.2.2
.

For any
\psi\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
, let
\varphi=t^{\frac{1}{p}}\psi(\omega^{\prime})^{-1}
,
t>0
. Denote by
D
the set of all such
\varphi
. If
\varphi\in D
, then the function defined by

\displaystyle k(x,\varphi\nu)=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\psi(y,t)dy\right)t^{\frac{1}{p}-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

belongs to
C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Moreover, the set
D
is dense in
L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
.

Proof.

Suppose that
{\rm supp}(\psi)\subseteq[-M,M]^{n+1}
for some
M>\rho
. Then

\displaystyle{\rm supp}(k(\cdot,\varphi\nu))\subseteq[-2M,2M]^{n}.

To see this, if
(y,t)\in{\rm supp}(\psi)
,
|x-y|_{\infty}<t
, and
0<t<\rho
, then

\displaystyle|x|_{\infty}\leq|x-y|_{\infty}+|y|_{\infty}<t+|(y,t)|_{\infty}<2M,

which yields
x\in[-2M,2M]^{n}
, as expected. To see that
k(\cdot,\varphi\nu)
is continuous, let
x_{N}\rightarrow x_{0}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For fixed
0<t<\rho
, let

\displaystyle F(x,t)=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\leq t}\psi(y,t)dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\chi_{|x-y|_{\infty}\leq t}\psi(y,t)dy,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Observe that for all
y
with
|x_{0}-y|_{\infty}\neq t
, one has

\displaystyle\chi_{|x_{N}-\cdot|_{\infty}<t}(y)\rightarrow\chi_{|x_{0}-\cdot|_{\infty}<t}(y).

Since
\{|x_{0}-\cdot|_{\infty}=t\}
has measure zero, the above pointwise convergence holds almost everywhere. We also have

\displaystyle\left|\chi_{|x_{N}-\cdot|_{\infty}<t}\psi(\cdot,t)\right|\leq M_{0}\cdot\chi_{[-M,M]^{n}},\quad M_{0}=\sup_{(y,t)}|\psi(y,t)|,

Using Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we have
F(x_{N})\rightarrow F(x_{0})
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle|F(x_{N},t)|\leq 2^{n}M_{0}t^{n},

and
t^{n}
is integrable with respect to
t^{\frac{1}{p}-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t}
, one can use Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem again to deduce that
k(x_{N},\varphi\nu)\rightarrow k(x_{0},\varphi\nu)
, which shows the continuity of
k(\cdot,\varphi)
.

Now we show for the density. Let
f\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
. Define
g=t^{-\frac{1}{p}}f\omega^{\prime}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|g(y)|^{p}\omega(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(y)|^{p}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}.

Now we choose
\psi_{N}\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\psi_{N}(y)-g(y)|^{p}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}\rightarrow 0.

Let
\varphi_{N}=t^{\frac{1}{p}}\psi_{N}(\omega^{\prime})^{-1}
. Then
\varphi_{N}\in D
and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\varphi_{N}(y)-f(y)|^{p}\omega(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\psi_{N}(y)-g(y)|^{p}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t},

which completes the proof of the density.
∎

Combining Proposition
3.1.10
and Lemma
3.2.2
, one obtains the following.

Proposition 3.2.3
.

If
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an arbitrary set with
0<\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)<\infty
, then there is a measure
\mu^{E}\in\mathcal{M}^{+}\left(\overline{E}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu^{E}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1\quad\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere}~x\in E,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu^{E}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 1\quad\text{for every}~x\in{\rm supp}\left(\mu^{E}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mu^{E}\left(\overline{E}\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu^{E}}(x)d\mu(x)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E).

We will prove that the capacities
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
are equivalent under the assumption that
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. If the stronger premise that
\omega\in A_{p}
is assumed, then the weighted Bessel capacities
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
are also equivalent with them. To prove these assertions, we need the Muckenhoupt-Wheeden type inequalities, which we will address in the next subsection.

3.3.
Muckenhoupt-Wheeden Type Inequalities

In the sequel, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
, and
0<\rho<\infty
, denote by

\displaystyle M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)=\sup_{0<r\leq\rho}\frac{1}{r^{n-\alpha}}\mu(Q_{r}(x)),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

where
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

We begin with the following “good-
\lambda
inequality”.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then there exists an
a=a_{n,\alpha}\geq 1
for which, for every
\eta>0
, there is an
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n,\alpha,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}},\eta)
such that
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
and the estimate

\displaystyle\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>a\lambda\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\eta\cdot\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>\lambda\right\}\right)+\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)>\varepsilon\lambda\right\}\right)

holds for every
\lambda>0
.

Proof.

Let
E_{\lambda}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>\lambda\}
for
\lambda>0
. Since
I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu
is lower semicontinuous, the set
E_{\lambda}
is open. Using Whitney’s theorem, we may decompose
E_{\lambda}
into closed, dyadic cubes
\{Q_{j}\}
with disjoint interiors such that for each cube
Q_{j}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\rm diam}(Q_{j})\leq{\rm dist}(Q_{j},E_{\lambda}^{c})\leq 4\cdot{\rm diam}(Q_{j})

(see
[
SE
, Page 16]
). We subdivide the cubes with diameter
>\frac{\rho}{8}
into dyadic subcubes with diameters
\leq\frac{\rho}{8}
but
>\frac{\rho}{16}
. Denote the modified decomposition by the same sequence
\{Q_{j}\}
.

Suppose that we can show that there is an
a\geq 1
such that for every
\eta>0
, there is an
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
satisfying

\displaystyle\omega\left(\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>a\lambda,~M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)\leq\varepsilon\lambda\right\}\right)\leq\eta\cdot\omega(Q)

(3.3.1)

for every
\lambda>0
and
Q\in\{Q_{j}\}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\omega\left(\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>a\lambda\right\}\right)\leq\eta\cdot\omega(Q)+\omega\left(\left\{x\in Q:M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)>\varepsilon\lambda\right\}\right),

and the lemma follows by summing over all such
Q
.

To show (
3.3.1
), note that the diameter of cubes
\{Q_{j}\}
in issue are
\leq\frac{\rho}{8}
. We measure the weight
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
by
\left[\cdot\right]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
. Given any
\eta>0
, we choose

\displaystyle\delta=\left(\frac{\eta}{\eta+C_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}}},

(3.3.2)

where the constants
C_{2}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
appear as in Theorem
2.5.3
(4) (see also Remark
3.3.3
for the behavior of
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
in the parameters of
\delta
). As a consequence, using the aforementioned theorem, if
Q\in\{Q_{j}\}
and
E
is a measurable subset of
Q
with
|E|\leq\delta|Q|
, then
\omega(E)\leq\eta\cdot\omega(Q)
. Thus, it suffices to prove that, for a given
\eta>0
, there is an
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>a\lambda,~M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)\leq\varepsilon\lambda\right\}\right|\leq\delta|Q|

(3.3.3)

for every
\lambda>0
and
Q\in\{Q_{j}\}
. Let
\eta>0
be arbitrary and
Q\in\{Q_{j}\}
be one of the undivided Whitney cubes. Let
P=Q_{8\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)}(c_{Q})
be the cube concentric to
Q
with side length
16\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)
,
d\mu_{1}(x)=\chi_{P}(x)d\mu(x)
, and
\mu_{2}=\mu-\mu_{1}
. For arbitrary
a
and
\varepsilon
, we define

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>a\lambda,~M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)\leq\varepsilon\lambda\right\},

\displaystyle M_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{1})(x)>\frac{a\lambda}{2},~M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)\leq\varepsilon\lambda\right\},

and

\displaystyle M_{2}=\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{2})(x)>\frac{a\lambda}{2},~M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)\leq\varepsilon\lambda\right\}.

In the sequel, it will be shown that there is a constant
C=C(n,\alpha)>0
such that

\displaystyle|M_{1}|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{a}\right)^{\frac{n}{n-\alpha}}|Q|

(3.3.4)

and, further, if
a\geq 4L^{n-\alpha}
, where
L
is an absolute constant, and if
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, then
M_{2}=\emptyset
. Fixing
a=\max\{4L^{n-\alpha},1\}
, we then find

\displaystyle|M|\leq|M_{1}|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{a}\right)^{\frac{n}{n-\alpha}}|Q|\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{n}{n-\alpha}}|Q|,

and this proves (
3.3.3
) if we choose

\displaystyle 0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{1}=\min\left\{\left(\frac{\delta}{C}\right)^{\frac{n-\alpha}{n}},1\right\}.

We now show that (
3.3.4
) holds. We may assume that there is an
x_{0}\in Q
such that
M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x_{0})\leq\varepsilon\lambda
. Then

\displaystyle|M_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{1})(x)>\frac{a\lambda}{2}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\{x\in Q:(I_{\alpha}\ast\mu_{1})(x)>\frac{a\lambda}{2}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha)\left(\frac{2}{a\lambda}\mu_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)^{\frac{n}{n-\alpha}},

where the last estimate is the weak Sobolev inequality for Riesz potentials (see
[
SE
, Page 120]
). If we let
\overline{P}=Q_{9\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)}(x_{0})
, then
P\subseteq\overline{P}
and hence

\displaystyle\mu_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\mu(P)\leq\mu\left(\overline{P}\right)\leq\left|\overline{P}\right|^{\frac{n-\alpha}{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x_{0})\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha)|Q|^{\frac{n-\alpha}{n}}\varepsilon\lambda,

which proves (
3.3.4
).

Suppose that
x_{1}\in E_{\lambda}^{c}
and
{\rm dist}(x_{1},Q)={\rm dist}(Q,E_{\lambda}^{c})
. Pick a
z\in Q
such that

\displaystyle{\rm dist}(x_{1},Q)\leq|x_{1}-z|\leq 5\cdot{\rm diam}(Q).

For any
x\in Q
and
y\notin P
, we have

\displaystyle 8\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)

\displaystyle<|y-c_{Q}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|y-x|_{\infty}+|x-c_{Q}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|y-x|_{\infty}+|x-c_{Q}|

\displaystyle\leq|y-x|_{\infty}+{\rm diam}(Q),

which yields
|y-x|_{\infty}>7\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)
. Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle|x-x_{1}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|x-z|_{\infty}+|z-x_{1}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|x-z|+|z-x_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq{\rm diam}(Q)+5\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)

\displaystyle=6\cdot{\rm diam}(Q),

which yields

\displaystyle|x-y|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}-|x-x_{1}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}-6\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)

\displaystyle>|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}-\frac{6}{7}|x-y|_{\infty}.

Denote by
L=\frac{13}{7}
. Then

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{2})(x)

\displaystyle\leq L^{n-\alpha}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu_{2}(y)}{|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq L^{n-\alpha}\left(\int_{|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu_{2}(y)}{|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}+\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho,|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}\geq\rho}\frac{d\mu_{2}(y)}{|x_{1}-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq L^{n-\alpha}\left((I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{2})(x_{1})+\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu_{2}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq L^{n-\alpha}\lambda+L^{n-\alpha}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x).

As a result, if
M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)\leq\varepsilon\lambda
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, then
(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{2})(x)\leq 2L^{n-\alpha}\lambda
, and hence
M_{2}=\emptyset
if
a\geq 4L^{n-\alpha}
.

We now consider one of the cubes
Q
obtained by subdividing a Whitney cube. Then
\frac{\rho}{16}<{\rm diam}(Q)\leq\frac{\rho}{8}
and
|M_{1}|\leq\delta|Q|
for
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{1}
. Furthermore, for any
x\in Q
and
y\notin P
, we have

\displaystyle|x-y|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq|y-c_{Q}|_{\infty}-|x-c_{Q}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle>8\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)-|x-c_{Q}|

\displaystyle\geq 8\cdot{\rm diam}(Q)-{\rm diam}(Q)

\displaystyle=7\cdot{\rm diam}(Q),

which yields

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{2})(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\frac{7}{16}\rho<|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{16}{7\rho}\right)^{n-\alpha}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}d\mu(y)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{16}{7}\right)^{n-\alpha}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x).

If
x\in M_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{2})(x)\leq\left(\frac{16}{7}\right)^{n-\alpha}\varepsilon\lambda.

This shows that
M_{2}=\emptyset
for
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{7}{16}\right)^{n-\alpha}a
. Therefore, if we fix
\varepsilon=\min\{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\}
, we obtain (
3.3.3
) and the proof is then finished.
∎

The Muckenhoupt-Wheenden type inequality reads as the following (see
[
MW
]
).

Proposition 3.3.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
0<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. For every positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the following estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

holds.

Proof.

Let
a
,
\eta
, and
\varepsilon
be as in Lemma
3.3.1
. Assume first that
\mu
has compact support. For any
R>0
, Lemma
3.3.1
yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>a\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\eta\int_{0}^{R}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{R}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)>\varepsilon\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda.

Note that

\displaystyle a^{-p}\int_{0}^{aR}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\eta\int_{0}^{aR}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle\qquad+\varepsilon^{-p}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon R}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda,

where both integrals in the right-sided of the last inequality are finite since both
I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu
and
M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu
have compact support. By choosing
\eta=\frac{a^{-p}}{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}a^{-p}\int_{0}^{aR}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-p}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon R}\lambda^{p-1}\omega\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda,

and the proposition follows by taking
R\rightarrow\infty
. If
{\rm supp}(\mu)
is not compact, we let
d\mu_{m}(x)=\chi_{Q_{m}(0)}(x)d\mu(x)
,
m=1,2,\cdots
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{m}\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}})\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu_{m}(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx.

The proof is now complete.
∎

Remark 3.3.3
.

As noted in Remark
2.5.4
, when
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
increases, the term
\varepsilon_{0}
decreases while
C_{2}=C_{1}
increases in Theorem
2.5.3
(4). A consequence of this is that the choice of
\delta
in (
3.3.2
) is decreasing since
0<\frac{\eta}{\eta+C_{2}}<1
. Then the constant
C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}})
in the proof of Proposition
3.3.2
, which is essentially
\varepsilon^{-p}
, where
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n,\alpha,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}},\eta)
appears as in Lemma
3.3.1
, increases when
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
increases.

The following corollary is an immediate consequence of Proposition
3.3.2
and Remark
3.3.3
by the simple observation that
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}\leq[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho_{0}}^{\rm loc}}
for all
0<\rho\leq\rho_{0}
.

Corollary 3.3.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
0<p<\infty
,
0<\rho_{0}<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. For every positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the following estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho_{0}}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

holds for every
0<\rho\leq\rho_{0}
.

For positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we can further show that
I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu
can be controlled by
M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu
in terms of integrals.

Corollary 3.3.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
0<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. For every positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the following estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

holds. As a result, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx.

Equivalently,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x).

Proof.

For every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}+\int_{\rho\leq|x-y|_{\infty}<2\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)+\frac{\mu(Q_{2\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}.

We are to show that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx,

(3.3.5)

then the corollary will follow from Proposition
3.3.2
and Remark
3.3.3
for the larger quantity that
[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}}\geq[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

We partition
\mathbb{R}^{n}
into closed cubes
\left\{\overline{Q}_{j}\right\}
such that each cube
\overline{Q}_{j}=\overline{Q}_{\rho/4}(x_{j})
has side length
\ell\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)=\frac{\rho}{2}
. Suppose that
x\in\overline{Q}_{j}
. Then

\displaystyle Q_{2\rho}(x)\subseteq\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)},

where
\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)}\in\left\{\overline{Q}_{l}\right\}
,
\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)}\subseteq 20\overline{Q}_{k^{\prime}}^{(j)}
for
k,k^{\prime}=1,...,m
, and
m
depends only on the dimension
n
. Since
\overline{Q}_{j}\subseteq\overline{Q}_{\rho/2}(x)\subseteq\overline{Q}_{2\rho}(x)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mu(Q_{2\rho}(x))^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}\int_{\overline{Q}_{j}}\mu(Q_{2\rho}(x))^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p)\sum_{j}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)}\right)^{p}\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p)\sum_{j}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)}\right)^{p}\omega\left(10\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\sum_{j}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)}\right)^{p}\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{k}^{(j)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\sum_{j}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)^{p}\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right).

The estimate (
3.3.5
) follows since
\overline{Q}_{j}\subseteq\overline{Q}_{\rho/2}(x)\subseteq Q_{\rho}(x)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\sum_{j}\int_{\overline{Q}_{j}}\left(\frac{\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\sum_{j}\int_{\overline{Q}_{j}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx.

The last assertion of this corollary follows by the fact that
M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)\leq(I_{\alpha}\ast\mu)(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
∎

Remark 3.3.6
.

In the proof of (
3.3.5
), it is clear that one can replace the constant
C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})
by
C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}})
for any
\rho^{\prime}\geq 20\rho
. Combining this fact with Remark
3.3.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(I_{\alpha,2k\rho}\ast\mu\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20k\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
. This fact will be used from time to time without further reference in the sequel.

To deal with the Bessel kernel
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
, we need to impose an extra condition on weights
\omega
, to wit:

\displaystyle\omega(tQ)\leq\exp\left(\frac{t}{\delta}\right)\omega(Q),\quad t\geq 1,\quad\ell(Q)=1,

(3.3.6)

where
\delta>1
is some constant independent of all
t\geq 1
and cubes
Q
with
\ell(Q)=1
.

Note that
A_{\infty}
weights satisfy (
3.3.6
) since the doubling condition of
\omega
entails
\omega(tQ)\leq Ct^{n}\omega(Q)
for any
t\geq 1
and cubes
Q
. On the other hand,
A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
weights do not necessarily satisfy (
3.3.6
), for instance,
\omega(\cdot)=e^{\left|\cdot\right|}
.

Proposition 3.3.7
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight that satisfying
(\ref{not too fast})
for some constant
\delta>1
. For every positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the following estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu\right)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,1}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

holds.

Proof.

Choose some
0<\varepsilon<1
such that
\frac{1}{\delta}<\varepsilon
. Recall the asymptotic behavior (
3.0.2
) of
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
at infinity that
G_{\alpha}(x)=O(e^{-\varepsilon|x|})
as
|x|\rightarrow\infty
. Then we have

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}\mu(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,\delta)(I_{\alpha,1}\ast\mu)(x)+C(n,\alpha,\delta)\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\geq 1}e^{-\varepsilon|x-y|_{\infty}}d\mu(y),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Denote by

\displaystyle I(x)=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\geq 1}e^{-\varepsilon|x-y|_{\infty}}d\mu(y),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(M_{\alpha,1}\mu)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx.

Partition
\mathbb{R}^{n}
into closed cubes
\left\{\overline{Q}_{j}\right\}
with
\ell\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)=\frac{1}{2}
. We have

\displaystyle I(x)^{p}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j}\int_{\overline{Q}_{j}}e^{-\varepsilon|x-y|}d\mu(y)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{j}\int_{Q_{j}}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}\left(x,\overline{Q}_{j}\right)}d\mu(y)\right)^{p},\quad d_{\infty}\left(x,\overline{Q}_{j}\right)=\inf\{|x-z|_{\infty}:z\in Q_{j}\}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}\left(x,\overline{Q}_{j}\right)}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,\delta)\sum_{j}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}\left(x,\overline{Q}_{j}\right)}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)^{p},

(3.3.7)

where (
3.3.7
) is due to Hölder’s inequality provided that

\displaystyle\sum_{j}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}\left(x,\overline{Q}_{j}\right)}\leq C(n,\delta)

(3.3.8)

uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. To show (
3.3.8
), note that
d_{\infty}\left(x,\overline{Q}_{j}\right)=d_{\infty}\left(0,\overline{R}_{j}\right)
, where
\left\{\overline{R}_{j}\right\}
is a partition of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
where each
\overline{R}_{j}
is the translate of
\overline{Q}_{j}
with
x
. On the other hand, for every partition
\left\{\overline{R}_{j}\right\}
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\overline{R}_{j}=\overline{Q}_{1/4}(c_{j})
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{j}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}\left(0,\overline{R}_{j}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k\leq|c_{j}|<k+1}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}\left(0,\overline{R}_{j}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k\leq|c_{j}|<k+1}e^{-\varepsilon(|c_{j}|_{\infty}-1)}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\delta)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}e^{-k\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\delta),

which yields (
3.3.8
).

Subsequently, using assumption (
3.3.6
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}\left(x,\overline{Q}_{j}\right)}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{-\varepsilon(|x-x_{j}|_{\infty}-1)}\omega(x)dx,\quad\overline{Q}_{j}=\overline{Q}_{1/4}(x_{j})

\displaystyle=C(\delta)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{k\leq|x-x_{j}|_{\infty}<k+1}e^{-\varepsilon|x-x_{j}|_{\infty}}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}e^{-\varepsilon k}\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{k+1}(x_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}e^{-\varepsilon k}e^{\frac{k+1}{\delta}}\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(\delta)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}e^{-(\varepsilon-\frac{1}{\delta})k}\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(\delta)\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)

since
\varepsilon-\frac{1}{\delta}>0
. Summarizing the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,\delta)\sum_{j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{-\varepsilon d_{\infty}(x,Q_{j})}\mu(Q_{j})^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,p,\delta)\sum_{j}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)^{p}\omega\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,p,\delta)\sum_{j}\int_{Q_{j}}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{j}\right)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,p,\delta)\sum_{j}\int_{Q_{j}}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{1/2}(x)\right)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,p,\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{1/2}(x)\right)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,p,\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,1}\mu(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx,

which completes the proof.
∎

3.4.
Wolff Type Inequalities

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The Wolff potential
\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\omega(Q_{t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Up to a constant
C(n,p)>0
depending only on
n
and
p
, we prefer to express the Wolff potential by

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)=C(n,p)\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}.

Recall the nonlinear potential
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
associated with
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
. We have the pointwise estimate of
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
in terms of
\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
and vice versa.

Proposition 3.4.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho/2}^{\mu}(x)\leq\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p)[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.4.1)

Proof.

Observe that
Q_{t}(y)\subseteq Q_{2t}(x)
for
|x-y|_{\infty}<t
. Then
\omega\in A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}
yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<2t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C(n)[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{2t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{2t}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}t^{\alpha}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}}\int_{0}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x).

Besides that, by writing
1=\omega^{\frac{1}{p}}\omega^{-\frac{1}{p}}
, Hölder’s inequality gives

\displaystyle 1\leq\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}dy\right)^{p-1}.

Since
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{2t}(y)
for
|x-y|_{\infty}<t
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}2t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}t^{\alpha}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\omega(Q_{t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho/2}^{\mu}(x),

and the estimate (
3.4.1
) follows.
∎

The nonlinear potential
V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
associated with
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is comparable to
\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
in terms of integrals with respect to
d\mu
. We start with the following technical lemma.

Lemma 3.4.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}=(n-\alpha)\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\mu(Q_{r}(x))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}+\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}},

(3.4.2)

and

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\geq\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}=(n-\alpha)\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\frac{\mu(Q_{r}(x))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}-\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}.

(3.4.3)

As a result, for any
0<\delta<\rho<\infty
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\delta\leq|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}=(n-\alpha)\int_{\delta}^{\rho}\frac{\mu(Q_{r}(x))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}+\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}-\frac{\mu(Q_{\delta}(x))}{\delta^{n-\alpha}}.

(3.4.4)

Proof.

We first prove for (
3.4.2
). Indeed, we have

\displaystyle(n-\alpha)\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\mu(Q_{r}(x))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}+\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=(n-\alpha)\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{1}{r^{n-\alpha}}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<r}d\mu(y)\frac{dr}{r}+\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=(n-\alpha)\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}}^{\rho}\frac{1}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}d\mu(y)+\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\left(\frac{1}{|x-y|_{\infty}}-\frac{1}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}\right)d\mu(y)+\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}.

Whereas for (
3.4.3
), we have

\displaystyle(n-\alpha)\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\frac{\mu(Q_{r}(x))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}-\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=(n-\alpha)\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{n-\alpha}}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<r}d\mu(y)\frac{dr}{r}-\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=(n-\alpha)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\max(|x-y|_{\infty},\rho)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}d\mu(y)-\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{\max(|x-y|_{\infty},\rho)^{n-\alpha}}d\mu(y)-\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\geq\rho}\frac{1}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}d\mu(y)+\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{1}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}d\mu(y)-\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\geq\rho}\frac{1}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}d\mu(y).

Finally, (
3.4.4
) follows by (
3.4.3
) by noting that

\displaystyle\int_{\delta\leq|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\geq\delta}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}-\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}\geq\rho}\frac{d\mu(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}.

The proof is now complete.
∎

Theorem 3.4.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight. For any positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p)V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.4.5)

Assume further that
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle C^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x),

(3.4.6)

where
C=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})>0
is a constant depending only on the indicated parameters.

Proof.

We note that the second estimate in (
3.4.6
) follows by (
3.4.5
) and Corollary
3.3.5
. Indeed, since
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, it follows that
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
and hence

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\rho}\mu(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x).

Now we prove (
3.4.5
). Let
f=(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}
. Using (
3.4.2
), one has

\displaystyle V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<2\rho}\frac{f(y)\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}dy

\displaystyle=(n-\alpha)\int_{0}^{2\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}f(y)\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\qquad+(2\rho)^{-(n-\alpha)}\int_{|x-y|<2\rho}f(y)\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle\geq(n-\alpha)\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}f(y)\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t}.

Further, if
|x-y|_{\infty}<t<\rho
, then
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{2t}(y)
, and hence

\displaystyle f(y)

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<2\rho}\frac{d\mu(z)}{|y-z|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}
