\displaystyle\geq\left(\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<2t}\frac{d\mu(z)}{|y-z|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{(2t)^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}.

Hence Hölder’s inequality gives

\displaystyle V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle\geq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))^{p^{\prime}-1}}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}-n}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))^{p^{\prime}-1}}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}-n}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}t^{-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x),

which yields (
3.4.5
).

It remains to prove the first estimate in (
3.4.6
). As before, since
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
for
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, Corollary
3.3.5
entails

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha;\rho/4}(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx.

On the other hand, for any
0<r\leq\frac{\rho}{4}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\geq\left(\int_{r}^{2r}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\geq\frac{(\log 2)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}}{2^{n-\alpha}}\frac{\mu(Q_{r}(x))}{r^{n-\alpha}},

it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha;\rho/4}(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mu(Q_{t}(x))^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\mu(Q_{t}(x))^{p^{\prime}-1}d\mu(y)\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\mu(Q_{2t}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}d\mu(y)\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)t^{-(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mu(Q_{2t}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(y)|}\int_{Q_{t}(y)}\omega^{\prime}(x)dxd\mu(y)\right)t^{n-(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mu(Q_{2t}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\left(\frac{\omega(Q_{t}(y))}{|Q_{t}(y)|}\right)^{1-p^{\prime}}d\mu(y)\right)t^{n-(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{2t}(y))}{\omega(Q_{t}(y))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}d\mu(y)\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{0}^{\frac{\rho}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{2t}(y))}{\omega(Q_{2t}(y))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}d\mu(y)\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C^{(3)}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x),

where the last inequality follows by the local doubling property of
\omega
with respect to
20\rho
, and the proof is now finished.
∎

A variant of Wolff potential is given as follows. Assume that
\omega
is a weight such that
\omega^{\prime}
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We define
W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
to be

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dt}{t},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Note that the term
W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
can also be expressed by

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)=C(n)\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dt}{t},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

The “homogeneous” version of
W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
has been introduced by
[
AD
, Theorem 3.2]
.

Theorem 3.4.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight such that
\omega^{\prime}
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p)V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Assume further that
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle C^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x),

where
C=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})>0
is a constant depending only on the indicated parameters.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C(n)\int_{0}^{\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\omega^{\prime}(y)t^{-\frac{n-\alpha p}{p-1}-n}\left(\int_{|x-z|_{\infty}<t}d\mu(z)\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C(n)\int_{0}^{\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\omega^{\prime}(y)t^{-(n-\alpha)}\left(\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<2t}t^{-(n-\alpha)}d\mu(z)\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\omega^{\prime}(y)\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}}^{\infty}t^{-(n-\alpha)}\frac{dt}{t}\left(\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<2\rho}\frac{d\mu(z)}{|y-z|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}dy

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\omega^{\prime}(y)I_{\alpha,\rho}(x-y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{\alpha,2\rho}(y-z)d\mu(z)\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}dy

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\left(\left(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\omega^{\prime}\right)(x)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x).

On the other hand, if
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
, then Corollary
3.3.5
entails

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,\frac{\rho}{2}}\mu(x)^{\frac{s}{s-1}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{0<r\leq\frac{\rho}{2}}\int_{r}^{2r}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}d\mu(y)\frac{dt}{t^{n-\alpha+1}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{\rho}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}d\mu(y)\frac{dt}{t}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\omega^{\prime}(x)\frac{dx}{t^{n}}\frac{dt}{t}d\mu(y)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dt}{t}d\mu(y)

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x).

Repeating the first part argument in the proof of Theorem
3.4.3
, one deduces that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C^{(3)}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;4\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho/2}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x),

the same reasoning also applies to the following

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x),

which completes the proof.
∎

Combining Proposition
3.4.1
, Theorems
3.4.3
and
3.4.4
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 3.4.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
be a weight. For any positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\approx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\approx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)\approx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x),

where the implicit constants depend only on
n
,
\alpha
,
p
, and
[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

3.5.
Weighted Capacities Associated with
A_{p;\rho}^{\rm loc}

In this subsection we show that the capacities
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}
are equivalent provided that
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. If the stronger condition
\omega\in A_{p}
is given, they are also equivalent to
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
.

We start by showing that the capacities
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
give rise to the same capacity regardless of the scaling
\rho
.

Proposition 3.5.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(E),

where
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an arbitrary set. As a result, it holds that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho_{1}}^{\omega}(\cdot)\approx R_{\alpha,p;\rho_{2}}^{\omega}(\cdot)

for all
0<\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}<\infty
, where the implicit constants depend only on
n
,
\alpha
,
p
,
[\omega]_{A_{p;c\rho_{1}}^{\rm loc}}
, and
c
is a suitable constant multiplied to
\frac{\rho_{2}}{\rho_{1}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then Corollary
3.3.5
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx.

We deduce that

\displaystyle V_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x),

and hence Proposition
3.1.2
entails

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(K)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(K)

for any compact set
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any open set
G\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the inner regularity of
R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
holds for open sets by Proposition
3.1.7
, it follows that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(G)

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(K):K\subseteq G,~K~\text{compact}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\sup\left\{R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(K):K\subseteq G,~K~\text{compact}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(G).

Subsequently, we use Proposition
3.1.3
, the outer regularity of capacities, to obtain

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(G):G\supseteq E,~G~\text{open}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\sup\left\{R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(G):G\supseteq E,~G~\text{open}\right\}

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})R_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(E).

The last assertion of this proposition simply follows by the simple observation that
I_{\alpha,\rho_{1}}(x)\leq I_{\alpha,\rho_{2}}(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and hence

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho_{2}}^{\omega}(E)\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho_{1}}^{\omega}(E),\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}

holds for any
0<\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}<\infty
.
∎

Theorem 3.5.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega^{\prime}
be a weight that satisfying
(\ref{not too fast})
. Then

\displaystyle C(n,\alpha,p,\delta)R_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(E)\leq B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha)R_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(E),

(3.5.1)

where
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an arbitrary set. Assume further that
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}
. Then

\displaystyle C(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty}})R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\leq B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha,\rho)R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E),

(3.5.2)

holds for all
0<\rho<\infty
and arbitrary sets
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proof.

The second estimate of (
3.5.1
) follows by the asymptotic behaviors (
3.0.1
) and (
3.0.2
) of
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
that
I_{\alpha,1}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha)G_{\alpha}(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

For the proof of the first estimate of (
3.5.1
), we appeal to Proposition
3.3.7
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,1}\mu(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx.

Using (
3.1.2
), one obtains

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(K)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\delta)B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(K)

for all compact sets
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. To extend the above estimate to general sets, one argues as in the proof of Proposition
3.5.1
, which yields the first estimate of (
3.5.1
).

As noted in the first paragraph, the second estimate of (
3.5.2
) follows by the fact that
I_{\alpha,\rho}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,\rho)G_{\alpha}(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For the first estimate of (
3.5.2
), first note that

\displaystyle B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(E)\geq C(n,\alpha,p,\delta)R_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(E),\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}

(3.5.3)

holds with
\delta
depending on
[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty}}
. To see this, the doubling property of
\omega^{\prime}
entails the existence of such a
\delta
in (
3.3.6
), which depends on the constant
[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty}}
. By repeating the proof of Proposition
3.3.7
, one sees that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,1}\mu(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx,

and hence (
3.5.3
) follows. Denote
C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty}})
by
C
at the moment. By repeating the proof of Proposition
3.3.5
, one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,1}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,1/2}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx,

and hence
R_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(E)\geq CR_{\alpha,p;1/2}^{\omega}(E)
. Then for any
0<\rho\leq 1
, choose
k=k(\rho)
such that
\frac{1}{2^{k}}\leq\rho
. Iteration gives
R_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(E)\geq C^{k}R_{\alpha,p;1/2^{k}}^{\omega}(E)\geq C^{k}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)
. We conclude that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(E)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E),\quad\rho>1,\\
C^{k(\rho)}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E),\quad\rho\leq 1.\end{cases}

We obtain the first estimate of (
3.5.2
) by combining the above with (
3.5.3
).
∎

Theorem 3.5.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})^{-1}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\leq\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)

holds for arbitrary set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proof.

Let
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Using Corollary
3.4.5
, we have

\displaystyle C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}V_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)d\mu(x).

The rest of the proof is similar to that of Proposition
3.5.1
.
∎

By combining (
3.5.2
) of Theorem
3.5.2
and the above theorem, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 3.5.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}
. Then

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\approx\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\approx B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot),

where the implicit constants depend only on
n
,
\alpha
,
p
,
\rho
, and
[\omega]_{A_{p}}
.

A result from Aikawa
[
AH
, Lemma 20]
states that the weighted Riesz capacity
R_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is nontrivial, i.e.,
R_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})>0
, if and only if
\omega\in A_{p}
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(x)}{(1+|x|)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}dx<\infty.

As suggested by (
3.3.6
), the weighted Bessel capacity
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is nontrivial provided that the weight
\omega^{\prime}
does not grow too fast, equivalently,
\omega
does not decay too fast. Therefore,
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is not equivalent to
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
for general
\omega\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
.

Theorem 3.5.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
, and
1<p<\infty
. The
A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
weight
\omega
defined by
\omega=e^{-3p\left|\cdot\right|}
satisfies that

\displaystyle B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=0.

Proof.

Fix a cube
Q_{N}(0)
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(Q_{N}(0))
. Note that the nonlinear potential of
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is given by
G_{\alpha}\ast\left((G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}\omega^{\prime}\right)
. In view of Proposition
3.1.2
, we assume that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(G_{\alpha,p}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\leq 1<\infty.

(3.5.4)

Then by viewing
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
as a decreasing radial function
G_{\alpha}(r)
,
r>0
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(G_{\alpha,p}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\geq\int_{|x|\geq N}\left(\int_{|y|\leq N}G_{\alpha}(x-y)d\mu(y)\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\geq\int_{|x|\geq N}\left(\int_{|y|\leq N}G_{\alpha}(2|x|)d\mu(y)\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\mu(Q_{N}(0))^{p^{\prime}}\int_{|x|\geq N}G_{\alpha}(2|x|)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx.

Using the asymptotic behavior (
3.0.2
) of
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geq N}G_{\alpha}(2|x|)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\geq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{|x|\geq N}\left(\frac{1}{(2|x|)^{\frac{n+1-\alpha}{2}}}e^{-2|x|}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{|x|\geq N}\frac{1}{|x|^{\frac{n+1-\alpha}{2}p^{\prime}}}e^{-2p^{\prime}|x|}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\infty

by noting that
\omega^{\prime}=e^{3p^{\prime}\left|\cdot\right|}
. Since the term (
3.5.4
) is finite, we have
\mu(Q_{N}(0))=0
, and hence Proposition
3.1.2
entails

\displaystyle B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(Q_{N}(0))=0.

Then the monotonicity (
3.1.6
) of
B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\cdot)
entails

\displaystyle B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}B_{\alpha,p}^{\omega}(Q_{N}(0))=0,

as expected.
∎

3.6.
Weighted Capacities of Cubes

In this subsection we compute the weighted capacities of cubes. Recall that

\displaystyle{\rm cap}_{\alpha,p}(Q_{r}(0))=r^{n-\alpha p}{\rm cap}_{\alpha,p}(Q_{r}(0)),\quad 0<r<\infty,\quad\alpha p<n,

\displaystyle{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(Q_{r}(0))\approx r^{n-\alpha p},\quad 0<r\leq 1,\quad\alpha p<n

(see
[
AH
, Propositions 5.1.2 and 5.1.4]
). The estimate of general capacities of cubes is also given by
[
KV
, Section 3.2]
, but their kernels are not applicable to
I_{\alpha,\rho}(\cdot)
.

Lemma 3.6.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;6\rho}^{\rm loc}})^{-1}\frac{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}{\rho^{\alpha p}}\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{\rho}(a))\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;6\rho}^{\rm loc}})\frac{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}{\rho^{\alpha p}}

holds for every
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proof.

We first prove the upper bound. Let
f=A\chi_{Q_{\rho}(a)}\omega^{\prime}
, where the constant
A>0
will be determined later. For any
x\in Q_{\rho}(a)
, it holds that

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)=A\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho,|y-a|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}\geq A\frac{\omega^{\prime}(E)}{\rho^{n-\alpha}},

where
E=Q_{\rho}(x)\cap Q_{\rho}(a)
. We claim that
Q_{\rho/2}\left(\frac{x+a}{2}\right)\subseteq E
. Indeed, for any
z\in Q_{\rho/2}\left(\frac{x+a}{2}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle|z-x|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left|z-\frac{x+a}{2}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\frac{x+a}{2}-x\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle<\frac{\rho}{2}+\frac{|x-a|_{\infty}}{2}

\displaystyle<\rho,

which yields
z\in Q_{\rho}(x)
. In a similar fashion, one shows that
z\in Q_{\rho}(a)
, the claim follows. Since
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{p^{\prime};2\rho}^{\rm loc}
, Proposition
2.2.4
gives

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(a))

\displaystyle\leq[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{p^{\prime};2\rho}^{\rm loc}}\left(\frac{|Q_{\rho}(a)|}{|E|}\right)^{p}\omega^{\prime}(E)

\displaystyle\leq[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{p^{\prime};2\rho}^{\rm loc}}\left(\frac{|Q_{\rho}(a)|}{|Q_{\rho/2}\left(\frac{x+a}{2}\right)|}\right)^{p}\omega^{\prime}(E)

\displaystyle=C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\omega^{\prime}(E).

Now we let
A=\rho^{n-\alpha}C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(a))^{-1}
, then
I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f\geq 1
on
Q_{\rho}(a)
. Further, Hölder’s inequality implies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=A^{p}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(a))

\displaystyle=\rho^{(n-\alpha)p}C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})^{p}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(a))^{-p}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(a))

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\rho^{n-\alpha p}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(a)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(a)}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)^{1-p}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\rho^{n-\alpha p}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(a)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(a)}\omega(x)dx\right)

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\rho^{n-\alpha p}\omega(Q_{\rho}(a)),

which yields the upper bound for
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{\rho}(a))
.

Now we address on the lower bound. Let
\varepsilon>0
,
f\geq 0
, and
I_{\alpha;\rho}\ast f\geq 1
on
Q_{\rho}(a)
with

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx<R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{\rho}(a))+\varepsilon.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))=\int_{Q_{\rho}(a)}\omega(x)dx\leq\int_{Q_{\rho}(a)}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx.

Using the formula (
3.4.2
), one easily deduces that

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)\leq C(n,\alpha)\rho^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Hence Theorem
2.3.7
entails

\displaystyle\rho^{-\alpha p}\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathcal{M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;6\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx,

which yields

\displaystyle\rho^{-\alpha p}\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;6\rho}^{\rm loc}})\left(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{\rho}(a))+\varepsilon\right).

The arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
gives the lower bound for
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{\rho}(a))
.
∎

Theorem 3.6.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
, and
0<\rho<\infty
. Suppose that
\omega
is a weight such that
\omega^{\prime}
satisfies the local doubling property
(\ref{local doubling property})
with respect to
8\rho
that

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}(Q)\leq D\cdot\omega^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{3}Q\right),\quad\ell(Q)\leq 8\rho

for some constant
D>0
. Then for any
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
0<r\leq\rho
, one has

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))\leq C(n,\alpha,p,D)\left(\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1-p}.

(3.6.1)

If
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, then

\displaystyle\left(\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1-p}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a)).

(3.6.2)

Proof.

Let
C=\frac{2^{n-\alpha}D^{3}}{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}
,
f=CA^{-1}g
, where

\displaystyle A=\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t},

and

\displaystyle g(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}r^{-(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}\omega^{\prime}(x),\quad|x-a|_{\infty}<r,\\
|x-a|_{\infty}^{-(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}\omega^{\prime}(x),\quad r\leq|x-a|_{\infty}<2\rho,\\
0,\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\geq\int_{\rho}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\geq\log 2\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(a))}{(2\rho)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\geq\frac{\log 2}{D}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2\rho}(a))}{(2\rho)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}.

(3.6.3)

Using formula (
3.4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}g(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a))}{r^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}+(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}+\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2\rho}(a))}{(2\rho)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}-\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a))}{r^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,D)A.

(3.6.4)

Suppose that
x\in Q_{\rho}(a)
. Then

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast g)(x)

\displaystyle=r^{-(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}\int_{|y-a|_{\infty}<r,|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}dy

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{r\leq|y-a|_{\infty}<2\rho,|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}|y-a|_{\infty}^{(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}}dy

\displaystyle\geq r^{-(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(x))}{(2r)^{n-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2^{-(n-\alpha)}\int_{r\leq|y-a|_{\infty}<2\rho}\frac{\chi_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|y-a|_{\infty}^{(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}}dy

\displaystyle=2^{-(n-\alpha)}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(a))}{r^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}2^{-(n-\alpha)}\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(t))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\qquad+2^{-(n-\alpha)}\left(\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2\rho}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(t))}{(2\rho)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}-\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(t))}{r^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}2^{-(n-\alpha)}\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(t))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t},

where we have used a variant form of formula (
3.4.4
) in the second equality. Now we claim that

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(x))\geq D^{-3}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a)),\quad r\leq t\leq 2\rho.

(3.6.5)

When
t\leq\frac{1}{2}\rho
, we have
Q_{t}(a)\subseteq Q_{\rho}(x)
. Suppose that
\frac{1}{2}\rho\leq t\leq 2\rho
. Let
z=\frac{3a+x}{4}
. Then it is routine to check that
Q_{\rho/4}(z)\subseteq Q_{t}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(x)
and
Q_{t}(a)\subseteq Q_{4\rho}(z)
. The local doubling property of
\omega^{\prime}
with respect to
8\rho
entails

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a)\cap Q_{\rho}(a))

\displaystyle\geq\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho/4}(z))

\displaystyle\geq\omega^{\prime}(Q_{4\rho/27}(z))

\displaystyle\geq D^{-1}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{4\rho/9}(z))

\displaystyle\geq D^{-2}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{4\rho/3}(z))

\displaystyle\geq D^{-3}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{4\rho}(z))

\displaystyle\geq D^{-3}\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a)),

which proves (
3.6.5
). Combining the above estimates with (
3.6.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast g)(x)\geq 2^{-(n-\alpha)}D^{-3}(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}=\frac{A}{C},

which yields

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)=\frac{C}{A}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast g)(x)\geq 1,\quad x\in Q_{r}(a).

Further, we have

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{p}}{A^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}g(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,D)\left(\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1-p},

which is (
3.6.1
).

It remains to show (
3.6.2
). Let
\varepsilon>0
be arbitrary and choose an
f\geq 0
such that
I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f\geq 1
on
Q_{r}(a)
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx<R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))+\varepsilon.

Let
E=\left\{x\in Q_{r}(a):(I_{\alpha,r}\ast f)(x)<\frac{1}{2}\right\}
. If
E\neq\emptyset
, then for some
x\in Q_{r}(a)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r\leq|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{f(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}dy

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{r\leq|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}.

(3.6.6)

Using formula (
3.4.4
) once again, we have

\displaystyle\int_{r\leq|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}dy

\displaystyle=(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}\int_{r}^{\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}+\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}-\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(x))}{r^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}.

If
t\geq r
, then
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{2t}(a)
, and hence
\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(x))\leq\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2t}(a))
. Combining this with (
3.6.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{r\leq|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}dy

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})\left(\int_{r}^{\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}+\int_{\rho}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}.

Combining the above with (
3.6.6
), it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})\left(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))+\varepsilon\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}},

and hence

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))\geq C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})^{-1}\left(\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1-p}.

If
E=\emptyset
, then
I_{\alpha,r}\ast(2f)\geq 1
on
Q_{r}(a)
, and hence

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;r}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(2f(x))^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq 2^{p}\left(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))+\varepsilon\right),

which implies that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))\geq 2^{-p}R_{\alpha,p;r}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a)).

(3.6.7)

On the other hand, Hölder’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q_{r}(a))}{|Q_{r}(a)|}\geq\left(\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a))}{|Q_{r}(a)|}\right)^{1-p},

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q_{r}(a))}{r^{\alpha p}}\geq\left(\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a))}{r^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\right)^{1-p}.

Since

\displaystyle\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\geq\int_{r}^{2r}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\geq C(n,\alpha,p)\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{r}(a))}{r^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}},

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(Q_{r}(a))}{r^{\alpha p}}\geq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\left(\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1-p}.

Combining (
3.6.7
) with Lemma
3.6.1
, one obtains

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))\geq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;6\rho}^{\rm loc}})^{-1}\left(\int_{r}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{t}(a))}{t^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1-p},

which yields (
3.6.2
).
∎

3.7.
Bounded Maximum Principle of Nonlinear Potential

The classical bounded maximum principle reads as

\displaystyle(g\ast\mu)(x)\leq C(n)\sup_{y\in{\rm supp}(\mu)}(g\ast\mu)(y),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

where
g
is a radially decreasing function and
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
(see
[
AH
, Theorem 3.6.2]
). To put it another way, if
g\ast\mu
is bounded on its support, then it is bounded everywhere. For the nonlinear potential
G_{\alpha}\ast(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}
associated with the unweighted Bessel capacities
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
, the bounded maximum principle is also valid (see
[
AH
, Theorem 2.6.3]
). However, the proof of
[
AH
, Theorem 2.6.3]
seems to be working only for the convolution kernels. It is unknown if the same principle holds for the weighted nonlinear potential like
I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\left((I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}\omega^{\prime}\right)
. Nevertheless, we can show that such a principle holds for the Wolff potentials.

Theorem 3.7.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. If
\omega
is a weight that satisfies

\displaystyle\omega(Q)\leq C\left(\frac{|Q|}{|E|}\right)^{\varepsilon}\omega(E)

(3.7.1)

for some constants
0<C,\varepsilon<\infty
, where
Q
is any cube with length
\ell(Q)\leq 4\rho
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq 2^{\frac{n\varepsilon-\alpha p}{p-1}}C^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\sup\left\{\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(z):z\in{\rm supp}(\mu)\right\},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

In particular, if
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, then one may take
C=[\omega]_{A_{p;4\rho}^{\rm loc}}
and
\varepsilon=p
.

Proof.

Let
x\notin{\rm supp}(\mu)
and
x_{0}\in K={\rm supp}(\mu)
be the point that minimizes the distance from
x
to
{\rm supp}(\mu)
with respect to the norm
\left|\cdot\right|_{\infty}
. If
Q_{t}(x)\cap K\neq\emptyset
, then
t>|x-x_{0}|_{\infty}
, which in turn implies that
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{2t}(x_{0})
. Consequently, (
3.7.1
) implies that

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{2t}(x_{0}))}{\omega(Q_{t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{n\varepsilon}{p-1}}C^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{2t}(x_{0}))}{\omega(Q_{2t}(x_{0}))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=2^{\frac{n\varepsilon-\alpha p}{p-1}}C^{\frac{1}{p-1}}W_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x_{0}),

and the bounded maximum principle holds. The last assertion of this lemma follows by Proposition
2.2.4
.
∎

If we consider the variant type of Wolff potential
W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
, then the restriction on weights
\omega
can be relaxed to the local integrability of
\omega^{\prime}
.

Theorem 3.7.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\mu
be a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose that
\omega
is a weight such that
\omega^{\prime}
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq 2^{\frac{(n-\alpha)p}{p-1}}\sup\left\{W_{\omega,2\rho}^{\mu}(y):y\in{\rm supp}(\mu)\right\},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
3.7.1
, we let
x\notin{\rm supp}(\mu)
and
x_{0}\in K={\rm supp}(\mu)
be the point that minimizes the distance from
x
to
{\rm supp}(\mu)
with respect to the norm
\left|\cdot\right|_{\infty}
. If
Q_{t}(x)\cap K\neq\emptyset
, then
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{2t}(x_{0})
. Consequently,

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2t}(x_{0}))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{2t}(x_{0})|}\int_{Q_{2t}(x_{0})}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=2^{\frac{(n-\alpha)p}{p-1}}W_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x_{0}),

which completes the proof.
∎

The bounded maximum principle holds for the nonlinear potential
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
which associated with
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
. Note that if one assume further that
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, then the following is a consequence of Proposition
3.4.1
and Theorem
3.7.1
with the bound depending also on
[\omega]_{A_{p;4\rho}^{\rm loc}}
.

Theorem 3.7.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq 3^{\frac{(n-\alpha)p}{p-1}}\sup\{\mathcal{V}_{\omega;3\rho}^{\mu}(y):y\in{\rm supp}(\mu)\},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
3.7.1
, we let
x\notin{\rm supp}(\mu)
and
x_{0}\in K={\rm supp}(\mu)
be the point that minimizes the distance from
x
to
{\rm supp}(\mu)
with respect to the norm
\left|\cdot\right|_{\infty}
. If
Q_{t}(y)\cap K\neq\emptyset
for
|x-y|_{\infty}<t
, say,
z\in Q_{t}(y)\cap K
, then
z\in Q_{2t}(x)\cap K
, which yields
|x-x_{0}|_{\infty}<2t
as
x_{0}
is the minimizer of the distance. As a consequence, we have
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{3t}(x_{0})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x_{0}-y|_{\infty}<3t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x_{0}-y|_{\infty}<3t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{3t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=3^{\frac{(n-\alpha)p}{p-1}}\mathcal{V}_{\omega;3\rho}(x_{0}),

and the bounded maximum principle follows.
∎

3.8.
Weak Type Boundedness of Nonlinear Potential

In proving the boundedness of local maximal function on the spaces of Choquet integrals, we need the weak type estimate of nonlinear potential in the form that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{V}_{1;1}^{\mu}(x)\right\}\right)\leq\frac{C}{t^{p-1}}\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad 0<t<\infty

(see
[
OP2
]
). The above estimate also appears in the theory of thinness of sets (see
[
AH
, Section 6.3]
). The weighted analogue of such a weak type estimate reads as the following.

Theorem 3.8.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)>t\right\}\right)\leq C(n)\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{t^{p-1}},\quad 0<t<\infty.

Proof.

Let
\gamma
be the measure associated with a compact subset
K
of

\displaystyle\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)>t\right\}

(3.8.1)

such that
\gamma(K)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(K)
and
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\gamma}(x)\leq 1
for all
x\in{\rm supp}(\gamma)
as in Proposition
3.2.3
. Let

\displaystyle{\bf{M}}_{\gamma}\mu(x)=\sup_{t>0}\dfrac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\gamma(Q_{t}(x))},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

For all
x\in{\rm supp}(\gamma)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<t}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{\gamma(Q_{t}(y))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{\gamma(Q_{t}(y))}{t^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega(y)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}{t^{n-\alpha}}dy\right)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq{\bf{M}}_{\gamma}\mu(x)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\gamma}(x)

\displaystyle\leq{\bf{M}}_{\gamma}\mu(x)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

As a result, we have

\displaystyle\text{supp}(\gamma)\subseteq\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf{M}}_{\gamma}\mu(x)>t^{p-1}\right\}.

Note that

\displaystyle{\bf{M}}_{\gamma}\mu(x)=\sup_{t>0}\dfrac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\gamma(Q_{t}(x))}=\sup_{t>0}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{t-1/N}(x)\right)}{\gamma\left(\overline{Q}_{t-1/N}(x)\right)}\leq\sup_{t>0}\dfrac{\mu\left(\overline{Q}_{t}(x)\right)}{\gamma\left(\overline{Q}_{t}(x)\right)},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

By Besicovitch covering theorem (see
[
DE
, Theorem 18.1]
), there are collections of closed cubes
A_{i}=\left\{\overline{Q}_{n_{i}}\right\}
,
i=1,...,C(n)
such that
A_{i}
is disjoint and

\displaystyle\text{supp}(\gamma)\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{C(n)}\bigcup_{\overline{Q}\in A_{i}}\overline{Q},\quad\frac{\mu\left(\overline{Q}\right)}{\gamma\left(\overline{Q}\right)}>t^{p-1},\quad\overline{Q}\in A_{i}.

As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(K)=\gamma(K)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{C(n)}\sum_{\overline{Q}\in A_{i}}\gamma\left(\overline{Q}\right)\leq\frac{1}{t^{p-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{C(n)}\sum_{\overline{Q}\in A_{i}}\mu\left(\overline{Q}\right)\leq\frac{C(n)}{t^{p-1}}\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

Since
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
is lower semicontinuous, the set in (
3.8.1
) is open. The result then follows by Proposition (
3.1.7
), the inner regularity of
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
.
∎

Using Proposition
3.4.1
that
\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\mathcal{V}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the first assertion of the following corollary is an immediate consequence of Theorem
3.8.1
. While the second assertion follows by Theorem
3.5.3
.

Corollary 3.8.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;2\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)>t\})\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{t^{p-1}},\quad 0<t<\infty.

Assume further that
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)>t\})\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;40\rho}^{\rm loc}})\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{t^{p-1}},\quad 0<t<\infty.

The weak type estimate for the variant Wolff potential
W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
is given as follows.

Theorem 3.8.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)>t\right\}\right)\leq C(n)\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{t^{p-1}},\quad 0<t<\infty.

Proof.

The proof is almost resemble that of Theorem
3.8.1
. Simply note that

\displaystyle W_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\gamma(Q_{t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{\gamma(Q_{t}(x))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq{\bf{M}}_{\gamma}\mu(x)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}W_{\omega;\rho}^{\gamma}(x).

The rest is identical to the argument therein.
∎

It is worth noting that the weak type estimate for the unweighted case is not valid for the nonlinear potential
G_{\alpha}\ast(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}
for
1<p\leq 2-\frac{\alpha}{n}
. We claim that in this case when
\mu=\delta_{0}
is the Dirac mass at the origin, then
G_{\alpha}\ast(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}
is identical infinity. Indeed, using the asymptotic behavior (
3.0.1
) of
G_{\alpha}(\cdot)
at zero, for any fixed
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, one obtains

\displaystyle\left(G_{\alpha}\ast(G_{\alpha}\ast\mu)^{p^{\prime}-1}\right)(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}G_{\alpha}(x_{0}-y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}G_{\alpha}(y-z)d\mu(z)\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}G_{\alpha}(x_{0}-y)G_{\alpha}(y)^{p^{\prime}-1}dy

\displaystyle\geq\int_{|y|<1}G_{\alpha}(x_{0}-y)G_{\alpha}(y)^{p^{\prime}-1}dy

\displaystyle\geq C(n,\alpha,p,x_{0})\int_{|y|<|x_{0}|+1}\frac{1}{|x_{0}-y|^{n-\alpha}}\frac{1}{|y|^{(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}}dy

\displaystyle\geq C(n,\alpha,p,x_{0})\int_{|y|<|x_{0}|+1}\frac{1}{|y|^{(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)}}dy

\displaystyle=\infty

since
1<p\leq 2-\frac{\alpha}{n}
entails
(n-\alpha)(p^{\prime}-1)\geq n
.

3.9.
Capacitary Strong Type Inequality

Given a positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it is known that the trace class inequalities of the form that

\displaystyle\|G_{\alpha}\ast f\|_{L^{p}(\mu)}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mu(K)\leq C^{\prime}{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(K),\quad K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}~\text{compact},

where the constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
are comparable to each other (see
[
AH
, Theorem 7.2.1]
and
[
MV2
]
). The proof therein uses the capacitary strong type inequality that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(G_{\alpha}\ast f)(x)>t\})dt^{p}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}dx,

where
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
f\geq 0
. The weighted analogue of the capacitary strong type inequality reads as the following.

Theorem 3.9.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. For any
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi)(x)>t\})dt^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx.

Proof.

Note that the
>t
in the left-sided of the above estimate can be replaced by
\geq t
and vice versa. Assume first that
\varphi\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
\varphi\geq 0
. Let

\displaystyle E_{t}=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi)(x)\geq t\right\},\quad 0<t<\infty.

Note that
E_{t}
is compact. Hence
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E_{t})<\infty
by Theorem
3.6.2
. Further, since
t\rightarrow R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E_{t})
is nonincreasing, it will be clear in the sequel that we may assume that
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E_{t})>0
, hence Proposition
3.2.1
is applicable. Denote by

\displaystyle J=\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E_{t})dt^{p}<\infty.

Let
\mu_{t}
be the measure associated with
E_{t}
as in Proposition
3.2.1
with respect to
R_{\alpha,p;32\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle V_{\omega;32\rho}^{\mu_{t}}(x)\leq 1\quad x\in E_{t},

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=R_{\alpha,p;32\rho}^{\omega}(E_{t}).

Using Theorems
3.4.4
and
3.7.2
, we have

\displaystyle W_{\omega;8\rho}^{\mu_{t}}(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.9.1)

We have

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi)(x)d\mu_{t}(x)t^{p-2}dt

\displaystyle=p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{t})(y)t^{p-2}dt\varphi(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq p\|\varphi\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}L^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}},

where

\displaystyle L=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu_{t})(y)t^{p-2}dt\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy.

To conclude the proof, we will show that
L\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty,\rho}^{\rm loc}})J
. Assume at the moment that
p\geq 2
. Let

\displaystyle\lambda_{u}(E)=\int_{u}^{\infty}t^{p-2}\mu_{t}(E)dt,\quad 0\leq u<\infty.

We have

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<\rho}\frac{1}{|y-z|_{\infty}^{n-\alpha}}d\mu_{t}(z)t^{p-2}dt\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<\rho}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}}^{2|y-z|_{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}d\mu_{t}(z)t^{p-2}dt\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\rho}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<r}d\mu_{t}(z)\frac{1}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}t^{p-2}dt\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{t}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}t^{p-2}dt\frac{dr}{r}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{2\rho}\frac{\lambda_{0}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\lambda_{0})(y)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}M_{\alpha,2\rho}\lambda_{0}(y)^{s^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{0<t\leq 2\rho}\int_{t}^{2t}\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dr}{r}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dr}{r}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy,

where we have used the formula (
3.4.2
) in the first inequality. On the other hand, integration by parts gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}(Q_{r}(y))^{p^{\prime}}=p^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda_{u}(Q_{r}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))u^{p-2}du.

Express
\mu_{u}(B_{r}(y))=\mu_{u}(B_{r}(y))^{(2-p^{\prime})p^{\prime}}\cdot\mu_{u}(B_{r}(y))^{(p^{\prime}-1)^{2}}
. Using Hölder’s inequality with respect to the exponents that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2-p^{\prime})^{-1}}+\frac{1}{(p^{\prime}-1)^{-1}}=1,

we obtain
L\leq C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})L_{1}^{2-p^{\prime}}L_{2}^{p^{\prime}-1}
, where

\displaystyle L_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}u^{p-1}du\frac{dr}{r}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy,

and

\displaystyle L_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{(\alpha-n)p^{\prime}}\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\lambda_{u}(Q_{r}(y))du\frac{dr}{r}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy.

We have

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\left(\frac{\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{Q_{r}(y)}d\mu_{u}(z)\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{r^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dr}{r}u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<r}\left(\frac{\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{r^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dr}{r}d\mu_{u}(z)u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<r}\left(\frac{\mu_{u}(Q_{2r}(z))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{r^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dr}{r}d\mu_{u}(z)u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\left(\frac{\mu_{u}(Q_{2r}(z))}{r^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{r^{n}}\int_{Q_{r}(z)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dr}{r}d\mu_{u}(z)u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;8\rho}^{\mu_{u}}(z)d\mu_{u}(z)u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\infty}\mu_{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n})u^{p-1}du

(3.9.2)

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;32\rho}^{\omega}(E_{u})u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E_{u})u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p)J,

where we have used (
3.9.1
) in (
3.9.2
). Subsequently, we have

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{(\alpha-n)p^{\prime}}\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\int_{u}^{\infty}t^{p-2}\mu_{t}(Q_{r}(y))dtdu\frac{dr}{r}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{u}^{\infty}t^{p-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}r^{(\alpha-n)p^{\prime}}\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\int_{Q_{r}(y)}d\mu_{t}(z)\frac{dr}{r}\omega^{\prime}(y)dydtdu

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{u}^{\infty}t^{p-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<r}\left(\frac{\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{r^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dr}{r}d\mu_{t}(z)dtdu

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{u}^{\infty}t^{p-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{|y-z|_{\infty}<r}\left(\frac{\mu_{u}(Q_{2r}(z))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{r^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dr}{r}d\mu_{t}(z)dtdu

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{u}^{\infty}t^{p-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;8\rho}^{\mu_{u}}(z)d\mu_{t}(z)dtdu

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{u}^{\infty}t^{p-2}\mu_{t}(\mathbb{R}^{n})dtdu

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)J.

Now we consider the case where
1<p<2
. We write

\displaystyle\lambda^{u}(E)=\int_{0}^{u}t^{p-2}\mu_{t}(E)dt,\quad 0<u\leq\infty,

and integration by parts gives

\displaystyle\lambda^{\infty}(Q_{r}(y))^{p^{\prime}}=p^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{u}(Q_{r}(y))^{p^{\prime}-1}\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))u^{p-2}du.

Repeating the estimates of
L
in the first part, we obtain

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{2\rho}\frac{\lambda^{\infty}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\frac{dr}{r}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,2\rho}\ast\lambda^{\infty})(y)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{\lambda^{\infty}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{dr}{r}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\lambda^{u}\left(Q_{(\cdot)}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}-1}(\sigma_{u})}^{p^{\prime}-1}u^{p-2}du,

where

\displaystyle d\sigma_{u}(y,r)=\chi_{0<r<4\rho}\cdot r^{(\alpha-n)p^{\prime}}\mu_{u}(Q_{r}(y))\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dr}{r}.

Note that

\displaystyle\left\|\lambda^{u}\left(Q_{(\cdot)}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}-1}(\sigma_{u})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{u}t^{p-2}\left\|\mu_{t}\left(Q_{(\cdot)}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}-1}(\sigma_{u})}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{u}t^{p-2}\left(\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{\mu_{t}(Q_{r}(y))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{Q_{r}(y)}d\mu_{u}(z)\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{r^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dr}{r}\right)^{p-1}dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{u}t^{p-2}\left(\int_{0}^{4\rho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{\mu_{t}(Q_{2r}(z))}{r^{n-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{Q_{r}(y)}d\mu_{u}(z)\frac{\omega^{\prime}(y)}{r^{n-\alpha}}dy\frac{dr}{r}\right)^{p-1}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{u}t^{p-2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{0}^{4\rho}\left(\frac{\mu_{t}(Q_{2r}(z))}{r^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{r^{n}}\int_{Q_{r}(z)}\omega^{\prime}(y)dy\frac{dr}{r}d\mu_{u}(z)\right)^{p-1}dt

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\int_{0}^{u}t^{p-2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}W_{\omega;8\rho}^{\mu_{t}}(z)d\mu_{u}(z)\right)^{p-1}dt

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p)\mu_{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{p-1}u^{p-1},

where (
3.9.1
) is used in the last inequality. We conclude that

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{0}^{\infty}\mu_{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n})u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;32\rho}^{\omega}(E_{u})u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E_{u})u^{p-1}du

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})J,

and the result holds for the case where
\varphi\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
\varphi\geq 0
. For general
\varphi\in L^{p}(\omega)
with
\varphi\geq 0
, we approximate
\varphi
by a sequence
\{\varphi_{j}\}
of nonnegative functions in
C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Indeed, if
\varphi_{j}\rightarrow\varphi
in
L^{p}(\omega)
and
\varphi_{j}(x)\rightarrow\varphi(x)
a.e., then

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi)(x)\leq\liminf_{j\rightarrow\infty}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi_{j})(x)

everywhere
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and hence

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi)(x)>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\liminf_{j\rightarrow\infty}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi_{j})(x)>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\bigcap_{k\geq j}\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi_{j})(x)>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\bigcap_{k\geq j}\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi_{j})(x)>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\inf_{k\geq j}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\varphi_{j})(x)>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{j\rightarrow\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,1}\ast\varphi_{j})(x)>t\}),

where we have used Proposition
3.1.6
in the first equality. The proof is concluded by an application of Fatou’s lemma.
∎

4.
Some Applications

Having established the theory of capacities associated with
A_{p}^{\rm loc}
weights, we are ready to give some applications. We start with the rather easy application about the absolute continuity of
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
with respect to Lebesgue measure under certain constrains on the weights
\omega
.

4.1.
Absolute Continuity Associated with Weighted Capacities

We have the standard Sobolev embedding theorem that

\displaystyle|E|^{1-\frac{\alpha p}{n}}\leq C{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E),\quad E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad 1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha},\quad 0<\alpha<n,\quad n\in\mathbb{N}.

An immediate consequence of the above embedding is that
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(E)=0
entails
|E|=0
, which is known to be the absolute continuity of the Lebesgue measure with respect to
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
. The weighted analogue reads as the following.

Theorem 4.1.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfies
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)=0
. Then
|E|=0
. The same conclusion holds if
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
is replaced by
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
G
be an open set with
G\supseteq E
and
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(G)<\varepsilon
. Assume that
f\geq 0
satisfies
I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f\geq 1
on
G
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx<\varepsilon.

Note that the formula (
3.4.2
) gives

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,\rho)\mathcal{M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Using Theorem
2.3.7
, one has

\displaystyle\omega(G)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{G}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega]_{p;6\rho}^{\rm loc})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\rho,[\omega]_{p;6\rho}^{\rm loc})\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we deduce that
\omega(G)=0
and hence
|G|=0
as
\omega(x)>0
almost everywhere. Then
|E|=0
follows by the outer regularity of Lebesgue measure. The last assertion follows by Theorem
3.5.3
.
∎

To get rid of the assumption that
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
in the previous theorem, we have the following variant form of absolute continuity.

Theorem 4.1.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<2\rho\leq\rho^{\prime}<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight such that
{\bf M}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}\omega
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose that
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfies
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{{\bf M}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}\omega}(E)=0
. Then
|E|=0
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
4.1.1
. Note that

\displaystyle(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,\rho){\bf M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,\rho){\bf M}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}f(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Using (
2.3.9
) of Theorem
2.3.5
, one has

\displaystyle\omega(G)\leq\int_{G}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)^{p}\omega(x)dx\leq C(n,\alpha,p,\rho)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)^{p}{\bf M}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\rm loc}\omega(x)dx

The rest follows just as in the proof of Theorem
4.1.1
.
∎

4.2.
Choquet Integrals Associated with Weighted Capacities

Recall the general construction of capacities as in subsection 3.1 together with the notations therein. We define the Choquet integrals associated with
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
that

\displaystyle\int_{E}|f|dC_{k,\nu,p}=\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|f(x)|>t\})dt

for any function
f:E\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
and set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The Choquet integrals form the function spaces
L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})
defined by

\displaystyle L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})=\left\{f:E\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]:\|f\|_{L^{q}(C_{k,\nu,p})}<\infty\right\},

where
0<q<\infty
and

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}=\left(\int_{E}|f|^{q}dC_{k,\nu,p}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

We also define
L^{\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})
and
L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})
by

\displaystyle L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})

\displaystyle=\left\{f:E\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]:\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}<\infty\right\},

\displaystyle L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})

\displaystyle=\left\{f:E\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]:\|f\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}<\infty\right\},\quad 0<q<\infty,

where

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}

\displaystyle=\inf\{a>0:|f(x)|\leq a~C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere on}~E\},

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t>0}tC_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in E:|f(x)|>t\})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

When
E=\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the symbol
E
will be hidden, i.e.,
L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})
will be abbreviated as
L^{q}(C_{k,\nu,p})
, and similar convention applies to others. It is immediate that

\displaystyle L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})\hookrightarrow L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p}),\quad 0<q<\infty.

To see this, simply note that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t>0}tC_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in E:|f(x)|>t\})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t>0}\left(\int_{0}^{t^{q}}C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\{x\in E:|f(x)|>t\}\right)d\lambda\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t>0}\left(\int_{0}^{t^{q}}C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\{x\in E:|f(x)|^{q}>\lambda\}\right)d\lambda\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\{x\in E:|f(x)|^{q}>\lambda\}\right)d\lambda\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\|f\|_{L^{q}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}.

On the other hand, we associate a functional
\mathcal{C}(\cdot)
with
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\inf\{\|f\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{p}:f\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})^{+},~k(x,f\nu)\geq|\varphi(x)|^{\frac{1}{p}}~C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere}\},

where
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
is any function. Then it is trivial that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\chi_{E})=C_{k,\nu,p}(E)

for any set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proposition 4.2.1
.

\mathcal{C}(\cdot)
is subadditive.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be the set of all
\varphi
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\varphi\geq 0
and
k(x,f\nu)(x)\geq\varphi(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}

C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere for some function
f\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})^{+}
with
\|f\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq 1
. We claim that
\mathcal{H}
is convex. Suppose that
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
and
0<c<1
. Then there are
f_{1},f_{2}\in L_{+}^{s}(\nu)
such that
k(x,f_{j}\nu)(x)\geq\varphi_{j}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}

C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere and
\|f_{j}\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq 1
for
j=1,2
. It follows by the reverse Minkowski’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left(k\left(x,(cf_{1}^{p}+(1-c)f_{2}^{p})^{\frac{1}{p}}\nu\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(x,y)\left(cf_{1}(y)^{p}+(1-c)f_{2}(y)^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}d\nu(y)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(x,y)\left(cf_{1}(y)^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}d\nu(y)\right)^{p}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}k(x,y)\left((1-c)f_{2}(y)^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}d\nu(y)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=c\left(k(x,f_{1}\nu)\right)^{p}+(1-c)\left(k(x,f_{2}\nu)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\geq(c\varphi_{1}+(1-c)\varphi_{2})(x).

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\left\|(cf_{1}^{p}+(1-c)f_{2}^{p})^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\left(cf_{1}(y)^{p}+(1-c)f_{2}(y)^{p}\right)d\nu(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\left(c\|f_{1}\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{p}+(1-c)\|f_{2}\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq(c+(1-c))^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=1.

As a result, the convexity of
\mathcal{H}
is justified. It is now routine to check that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\varphi)=\inf\{c>0:|\varphi|\in c\mathcal{H}\},

which yields the subadditivity of
\mathcal{C}(\cdot)
.
∎

Theorem 4.2.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{C}(\varphi)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\varphi|dC_{k,\nu,p}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mathcal{C}(\varphi)

(4.2.1)

holds for any function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
. As a result, the space
L^{1}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})
is normable by Proposition
4.2.1
.

Proof.

First of all, Theorem
3.9.1
shows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\varphi(x)|>t\})dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)^{p}>t\})dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)>t\})dt^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{p;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\|f\|_{L^{p}(\omega)}^{p}

for any
f\in L^{p}(\omega)^{+}
with
(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast f)(x)\geq|\varphi(x)|^{\frac{1}{p}}

C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere. Taking infimum with respect to all such
f
, one obtains the second inequality in (
4.2.1
).

For the first inequality in (
4.2.1
), it suffices to prove for
|\varphi|^{p}
in place of
|\varphi|
. Then we may assume that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\varphi|^{p}dC_{k,\nu,p}<\infty.

For every
N=1,2,\ldots
, we have

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\varphi(x)|=\infty\})\leq\frac{1}{N^{p}}\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\varphi(x)|^{p}>t\})dt.

Then
C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\varphi(x)|=\infty\})=0
. Denote by
\overline{\mathcal{C}}(\varphi)=\mathcal{C}(|\varphi|^{p})
for functions
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
. Assume at the moment that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{C}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\varphi_{i}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\overline{C}(\varphi_{i}),

(4.2.2)

where
\varphi_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
with
\{\varphi_{i}\neq 0\}\cap\{\varphi_{j}\neq 0\}=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
. Then

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{C}}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\mathcal{C}}(\varphi\chi_{\{|\varphi|<\infty\}})+\overline{\mathcal{C}}\left(\chi_{\{|\varphi|=\infty\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathcal{C}}(\varphi\chi_{\{|\varphi|<\infty\}})

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathcal{C}}\left(\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varphi|\chi_{\{2^{i}\leq|\varphi|^{p}<2^{i+1}\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\left(\varphi\chi_{\{2^{i}\leq|\varphi|^{p}<2^{i+1}\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{i+1}C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{i}\leq|\varphi(x)|^{p}<2^{i+1}\right\}\right).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\varphi(x)|^{p}>t\})dt

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{2^{i-1}}^{2^{i}}C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\varphi(x)|^{p}>t\right\}\right)dt

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{i-1}C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{i}\leq|\varphi(x)|^{p}<2^{i+1}\right\}\right),

which yields the first estimate of (
4.2.1
). It remains to show for (
4.2.2
). Let
\varepsilon>0
and
f_{i}\in L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})^{+}
be such that
k(x,f_{i}\nu)\geq|\varphi_{i}(x)|

C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere and

\displaystyle\|f_{i}\|_{L_{\nu}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{p}<\overline{\mathcal{C}}(\varphi_{i})+\frac{\varepsilon}{2^{i}}.

Let
f=\sup_{i}f_{i}
. Then the “disjoint support” of
\varphi_{i}
gives

\displaystyle k(x,f\nu)\geq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}|\varphi_{i}(x)|

C_{k,\nu,p}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere. Subsequently, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}f(y)^{p}d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}f_{i}(y)^{p}d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left(\overline{\mathcal{C}}(\varphi_{i})+\frac{\varepsilon}{2^{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\overline{\mathcal{C}}(\varphi_{i})+\varepsilon.

The arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
justifies the validity of (
4.2.2
).
∎

We claim that the spaces
L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})
and
L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})
satisfy the
q
-convexity for
0<q<1
.

Proposition 4.2.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. For any
0<q\leq 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Proof.

We estimate that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}

(4.2.3)

\displaystyle=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}|\right)^{q}\right\|_{L^{1}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}|^{q}\right\|_{L^{1}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\||f_{k}|^{q}\right\|_{L^{1}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

(4.2.4)

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},

where we have used Proposition
3.1.6
in (
4.2.3
) and also Theorem
4.2.2
for the normability of
L^{1}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})
in (
4.2.4
).
∎

Lemma 4.2.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<q<\infty
, and
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an arbitrary set. For any sequence
\{f_{k}\}_{k=1}^{m}
of functions
f_{k}:E\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
,
k=1,2,\ldots
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}^{q}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}^{q}.

Proof.

Indeed, for any
t>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\left\{x\in E:\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|f_{k}(x)|>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}\{x\in E:|f_{k}(x)|>t\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m}C_{k,\nu,p}(\{x\in E:|f_{k}(x)|>t\})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1}{t^{q}}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}^{q},

and hence

\displaystyle\left\|\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|f_{j}|\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}^{q}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t>0}t^{q}C_{k,\nu,p}\left(\left\{x\in E:\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|f_{k}(x)|>\alpha\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q,\infty}(E,C_{k,\nu,p})}^{q},

as expected.
∎

Proposition 4.2.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega^{\prime}\in A_{\infty}^{\rm loc}
. For any
0<q\leq 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Proof.

For any
t>0
, Proposition
3.1.6
entails

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sum_{k}|f_{k}|>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sum_{k}|f_{k}|>t,\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}\right)+R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sup_{k}|f_{k}|>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}|>t,\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}\right)+R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq N}|f_{k}|>t\right\}\right)\right)

Let

\displaystyle I=\sup_{t>0}t^{q}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}|>t,\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}\right).

As an instantiation of Lemma
4.2.4
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\max_{1\leq k\leq N}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q},\quad N\in\mathbb{N}.

(4.2.5)

Then (
4.2.5
) gives

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}\leq I+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}.

(4.2.6)

Now we estimate
I
. Note that for any
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle t^{q}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}|>t,\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq t^{q-1}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}|>\lambda\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq t^{q-1}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\left\{\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}:\sum_{k=1}^{N}|f_{k}(x)|>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{q-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\left\{\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}:|f_{k}(x)|>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda,

(4.2.7)

where
C=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})
and we have used Theorem
4.2.1
for the normability of
L^{1}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})
in (
4.2.7
). As a consequence,

\displaystyle t^{q}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{\sum_{k}|f_{k}|>t,\sup_{k}|f_{k}|\leq t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})t^{q-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\{|f_{k}|\leq t\}:|f_{k}(x)|>\lambda\right\}\right)d\lambda

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})t^{q-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|f_{k}(x)|>\lambda\})d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|f_{k}(x)|>\lambda\})\frac{1}{\lambda^{1-q}}d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega^{\prime}]_{A_{\infty;2\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q,\infty}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}.

(4.2.8)

The proof is complete by combining (
4.2.6
) and (
4.2.8
).
∎

4.3.
Boundedness of Local Maximal Function with Choquet Integrals

Since the Bessel capacities
{\rm Cap}_{\alpha,p}(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1}(\cdot)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1}^{1}(\cdot)
are equivalent, the main results in
[
OP2
]
can be rephrased as

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{1}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p)\|f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1})},\quad q=\frac{n-\alpha p}{n},

(4.3.1)

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{1}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,q)\|f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1})},\quad q>\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}.

(4.3.2)

This subsection is devoted to the weighted analogue of (
4.3.1
) and (
4.3.2
).

For any
0<\rho<\infty
and positive measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we define

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\mu(x)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Q\\
\ell(Q)\leq\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(Q)}{|Q|},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Hence (
2.3.1
) is an instantiation of the above with
d\mu=|f|dx
.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
\mu
is a compactly supported positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;2\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\frac{\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(x)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}{\bf{M}}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}\mu(x)^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}.

Proof.

Let
0<\delta\leq\rho
be determined later. We write

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)
