\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\omega(Q_{t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}+\int_{\delta}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\omega(Q_{t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Using Proposition
2.2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(x)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\omega(y)dy\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;2\rho}^{\rm loc}}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)dy,\quad 0<t<\delta.

Then

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\delta}t^{\frac{\alpha p}{p-1}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;2\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\bf{M}}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}\mu(x)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(x)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{0}^{\delta}t^{\frac{\alpha p}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C(\alpha,p)[\omega]_{A_{1;2\rho}^{\rm loc}}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\bf{M}}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}\mu(x)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(x)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\delta^{\frac{\alpha p}{p-1}}.

(4.3.3)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(n)\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(x)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{1}{t^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{p-1}}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C(n,\alpha,p)\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{\rho}(x)|}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{1}{\delta^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{p-1}}}.

(4.3.4)

Note that we may assume that
\mu
is supported in
Q_{\rho}(x)
, this gives

\displaystyle{\bf{M}}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}\mu(x)\geq\frac{\mu(Q_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{n}|Q_{1}(0)|}=\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{\rho^{n}|Q_{1}(0)|}.

If we choose

\displaystyle\delta=\left(\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{|Q_{1}(0)|{\bf{M}}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}\mu(x)}\right)^{\frac{1}{n}},

then
0<\delta\leq\rho
and the proof is complete by routine simplification of (
4.3.3
) and (
4.3.4
).
∎

Lemma 4.3.2
.

Let
0<p<\infty
and
(X,\mu)
be a positive measure space. Suppose that
E
be a
\mu
-measurable subset of
X
with
\mu(E)<\infty
. For
0<q<p
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{E}|f|^{q}d\mu\leq\frac{p}{p-q}\mu(E)^{1-\frac{q}{p}}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}^{q}.

Proof.

For any
0<t<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\mu(E\cap\{x\in X:|f(x)|>t\})\leq\min\left(\mu(E),t^{-p}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}^{p}\right)

Then

\displaystyle\int_{E}|f|^{q}d\mu

\displaystyle=q\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{q-1}\mu(E\cap\{x\in X:|f(x)|>t\})dt

\displaystyle\leq q\int_{0}^{\mu(E)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}}t^{q-1}\mu(E)dt

\displaystyle\qquad+q\int_{\mu(E)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}}^{\infty}t^{q-1}t^{-p}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}^{p}dt

\displaystyle=\mu(E)\mu(E)^{-\frac{q}{p}}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}+\frac{q}{p-q}\mu(E)^{\frac{p-q}{p}}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}^{p-(p-q)}

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{p-q}\mu(E)^{1-\frac{q}{p}}\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(X,\mu)}^{q},

which yields the estimate.
∎

The next proposition shows that the Wolff potential
(\mathcal{W}_{\omega,\rho}^{\mu})^{\delta}
with admissible exponents
\delta
satisfies a condition very closed to
A_{1}^{\rm loc}
but not exactly.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
\mu
is a positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any
0<\delta<\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{2\rho}^{\rm loc}\left((\mathcal{W}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu})^{\delta}\right)(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;6\rho}^{\rm loc}},\delta)\mathcal{W}_{\omega;3\rho}^{\mu}(x)^{\delta}.

Proof.

Let
Q
be an arbitrary cube that containing
x
with
\ell(Q)\leq 2\rho
. Denote by
0<r=\frac{\ell(Q)}{2}\leq\rho
. We need to estimate that

\displaystyle I=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{\omega(Q_{t}(y))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\delta}dy\leq C(I_{1}+I_{2}),

where

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\left(\int_{0}^{r}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{\omega(Q_{t}(y))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\delta}dy,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\left(\int_{r}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(y))}{\omega(Q_{t}(y))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\delta}dy.

For the integral
I_{1}
, we may assume that
\mu
is supported in
Q_{2r}(x)
. By Lemma
4.3.1
, it suffices to estimate

\displaystyle\int_{Q}{\bf{M}}_{2r}^{\rm loc}\mu(y)^{\frac{\delta(n-\alpha p)}{n(p-1)}}dy.

Let
0<q=\frac{\delta(n-\alpha p)}{n(p-1)}<1
. We appeal to the estimate in Lemma
4.3.2
that

\displaystyle\int_{E}|F(y)|^{q}dy\leq C(n,q)|E|^{1-q}\|F\|_{L^{1,\infty}(E)}^{q}

for any measurable set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
|E|<\infty
and
F\in L^{1,\infty}(E)
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;r}^{\mu}(y)^{\delta}dy

\displaystyle\leq C\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\frac{\delta\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x)|}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{\delta}{p-1}}\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}{\bf{M}}_{2r}^{\rm loc}\mu(y)^{\frac{\delta(n-\alpha p)}{n(p-1)}}dy

\displaystyle\leq C\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\frac{\delta\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x)|}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{\delta}{p-1}}\frac{1}{|Q|}|Q|^{1-q}\left\|{\bf{M}}_{2r}^{\rm loc}\mu\right\|_{L^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-\frac{\delta(n-\alpha p)}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x)|}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{\delta}{p-1}}\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\frac{\delta\alpha p}{n(p-1)}+q}

(4.3.5)

\displaystyle=C\left(\frac{r^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{2r}(x))}{\omega(Q_{r}(x))}\right)^{\frac{\delta}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{2r}^{3r}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(x))}{\omega(Q_{t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\delta}

(4.3.6)

\displaystyle\leq CW_{\omega;3\rho}^{\mu}(x)^{\delta},

(4.3.7)

where
C=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;6\rho}^{\rm loc}})
and the details of the above estimates are given as follows. We have used the weak type
(1,1)
boundedness of
{\bf M}
in (
4.3.5
) that

\displaystyle{\bf M}\mu(z)=\sup_{r>0}\frac{\mu(Q_{r}(z))}{|Q_{r}(z)|},\quad z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

together with
{\bf M}_{2r}^{\rm loc}\mu(z)\leq{\bf M}\mu(z)
for all
r>0
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Further, we have applied Proposition
2.2.4
to
\omega\in A_{1;6\rho}^{\rm loc}
in (
4.3.6
).

For the integral
I_{2}
, observe that
Q_{t}(y)\subseteq Q_{2t}(x)
for
y\in Q_{r}(x)
and
t>r
. Using Proposition
2.2.4
for
\omega\in A_{1;6\rho}^{\rm loc}
again, one obtains

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;6\rho}^{\rm loc}},\delta)\left(\int_{r}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{2t}(x))}{\omega(Q_{2t}(x))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;6\rho}^{\rm loc}},\delta)\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)^{\delta}.

(4.3.8)

The proof is complete by combining (
4.3.7
) and (
4.3.8
).
∎

Lemma 4.3.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
\mu
is a compactly supported positive measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any
0<r\leq\rho
and
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}\right\|_{L^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p},\infty}(Q_{r}(x_{0}))}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})|Q_{r}(x_{0})|^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;4\rho}^{\mu}(x_{0}).

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle P_{1}=\left\|\int_{0}^{r}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(\cdot))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(\cdot)|}\int_{Q_{t}(\cdot)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right\|_{L^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p},\infty}(Q_{r}(x_{0}))}

and

\displaystyle P_{2}=\left\|\int_{r}^{4\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(\cdot))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(\cdot)|}\int_{Q_{t}(\cdot)}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right\|_{L^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p},\infty}(Q_{r}(x_{0}))}.

It suffices to estimate the terms
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
. To bound
P_{1}
, we may assume that
\mu
is supported in
Q_{2r}(x_{0})
since
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{2r}(x_{0})
for
x\in Q_{r}(x_{0})
and
0<t<r
. Using Lemma
4.3.1
, the weak type
(1,1)
boundedness of
{\bf{M}}
, and Proposition
2.2.4
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q_{2r}(x_{0})|}\int_{Q_{2r}(x_{0})}\omega(y)dy\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;4\rho}^{\rm loc}}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{r}(x)}\omega(y)dy,~x\in Q_{r}(x_{0}),~0<t<r,

one has

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\omega(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))}{|Q_{2r}(x_{0})|}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mu(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))^{\frac{\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\left\|{\bf M}(\mu)^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\right\|_{L^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p},\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}

\displaystyle=C\left(\frac{\omega(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))}{|Q_{2r}(x_{0})|}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mu(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))^{\frac{\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\left\|{\bf M}(\mu)\right\|_{L^{1,\infty}(Q_{r}(x_{0}))}^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mu(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))^{\frac{\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\left(\frac{\omega(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))}{|Q_{2r}(x_{0})|}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mu(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}

\displaystyle=C|Q_{r}(x_{0})|^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\left(\frac{\omega(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))}{|Q_{2r}(x_{0})|}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2r}(x_{0}))}{r^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C|Q_{r}(x_{0})|^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\int_{2r}^{4r}\left(\frac{\omega(Q_{t}(x_{0}))}{|Q_{t}(x_{0})|}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{t}(x_{0}))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C|Q_{r}(x_{0})|^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;4\rho}^{\mu}(x_{0}),

where
C=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})
. Now we bound for
P_{2}
. Observe that if
x\in Q_{r}(x_{0})
and
r\leq t\leq 2\rho
, then
Q_{t}(x)\subseteq Q_{2t}(x_{0})
and again Proposition
2.2.4
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q_{2t}(x_{0})|}\int_{Q_{2t}(x_{0})}\omega(y)dy\leq[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}}\frac{1}{|Q_{t}(x)|}\int_{Q_{t}(x)}\omega(y)dy,

which implies that

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{r}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{\mu(Q_{2t}(x_{0}))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{|Q_{2t}(x_{0})|}\int_{Q_{2t}(x_{0})}\omega(y)dy\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right\|_{L^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p},\infty}(Q_{r}(x_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})|Q_{r}(x_{0})|^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\mathcal{W}_{\omega;8\rho}^{\mu}(x_{0}),

and the lemma now follows by combining the estimates
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.
∎

Proposition 4.3.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
E
is a measurable subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf{M}}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}\right\|_{L^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n},\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;160\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;1}^{\omega}(E)^{\frac{n}{n-\alpha p}}.

Proof.

By choosing the nonlinear potential
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
as in Proposition
3.2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})

\displaystyle=\mu\left(\overline{E}\right)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1\quad\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere on}~E.

Since
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}\subseteq A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, Theorem
4.1.1
entails

\displaystyle\chi_{E}(x)\leq\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p}}\quad\text{a.e..}

Let
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
Q
be a cube that containing
x_{0}
with
\ell(Q)\leq\rho
. Denote by
r=\ell(Q)
. Then
Q\subseteq Q_{r}(x_{0})
and
0<r\leq\rho
. We have by (
3.4.1
) and Lemma
4.3.4
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Q|}\int_{Q}\chi_{E}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n}\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x_{0})|}\int_{Q_{r}(x_{0})}\chi_{E}(y)dy

\displaystyle=2^{n}\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x_{0})|}\|\chi_{E}\|_{L^{1,\infty}(Q_{r}(x_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n}\frac{1}{|Q_{r}(x_{0})|}\left\|\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}\right\|_{L^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p},\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p}}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mathcal{W}_{\omega;4\rho}^{\mu}(x_{0})^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p}},

which yields

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}(x_{0})\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mathcal{W}_{\omega;4\rho}^{\mu}(x_{0})^{\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p}}.

As a consequence, for any
t>0
, we have by Corollary
3.8.2
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;8\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}(x)>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;8\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{W}_{\omega;4\rho}^{\mu}(x)>\left(\frac{t}{C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})}\right)^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{t^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}}}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;8\rho}^{\rm loc}})\frac{\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)}{t^{\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}}}

and the proof is complete by appealing to Proposition
3.5.1
and Theorem
3.5.3
that
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;160\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;8\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
.
∎

The weighted analogue of (
4.3.1
) is given as follows.

Theorem 4.3.6
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. For any Lebesgue measurable function
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;160\rho}^{\rm loc}})\|f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}.

Proof.

First of all, Theorem
3.5.3
and Proposition
4.2.5
entail

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|f_{k}|\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;20\rho}^{\rm loc}})\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q,\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

(4.3.9)

with
0<q=\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}<1
. Suppose that
f\in L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|f(x)|=\infty\})\leq\frac{1}{N^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f|^{p}d\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega},\quad N=1,2,\ldots

gives
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|f(x)|=\infty\})=0
. As a result, we can write

\displaystyle f=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}f\chi_{\{2^{k-1}<|f|\leq 2^{k}\}}\quad\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere},

and hence

\displaystyle|f|\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k}\chi_{\{2^{k-1}<|f|\leq 2^{k}\}}\quad\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere}.

Then Theorem
4.1.1
entails

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k}{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{\{2^{k-1}<|f|\leq 2^{k}\}}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

We obtain by (
4.3.9
) and Proposition
4.3.5
that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{kq}\left\|{\bf{M}}^{\rm loc}\chi_{\{2^{k-1}<|f|\leq 2^{k}\}}\right\|_{L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{kq}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{k-1}<|f(x)|\leq 2^{k}\right\}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},

which yields

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{q,\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{2^{k-2}}^{2^{k-1}}t^{q}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{k-1}<|f(x)|\leq 2^{k}\})\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=C\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{q}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{k-1}<|f(x)|\leq 2^{k}\})\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=C\|f\|_{L^{q}(R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})},

where
C=C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;160\rho}^{\rm loc}})
, and the proof is now complete.
∎

Proposition 4.3.7
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
E
is a measurable subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any
q>\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;320\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Proof.

For any
q>\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}
, choose an
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{q}>0
such that

\displaystyle q>\frac{n-\alpha p+\varepsilon}{n}.

Denote by
\delta=\frac{n(p-1)}{n-\alpha p+\varepsilon}
. By choosing the nonlinear potential
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}
as in Proposition
3.2.1
and using (
3.4.1
), one has

\displaystyle\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})

\displaystyle=\mu\left(\overline{E}\right)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1\quad\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\text{-quasi-everywhere on}~E.

Since
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}\subseteq A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, Theorem (
4.1.1
) and estimate (
3.4.1
) entail

\displaystyle\chi_{E}(x)\leq\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(x)^{\delta}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;2\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(x)^{\delta}\quad{\rm a.e.}.

Then Proposition
4.3.3
yields

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}(x)\leq C{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\left((\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu})^{\delta}\right)(x)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;12\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)\mathcal{W}_{\omega;6\rho}^{\mu}(x)^{\delta},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Denote
C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;12\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)
by
C
for simplicity. Since
{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}\leq 1
and
\frac{p-1}{q\delta}<1
, one has by Corollary
3.8.2
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;12\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}(x)^{q}>t\right\}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}R_{\alpha,p;12\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}\chi_{E}(x)>t^{\frac{1}{q}}\right\}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;12\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{W}_{\omega;6\rho}^{\mu}(x)>\left(\frac{t}{C}\right)^{\frac{1}{q\delta}}\right\}\right)dt

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{C^{-1}}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;12\rho}^{\omega}\left(\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\mathcal{W}_{\omega;6\rho}^{\mu}(x)>t^{\frac{1}{q\delta}}\right\}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;12\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)\int_{0}^{C^{-1}}\frac{\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})}{t^{\frac{p-1}{q\delta}}}dt

\displaystyle=C^{\prime\prime}(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;12\rho}^{\rm loc}},q)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E).

The proof is complete by noting that
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;320\rho}^{\rm loc}})\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;12\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
.
∎

The next theorem addresses the weighted analogue of (
4.3.2
).

Theorem 4.3.8
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{1}^{\rm loc}
. For any Lebesgue measurable function
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
and
q>\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{A_{1;160\rho}^{\rm loc}})\|f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega})}.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}f(x)^{q}\leq{\bf M}_{\rho}^{\rm loc}(f^{q})(x),\quad q>1,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

We only need to prove the estimate for the exponent that
\frac{n-\alpha p}{n}<q\leq 1
. By using Propositions
4.2.3
and
4.3.7
, one obtains the result by repeating the argument given in the proof of Theorem
4.3.6
.
∎

Remark 4.3.9
.

In view of Theorem
4.1.1
, the functions
f
in Theorems
4.3.6
and
4.3.8
need no to be defined everywhere in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In other words, the assertions hold for
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-quasi-everywhere defined functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

4.4.
Thinness of Sets and the Kellogg Property

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega
be a weight. We say that a set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-thin at a point
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
if

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}=\infty

(4.4.1)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}<\infty.

(4.4.2)

If
E
is not
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-thin at
a
, then
E
is said to be
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-thick at
a
. The set of points where
E
is
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-thin is denoted by
e_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)
and its complement with respect to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the set
b_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)
. We remark that if
x\notin\overline{E}
, then
E\cap Q_{t}(a)=\emptyset
for sufficiently small
t
, and hence (
4.4.2
) holds trivially.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Suppose that
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a Borel set such that
0<R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)<\infty
. Assume that
\mu^{E}
is the measure associated with
E
as in Proposition
3.2.1
. For any open set
G\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mu^{E}(G)\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(G\cap E).

(4.4.3)

Proof.

By Proposition
3.1.7
, there is an increasing sequence
\{K_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
of compact subsets of
E
such that
\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(K_{N})=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)
. Further, by the proof of
[
TB
, Proposition 3.2.15]
(the proof therein mainly uses Banach-Alaoglu theorem and the uniform convexity of
L^{p}
spaces), we may assume that
\mu_{N}=\mu^{K_{N}}
converges weakly to
\mu^{E}
, where
\mu_{N}
and
\mu^{E}
are the measures associated with
K_{N}
and
E
respectively as in Proposition
3.2.1
. Let
K\subseteq G
be compact and
\sigma_{N}=\mu_{N}|_{K}
. Then
V_{\omega;\rho}^{\sigma_{N}}\leq 1
on
{\rm supp}(\sigma_{N})\subseteq K\cap K_{N}
. By Proposition
3.1.9
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{N}(K)=\sigma_{N}(K\cap K_{N})\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(K\cap K_{N})\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(G\cap E),

which implies that

\displaystyle\mu_{N}(G)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\subseteq G\\
K~\text{compact}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{N}(K)\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(G\cap E).

Let
\varphi\in C_{0}(G)
be such that
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
on
G
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{G}\varphi(x)d\mu^{E}(x)=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{G}\varphi_{N}(x)d\mu_{N}(x)\leq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mu_{N}(G)\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(G\cap E).

Use the fact that
\mu^{E}(G)
is the supremum of
\int_{G}\varphi(x)d\mu^{E}(x)
over all such
\varphi
(see
[
DN
, Chapter II, Section 3]
). This proves the estimate (
4.4.3
).
∎

Lemma 4.4.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. If
(\ref{361})
holds for some
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}=\infty.

Proof.

For any
0<t\leq 2\rho
, we define

\displaystyle\varphi(t)

\displaystyle=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{\rho}(a))^{p^{\prime}-1}

\displaystyle\psi(t)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{1}{t},

and

\displaystyle\Psi(t)=\int_{t}^{2\rho}\psi(r)dr.

In view of Theorem
3.6.3
, the term
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{t}(a))^{p^{\prime}-1}
is comparable to
\Psi(t)^{-1}
for
0<t\leq\rho
. It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\psi(t)}{\Psi(t)}dt=\infty.

We note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\psi(t)}{\Psi(t)}dt=\log\Psi(0)-\log\Psi(\rho)=\infty,

since

\displaystyle\Psi(0)=\int_{0}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}=\infty,

by assumption, and

\displaystyle 0<\Psi(\rho)=\int_{\rho}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{1}{t}<\infty,

which completes the proof.
∎

The next lemma says that the
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-capacitary density of a set is
0
at every point where the set is
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-thin.

Lemma 4.4.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
,
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, and
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose that
E
is
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-thin at a point
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{r}(a))}{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))}=0.

(4.4.4)

Proof.

Let
\varphi
,
\psi
, and
\Psi
be as in the proof of Lemma
4.4.2
. Note that
\Psi(2\rho)=0
and
\Psi^{\prime}(t)=-\psi(t)
. Integration by parts yields

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{2\rho}\varphi(r)\psi(r)dr=\varphi(t)\Psi(t)+\int_{t}^{2\rho}\Psi(r)d\varphi(r).

(4.4.5)

Since
\varphi
and
\Psi
are nonnegative, it follows by (
4.4.4
) and (
4.4.5
) that

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{2\rho}\Psi(r)d\varphi(r)\leq\int_{t}^{2\rho}\varphi(r)\psi(r)dr\leq\int_{0}^{2\rho}\varphi(r)\psi(r)dr<\infty,\quad 0<t\leq 2\rho.

As
\varphi
is nondecreasing, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\rho}\Psi(r)d\varphi(r)<\infty.

Then we deduce from (
4.4.5
) that the limit
l=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0}\varphi(t)\Psi(t)
exists. If we can show that
l=0
, then (
4.4.4
) will follow by Theorem
3.6.3
. Suppose that
l>0
. Using Theorem
3.6.3
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{r}(a))}{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))}\geq C\varphi(t)\Psi(t).

If
t
is small enough, say,
0<t\leq t_{0}\leq\rho
, then

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{\rho}(a))^{p^{\prime}-1}\geq\frac{Cl}{2}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{\rho}(a))^{p^{\prime}-1},

which contradicts (
4.4.2
), since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{Cl}{2}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\infty

by Lemma
4.4.2
. Hence, we conclude that (
4.4.4
) holds.
∎

Lemma 4.4.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
,
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, and
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose that
a\in\overline{E}\cap e_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)
. Assume that
\varepsilon>0
and
a\in Q
for some cube
Q
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu^{E\cap Q}}(a)<\varepsilon

if
Q
is small enough, where
\mu^{E\cap Q}
is the measure associated with
E\cap Q
as in Proposition
3.2.1
.

Proof.

Consider first that
E
is a Borel set. Denote
\mu^{E\cap Q}
by
\mu
for simplicity. We have by (
3.4.1
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(a)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{W}_{\omega;2\rho}^{\mu}(a)

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(B_{t}(a))}{\omega(B_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}+C\int_{\delta}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t},

where
0<\delta\leq\rho
is arbitrary. Now choose
\delta
small enough such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}<\varepsilon^{\prime},

where
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
will be determined later. Then Lemma
4.4.1
entails

\displaystyle\mu(Q_{t}(a))\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q\cap Q_{t}(a))\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a)),\quad 0<t<\infty,

which yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}\leq\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}<C\varepsilon^{\prime}.

Now suppose that
Q\subseteq Q_{\delta}(a)
. Using Lemma
4.4.1
once again, we have

\displaystyle\mu(Q_{t}(a))\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q\cap Q_{t}(a))\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a)),\quad 0<t<\infty.

Combining the above with Theorem
3.6.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\delta}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{\delta}(a))^{p^{\prime}-1}\int_{\delta}^{2\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}\mu(B_{t}(a))}{\omega(B_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\left(\frac{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{r}(a))}{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}.

In view of Lemma
4.4.3
, we choose
\delta
small enough such that

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\left(\frac{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{r}(a))}{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{r}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}<C\varepsilon^{\prime}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(a)<2C\varepsilon^{\prime},

and the assertion follows by taking
\varepsilon=\frac{\varepsilon}{2C}
.

Consider now that
E
is a general set. We first claim that (
4.4.2
) holds if and only if

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2^{-\alpha pk}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right)}{\omega(Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}<\infty.

(4.4.6)

Assume that (
4.4.2
) holds. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\frac{\rho}{2^{k+1}}}^{\frac{\rho}{2^{k}}}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\geq\rho^{\alpha p}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\frac{\rho}{2^{k+1}}}^{\frac{\rho}{2^{k}}}\left(\frac{2^{-\alpha p(k+1)}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{2^{-(k+1)\rho}}(a))}{\omega(Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=(\log 2)\rho^{\alpha p}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2^{-\alpha p(k+1)}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{2^{-(k+1)\rho}}(a))}{\omega(Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}

\displaystyle\geq C(\log 2)\rho^{\alpha p}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2^{-\alpha p(k+1)}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{2^{-(k+1)\rho}}(a))}{\omega(Q_{2^{-(k+1)\rho}}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1},

where the last inequality follows by the local doubling property of
\omega
, which shows that (
4.4.6
) holds. For the converse, using the local doubling property again, one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\frac{\rho}{2^{k+1}}}^{\frac{\rho}{2^{k}}}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{\alpha p}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\frac{\rho}{2^{k+1}}}^{\frac{\rho}{2^{k}}}\left(\frac{2^{-\alpha pk}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a))}{\omega(Q_{2^{-(k+1)\rho}}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\leq C(\log 2)\rho^{\alpha p}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2^{-\alpha pk}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a))}{\omega(Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1},

which yields (
4.4.2
), as expected.

We choose, for
k=0,1,\ldots
,
G_{\delta}
sets
F_{k}\supseteq E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)
such that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(F_{k})=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right).

Define

\displaystyle F=\bigcap_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[F_{k}\cup\left(Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right)^{c}\right].

The set
F
is Borel and
F\supseteq E
. For each
k=0,1,\ldots
, it holds that

\displaystyle E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\subseteq F\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\subseteq F_{k}\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a),

which yields

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(F\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(F_{k}\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(F_{k})

\displaystyle=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right),

and hence
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(F\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right)=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(E\cap Q_{2^{-k\rho}}(a)\right)
. The equivalence between (
4.4.2
) and (
4.4.6
) shows that
F
is
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\cdot)
-thin at
a
.

Now we choose a
G_{\delta}
set
H
such that
E\cap Q\subseteq H
and
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q)=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(F)
. Since
E\subseteq F
, we have
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q)=R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(F\cap Q)
. Let
\sigma
be the measure associated with
F\cap Q
as in Proposition
3.2.1
. Then
[
AH
, Theorem 2.3.10]
implies that
\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu}(a)=\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\nu}(a)
. Thus the lemma follows by the first part of the proof.
∎

The following theorem is known to be “the Kellogg property”.

Theorem 4.4.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
, and
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
. Then

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}\left(E\cap e_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\right)=0.

Proof.

Let
\{Q_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
be the enumeration of the cubes with rational vertices. Define
\mu_{N}=\mu^{E\cap Q_{N}}
as in Lemma
4.4.4
. For each
N=1,2,\ldots
, let

\displaystyle E_{N}=\left\{x\in E\cap Q_{N}:\mathcal{V}_{\omega;\rho}^{\mu_{N}}(x)<1\right\}.

Then Proposition
3.2.3
and Theorem
3.5.3
entail
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E_{N})=0
. The preceding lemma shows that

\displaystyle E\cap e_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)\subseteq\bigcup_{N=1}^{\infty}E_{N},

which proves the theorem.
∎

A different way in measuring the thinness of sets is given by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{t^{n-\alpha p}}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}<\infty

(see
[
AH
, Definition 6.3.7]
). The weighted analogue of the above estimate is given in the next theorem.

Theorem 4.4.6
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
,
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, and
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a Borel set. Suppose that
a\in e_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}<\infty.

Proof.

It suffices to show that there exists a constant
C>0
such that if
t
is small enough, then

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))\leq CR_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a)).

Assume without loss of generality that
R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))>0
. Let
0<t\leq\frac{\rho}{2}
. By Proposition
3.1.1
, there is a probability measure
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))
such that

\displaystyle\|I_{\alpha,3t}\ast\mu\|_{L_{\omega^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq 2R_{\alpha,p;3t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))^{-\frac{1}{p}}.

Using the same proposition again, one has

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))^{-\frac{1}{p}}\leq\|I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu\|_{L_{\omega^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

We have

\displaystyle\|I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu\|_{L_{\omega^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{|x-a|_{\infty}<2t}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\int_{|x-a|_{\infty}\geq 2t}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}},

and by Minkowski’s inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{|x-a|_{\infty}\geq 2t}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|y-a|_{\infty}<t}\left(\int_{|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho,|x-a|\geq 2t}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(x)}{|x-y|_{\infty}^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}dx\right)d\mu(y).

Now we estimate the last integral. Suppose that
|y-a|_{\infty}<t
. Note that

\displaystyle|x-y|_{\infty}\geq\frac{1}{2}|x-a|_{\infty},\quad\text{if}~|x-a|_{\infty}\geq 2t,

\displaystyle|x-a|_{\infty}<\frac{3\rho}{2},\quad\text{if}~|x-y|_{\infty}<\rho.

Combining the above with (
3.4.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\int_{|x-a|_{\infty}\geq 2t}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n-\alpha}\mu(E\cap Q_{t}(a))\left(\int_{2t\leq|x-a|_{\infty}<2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(x)}{|x-a|_{\infty}^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=2^{n-\alpha}\left(\int_{2t}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{s}(a))}{s^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{ds}{s}+\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2\rho}(a))}{(2\rho)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}-\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2t}(a))}{(2t)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{2t}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{s}(a))}{s^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{ds}{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}},

where the last inequality follows by the fact that

\displaystyle\int_{2t}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{s}(a))}{s^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{ds}{s}\geq\int_{\rho}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{s}(a))}{s^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{ds}{s}\geq C\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{\rho}(a))}{(2\rho)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\geq C^{\prime}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{2\rho}(a))}{(2\rho)^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}},

which is a consequence of the local doubling property of
\omega
. Using Theorem
3.6.3
and Lemma
4.4.3
together with the assumption, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\int_{2t}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{s}(a))}{s^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{ds}{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{t}^{2\rho}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(Q_{s}(a))}{s^{(n-\alpha)p^{\prime}}}\frac{ds}{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{t}(a))^{-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))^{-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))^{-\frac{1}{p}}

for
t
sufficiently closed to
0
. We thus have shown that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))^{-\frac{1}{p}}\leq 2\left(\int_{|x-a|_{\infty}<2t}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

(4.4.7)

if
t
is small enough. If
|x-a|_{\infty}<2t
and
|y-a|_{\infty}<t
, then
|x-y|_{\infty}<3t
. Hence

\displaystyle\int_{|x-a|_{\infty}<2t}(I_{\alpha,\rho}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(I_{\alpha,3t}\ast\mu)(x)^{p^{\prime}}\omega^{\prime}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p^{\prime}}R_{\alpha,p;3t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{p}}.

Combining the above with (
4.4.7
), one shows that

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;3t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))\leq CR_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a)).

Using Proposition
3.5.1
, we have

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{40t})R_{\alpha,p;3t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))

\displaystyle\leq C(n,\alpha,p,[\omega]_{20\rho})R_{\alpha,p;3t}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))

for
0<t<\frac{\rho}{2}
, where the last inequality follows by Remark
3.3.6
.
∎

The converse of Theorem
4.4.6
also holds, as the following shown.

Theorem 4.4.7
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<\alpha<n
,
1<p<\infty
,
0<\rho<\infty
,
\omega\in A_{p}^{\rm loc}
, and
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose that
a\in E
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}<\infty.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}=\infty.

(4.4.8)

Proof.

Recall the function
\psi

\displaystyle\psi(t)=\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}}{\omega(Q_{t}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{1}{t},\quad 0<t\leq 2\rho

defined as in Lemma
4.4.2
. Then
\psi
is continuous on
(0,2\rho]
and
\int_{0}^{2\rho}\psi(t)dt<\infty
. Moreover, if
0<\delta\leq\rho
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\delta}^{\rho}\frac{\psi(t)}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2t}\psi(r)dr}dt=\log\left(\int_{0}^{2\rho}\psi(t)dt\right)-\log\left(\int_{0}^{2\delta}\psi(t)dt\right).

As
\delta\rightarrow 0
, the right-sided of the above identity tends to
\infty
.

On the other hand, since

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}(Q_{\delta}(a))\leq R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}\left(\overline{Q}_{\delta}(a)\right)\leq R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}\left(Q_{\delta+1/N}(a)\right),\quad\delta>0,\quad N=1,2,\ldots,

in view of Theorem
3.6.3
, we have

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}\left(\overline{Q}_{\delta}(a)\right)\approx\left(\int_{\delta}^{2t}\psi(r)dr\right)^{-1},\quad 0<\delta\leq t.

Since
\overline{Q}_{\delta}(a)
is decreasing to
\{a\}
as
\delta\downarrow 0
, using Proposition
3.1.5
, one has

\displaystyle R_{\alpha,p;t}^{\omega}(\{a\})\approx\left(\int_{0}^{2t}\psi(r)dr\right)^{-1},\quad 0<t<\infty,

which yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(\{a\})}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\psi(t)}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2t}\psi(r)dr}dt

\displaystyle=\infty,

and the theorem follows.
∎

Remark 4.4.8
.

Let
S=\{a\in E:(\ref{3619})~\text{does not hold}\}
. Since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{1}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t}\leq\int_{0}^{\rho}\left(\frac{t^{\alpha p}R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E\cap Q_{t}(a))}{\omega(Q_{\rho}(a))}\right)^{p^{\prime}-1}\frac{dt}{t},

the theorem just proved gives
S\subseteq E\cap e_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(E)
. By “the Kellogg property”, Theorem
4.4.5
, we have
R_{\alpha,p;\rho}^{\omega}(S)=0
.
